## Recording folder shapes: `.ct` is not always a container (M41, 2026-07-30)

`ct` accepts three on-disk shapes for a recording folder, classified in one
place: `src/ct/trace/trace_container.nim::detectTraceFolderShape`.

  1. a `*.ct` CTFS container FILE (magic-checked) — what `ct record` writes;
  2. a materialized `runtime_tracing` directory (`trace.json` / `trace.bin`
     plus `trace_metadata.json` + `trace_paths.json`) — what `ct record-web`
     writes for browser recordings, and what the Rust replay engine has always
     autodetected (`dap_server.rs::auto_detect_materialized_trace_file`);
  3. a `session.toml` multi-recording session manifest.

Two traps this resolves:

  * A **directory** named `foo.ct` is NOT a container. The cross-process
    fixture names its per-process recordings `frontend.ct/` etc., which is why
    `ct host --trace-path <fixture-root>` used to die with "trace folder
    missing `.ct` CTFS container" against a folder that visibly contained
    `backend.ct`. `describeMissingTraceContainer` now names what it found.
  * There used to be two `findCtFileInFolder` implementations with different
    semantics (`storage_and_import.nim` accepted any `*.ct` file;
    `online_sharing/mcr_enrichment.nim` also verified CTFS magic). They are
    unified in `trace_container.nim`; `mcr_enrichment` re-exports it.

Sessions: `ct` never parses `session.toml` itself — it shells out to
`replay-server session <manifest>` (~20 ms) so the only parser is
`db-backend/src/session_manifest.rs`. A session is registered in the recording
index IN PLACE (its `output_folder` is the manifest's directory, not a copy
under `codetracerTraceDir`), because `[[trace]]` paths only resolve next to the
manifest. The GUI needs no per-recording plumbing to open one: the DAP launch
sends that folder as `traceFolder`, and `dap_server.rs::resolve_launch_trace_file`
turns "folder contains session.toml" into a `setup_session` launch.

Beware: `dap_server.rs` has TWO `launch` handlers (the initial one in
`handle_message` and the stable thread's re-launch one). They MUST agree on
trace-file resolution or a session opens as a session on one path and as one of
its members on the other — which is silent, not a crash. Both now call
`resolve_launch_trace_file`.

Which test covers which handler: `cross_process_three_trace_dap_test::
a_session_folder_launches_as_the_whole_session` covers only the STABLE-thread
handler (`task_thread`) — reverting `handle_message`'s call to
`resolve_launch_trace_file` leaves that integration test green. The
`handle_message` half is what the cached-launch flow and tracepoint worker
threads replay, and it is pinned separately by
`dap_server::tests::handle_message_launch_resolves_a_session_folder_to_its_manifest`.
If you touch either handler, run both.

A third `launch` handler exists behind `#[cfg(feature = "browser-transport")]`
(`handle_message_browser`): it re-runs trace detection against the WASM VFS and
OVERWRITES `ctx.launch_trace_file`. `setup_from_vfs` has no session branch, so
that path now refuses a folder holding a `session.toml` outright rather than
latching onto a `trace.ct`/`trace.json` beside it.

Opening a recording MUTATES it, for the CTFS shape only: `importTraceFolder`
runs `materializeCtfsSources(<container>, <source folder>)`, which writes a
`paths.json` **into the folder you pointed at** — including committed fixtures
(`ct host --trace-path .../backend.ct` leaves an untracked `paths.json` in the
fixture). The materialized branch was fixed to normalize the copy in the
recording store instead; the CTFS branch still does it in place because
`importTrace` then copies that sidecar next to the `files/` payload. Discard the
stray file rather than committing it.

Nim test runners cover three roots and `src/ct` was not one of them:
`just test-vm` globs `src/tests/gui/tests/**/*_test.nim`,
`just test-vm-recorder-gated` covers `src/frontend/viewmodel/tests/unit/`, and
(new) `just test-ct-trace-units` covers `src/ct/trace/*_test.nim`. Before that
last recipe every suite under `src/ct/trace` was a test nothing invoked. If you
add a Nim test outside those three roots, add a runner too.

GUI-test flake to know about: `fixtures.ts::importTraceFolder` caps `ct host`
at a 15 s `spawnSync` timeout, and on a loaded machine that trips as
`spawnSync ... ETIMEDOUT` for ordinary CTFS folders too (seen on
`tracepoints_type_error.spec.ts`). It is not specific to sessions.

Not every recorded step belongs to a call. `DbStep.call_key` can be
`CallKey(NO_KEY)` — a FRAMELESS step — and the JavaScript recorder produces them
routinely: three bootstrap steps before `<module>` opens even in a trivial
script, plus one or more at the event-loop level between one callback returning
and the next being entered. Python traces have none. Anything that resolves
`call_key` and treats `None` as an error is wrong on JS traces; that is exactly
what `step_over_depths_step_id` did, which made step-over hold forever at the
last statement of a Node module body (M50). Frameless steps sit at the trace's
OUTERMOST level — `db::OUTERMOST_CALL_DEPTH` — because the `<module>` call and
every callback the loop invokes are all recorded with `depth = 0` and
`parent = NO_KEY`; there is no recorded frame that is shallower.

`step_over_depths_step_id` and `next_step_id_relative_to*` exist TWICE, as
deliberate mirrors: the `TraceReader` trait defaults in `trace_reader.rs` (the
live path — neither `CTFSTraceReader` nor `InMemoryTraceReader` overrides them)
and inherent copies on `Db` in `db.rs`. Change one, change the other.

`TestRecording::drop` does `fs::remove_dir_all(self.temp_dir)`. Never hand-build
a `TestRecording` around a committed fixture to reuse `initialize_and_launch` —
it deletes the fixture from the working tree when the test ends. Use
`DapStdioTestClient::initialize_and_launch_trace(path)` instead.

The tup rule for the db-backend (`src/db-backend/Tupfile`) *names* only
`src/main.rs` as its input, but that is not the whole dependency set: tup
discovers the rest through its FUSE sandbox, recording every file the cargo
invocation actually reads. Editing any file under `src/db-backend/src/` does
re-run the rule on the next `just build-once`, and `src/build-debug/bin/
replay-server` is refreshed — verified by editing `trace_reader.rs` alone and
observing tup execute the rule and cargo emit `Compiling replay-server`. Note
that `just build-once` runs tup twice (before and after webpack); the second
pass legitimately reports "No commands to execute", so read the FIRST pass when
checking whether the engine was rebuilt.

In the Electron build, `dap.nim::asyncSendCtRequest` used to be fire-and-forget:
it wrote the request frame over IPC and resolved the `Future[JsObject]` it
returned with an empty `js{}` immediately, having never seen a response.
Responses arrive on a *separate* IPC channel (`CODETRACER::dap-receive-response`)
and were only fanned out to `dap.on(...)` subscribers. So every
`BackendService.send(...)` in the desktop app resolved with `{}`, and any
ViewModel that read the resolved body silently got nothing — the VS Code build
was fine, because it goes through `activeDebugSession.customRequest`, which
really does return the body. M49 fixed this by correlating on the `request_seq`
the Backend Manager already stamps on every response (`DapApi.pendingResponses`,
settled from `ui_js.nim::onDapReceiveResponse`). If you are debugging a panel
that gets data through events but not through request bodies, this is the
history to know.

`DapApi` is built in four places, and only `dap.nim::newDapApi` uses the named
constructor — `types.nim` and `ui/session_switch.nim` build one from an object
literal per replay session. A field added to `DapApi` and initialised only in
`newDapApi` will be nil on most instances.

`data.dapApi` is NOT one object: it is a template for
`data.sessions[data.activeSessionIndex].dapApi`, so it follows whichever tab is
on screen, and each session's `DapApi` has its own `seq` counter starting at
zero. Anything that has to pair a response with the request that produced it
must therefore pick the session's `DapApi` by the `sessionId` the main process
stamps on the response (`ipc_subsystems/dap.nim::resolveSessionId`), not read
`data.dapApi` — otherwise it silently addresses the wrong session as soon as a
second tab exists. Note also that `ui_js.nim` captures `let dapRef = data.dapApi`
once when it builds the `SessionViewModel`, so the whole ViewModel layer still
talks to the session that was active at bootstrap.

A ViewModel command string needs THREE registrations to reach the wire: a
`CtEventKind` in `common/ct_event.nim`, a non-empty entry in
`dap.nim::EVENT_KIND_TO_DAP_MAPPING`, and a string in
`viewmodel/backend/dap_commands.nim::VALID_DAP_COMMANDS_SEQ`. Miss the first two
and `dapCommandToEventKind` raises `ValueError`, which kills the whole reactive
batch, and the request is never sent — silently, from the caller's point of
view. `scenarios/integration_test.nim`'s "all ViewModel commands are valid DAP
commands" suite is the guard, but it only sees commands the test actually
triggers: a command sent solely from a click handler needs its own case there.

`ct/pairIndexLookup` must be answered at the session layer
(`dap_server.rs::handle_request_via_session`), not by the per-trace `Handler`:
`Handler::build_local_pair_index` sees only its own recording's marker firings,
and a correlation marker's counterpart is by definition in another process.
- 2026-07-31 (ct record recorder dispatch): the per-language recorder table now lives in ONE place, `src/ct/trace/recorder_dispatch.nim` (tool name + override env var + sibling repo + install hint + argv + `--server` semantics), and `src/ct/db_backend_record.nim` consults it. Add a language there, not in a `case lang` inside `recordDb`. Four traps this consolidated: (1) language DETECTION and recorder DISPATCH were unconnected tables — `common/lang.nim` mapped `.php`/`.ex`/`.exs`/`.erl` and flagged them `usesMaterializedTraces`, but the dispatch chain had no arm for any of them, so `ct record app.php` printed `ERROR: unsupported trace kind db` and exited **0**; `src/tests/cli/record_dispatch_test.nim` now asserts the invariant "every `usesMaterializedTraces` language has an arm". (2) `ct record`'s `recordInternal` returned nil on a non-zero recorder exit and then let `ct` exit 0 — every recorder failure was invisible to `$?`; it now propagates the child's code, and `db_backend_record`'s `except CatchableError` no longer swallows the failure into a `trace_index.recordTrace` for a recording that never ran. (3) `mcrRecorderExe` was unreachable in the dev shell: `paths.nim` read `CODETRACER_CT_MCR_PATH` while `detect-siblings.sh` exports `CODETRACER_CT_MCR_CMD` and puts a binary named `ct_cli` (not `ct-mcr`) on PATH, so both lookups missed; it now tries CMD, PATH-var, `ct-mcr`, `ct_cli` in that order. (4) `--out-dir` does not mean the same thing to every recorder: the JS recorder writes `<out-dir>/trace-<n>/` and the PHP extension writes `<out-dir>/worker_<pid>/`, so `detectTraceFolderShape` (`src/ct/trace/trace_container.nim`) searches one level down as a LAST resort and refuses when several recordings are found there, since picking one would open the wrong one. Two guards are load-bearing and pinned by `trace_container_test.nim`: descent NEVER runs in a folder holding a `session.toml` (even under `allowSession = false`), because the three-recording session (produced into `target/test-recordings/cross-process-three-trace/<key>/`; see `scripts/materialize-recording.sh`) has materialized members in `frontend.ct/` and a REAL CTFS container at `backend.ct/server.ct` — an unguarded one-level search finds exactly one container there and would silently open a third of the program; and the resolved recording folder travels in `shape.folder`, which is what `importTrace` reads sidecars/`files/` from, since after a descent it is no longer the folder the caller passed.
- 2026-07-31 (PHP recorder has no executable): codetracer-php-recorder ships only a Zend extension. `ct` records PHP by running `php -d extension=<codetracer.so> <program>` with `CODETRACER_ENABLED=1` plus EITHER `CODETRACER_TRACE_DIR` (verbatim output directory — the single-process form) OR `CODETRACER_OUTPUT_DIR` (each worker writes `worker_<pid>/` beneath it — the multi-worker/server form). `ct record --server` is what chooses the second. `detect-siblings.sh` exports `CODETRACER_PHP_RECORDER_PATH` (the repo) and `CODETRACER_PHP_RECORDER_EXTENSION` (the built `.so`); there is nothing for `findTool` to find.
- 2026-07-31 (CLI test lane): `src/tests/cli/*_test.nim` is NOT reached by `just test-vm-native`, whose glob is `find src/tests/gui/tests`. Its runner is `just test-cli-record` and its registry is `CliRecordGateTests` in `src/ct_test/release_gate.nim` — both are required, and `release_gate_test.nim` now asserts the recipe exists so a gate entry can never again be the only registration.
- 2026-07-31 (WASM host state now rides in the event stream — M44b): `browser_stream_host.rs` writes the spec §3.3/§3.4 records TWICE — into `boundary_state.json` as before, and as `Event` records under `boundary_id: "wasm-host-state"` inside `trace.json`. The second is the only carrier a *streaming* consumer can use: `--boundary-stream` reads its metadata once at startup, §3.3 is only known at the module's first exported call, and — the deciding detail — `write_host_state` calls `open_stream`, which is what SPAWNS the `--snapshot-consumer` process, so a "re-read the sidecar" protocol races the sidecar's first write rather than merely arriving late. Consequence for anyone touching that translate arm: emit the `Event` through `self.emit(...)` (so the batch mirror stays byte-identical) and emit it only when the state actually CHANGED — a duplicate `HostInitialState` is ignored, and emitting for it would make the stream and the sidecar disagree, which `LoadRecording` now refuses outright rather than resolving. The test that used to assert host-state events changed `trace.json` by not one byte was replaced by one that proves the safe part of that claim directly: remove the host-state records from the produced document, as ORIGINAL byte ranges (not values round-tripped through `serde_json::Value`, which sorts keys), and what remains must equal a recording made without host state.
- 2026-07-31 (rustc cannot emit a multi-value wasm export): measured — `extern "C" fn(u32) -> (u32, u32)` compiles to `(i32,i32,i32) -> ()` on wasm32-unknown-unknown (hidden sret pointer), and `-C target-feature=+multivalue` does not change it. `src/db-backend/tests/fixtures/wasm-parity-corpus/modules/pair_stats/` gets one by assembling a nine-instruction wrapper from LLVM wasm assembly (`wrap.s`, `clang --target=wasm32-unknown-unknown -mmultivalue`) that calls the Rust half. Two traps: (1) `rustc --print link-args` shows a bare two-token `"--export" "<sym>"` for EVERY `#[no_mangle]` item and there is no flag to stop it — not dropping `pub`, not `.hidden` in the assembly, not `--no-export-dynamic` (all measured) — which matters because `ct-instrument` instruments an exported function's *body*, so an exported helper called from another export is recorded as a nested export crossing and refused by spec §8; the fixture links through `lld-explicit-exports`, which drops those pairs. (2) C is not an alternative: the recorder's DWARF stepping filter (`isUserRustSourcePath`) requires a `.rs` suffix, so a C module would carry DWARF and emit no steps.
- 2026-07-31 (the status bar patches, it no longer rebuilds — M46): `renderStatusInto` (`viewmodel/views/isonim_status_view.nim`) is now conditional. It stamps `statusStructureSignature(model)` on the `#status` host as `data-status-structure`; if the next model produces the same signature it PATCHES text/titles/classes in place and returns `false`, and only a structural change (location appears/disappears, FINISHED, disconnected, notification set, bug report) tears the subtree down and returns `true`. Two consequences to respect when adding a field to `StatusShellModel`: a field that only changes a node's text/class must stay OUT of the signature and gain a line in `patchStatusValues`, or it will render once and then never update; a field that adds or removes an element must go INTO the signature, or it will never appear. `requestStatusRender` also coalesces to one pass per task turn (six independent callers redraw the bar 60+ times per trace open, enumerated in the comment there), and the strip / icon-zone remount now runs only when the shell was actually rebuilt. `window.__ctStatusRenderStats()` exposes requests/passes/rebuilds; a clean open is ~66/11/2.

  KNOW ITS EDGE — it does not trip on "any spelling", and an early draft of this
  note said so wrongly. THE SECOND HALF OF THE PREDICATE EXISTS BECAUSE THE
  FIRST VERSION OF THIS NOTE WAS READ AS A ROUTE: it listed off-screen
  placement first among the misses, and `00fd68b7f "fix: status bar styling"`
  then wrote `position: absolute; left: -9999px` onto `#file-info-status` and
  `.status-right`, which shipped for two weeks with the guard green. Re-measured
  over the same 27 spellings after the change: 19 caught, 8 missed. CAUGHT —
  everything that removes the BOX (`display`, `visibility`, `opacity`,
  `transform: scale(0)`, `content-visibility`, `font-size: 0`, zero
  width/height, flex collapse, and all of those on an ancestor, under any
  selector syntax including `@media` and `:has()`), plus everything that MOVES
  the box off-screen (`position: absolute/fixed` with a big negative
  `left`/`top`/`right`, `transform: translateX(-9999px)`). MISSED — everything
  that keeps a full-size box AT ITS PROPER COORDINATES and changes only the
  PAINTING: `clip-path`, the legacy `position: absolute; clip: rect(0,0,0,0)`
  idiom (which the old note mis-filed as off-screen — its `absolute` leaves the
  element where it was), `filter: opacity(0)` (yes, while bare `opacity: 0` is
  caught) and `filter: blur()`, opacity `0.001`, `color: transparent`, a colour
  equal to the background, and occlusion by an overlay. `readyOnEntryTest` and
  Playwright's `toBeVisible` are box+`visibility` predicates and miss BOTH
  families — Playwright calls an off-screen `.location-path` "visible, enabled
  and stable" — so the shared predicate is now strictly stronger than the
  readiness wait, and the painting family is still guarded by nothing. Also: the
  guard reads whatever is in `src/build-debug`, so editing `status_bar.styl` and
  running `just test-gui-prebuilt` WITHOUT `just build-once` reports green
  against the old build; a MISSING build fails loudly, a STALE one does not. Add
  new hiding spellings to `CAUGHT_HIDE_SPELLINGS` rather than rewriting it — and
  if you strengthen the predicate, move the entry out of
  `SURVIVING_HIDE_SPELLINGS` and re-derive the prose in all four places that
  carry it (that module, the guard's header, `status_bar.styl`, and this note).
  `verify_the_blind_spot_is_where_the_guard_says_it_is` fails until you do.

- 2026-08-06 (a readyOnEntry timeout means a SLOW LAUNCH, and the old 'stalled locator' note here was wrong — M46): `lib/fixtures.ts::readyOnEntryTest` is ONE strict visibility wait on `.location-path` with `READY_ON_ENTRY_BUDGET_MS = 30_000`, and it prints `# timing: ready on entry ...: Xms / 30000ms (N%)` on EVERY launch, passing or not — that line is the measurement, use it before touching the budget. `.location-path` is rendered only when `locationText` is non-empty, so its appearance IS the end of the launch: median ~8-11s, observed max ~14.7s on a 32-core host at load average 33-82, and 17.4s for a three-recording session launch. A `Locator` stores only `_frame` and `_selector` and `waitFor` is a plain `frame._channel.waitForSelector(...)`, so a fresh locator is a byte-identical call; and the missing `locator resolved to ...` line it rested on is just what Playwright logs for a selector matching ZERO elements (the injected predicate in `server/frames.js` sets `log` only on a match, and the caller guards with `if (log)`) — verified directly: a page with no `.location-path` logs only the `waiting for` line for 30s, while a page with a `display:none` one logs `62 x locator resolved to hidden ...`. Still true and still worth keeping: removing the status bar's per-redraw DOM churn did not change the flake rate (2/22 before, 4/54 after, same host), so do not expect a frontend churn fix to cure a readiness timeout. What IS unexplained: in the pre-churn-fix artefacts (`test-diagnostics/2026-07-30T01-*`) the status bar logged a non-empty location 57-66 times INSIDE the failing 15s window, which no 'element was absent' story covers; see the M46 completion note.
- 2026-08-01 (test recordings are PRODUCED, never committed — M53): no `.ct` is
  committed anywhere in this repo any more. `scripts/materialize-recording.sh
  <fixture>` records one from the current tree and prints the directory; it
  caches under the gitignored `target/test-recordings/` and serialises with
  `flock`, so the five Rust tests in one binary, a Playwright worker, the
  `backend-manager` unit tests and the three `verify.sh` checks all share one
  ~40 s production. Consumers: `test_harness::three_trace_recordings()` /
  `three_trace_sources()` (Rust), `threeTraceRecordingRoot()` /
  `threeTraceSourceRoot()` (Playwright — kept SEPARATE so a spec cannot launch
  Electron on the source directory), `browser_stream_host::tests_support` (the
  backend-manager crate), and each fixture's `verify.sh`.
  **The cache key is the whole point**: it hashes the fixture's tracked sources
  AND the *content* of `ct-instrument`, the `session-manager` binary, the
  instrumenter's `recorder-runtime/` and the JS recorder's `packages/*/dist`
  AND `packages/*/src`. Both halves of that last one are load-bearing and it
  was measured: the backend tier runs `packages/cli/dist/index.js` and resolves
  siblings through their `main` fields, but the demo's `vite.config.js` aliases
  `@codetracer/runtime-browser` straight at `packages/runtime-browser/src/
  index.ts` so the page exercises the working tree — so a key over `dist/` alone
  did not move when that TypeScript changed, and the suite was served a
  recording made by the previous source.
  Rebuild a recorder and the demo is recorded again. A cache
  that could survive a recorder change would restore the bug this removed: a
  container written by today's recorder and replayed by today's replayer proves
  only that the two agree with each other, and keeps proving it afterwards.
  Two traps. (1) The materialiser scrubs `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `RUSTFLAGS` and
  friends before running `regenerate.sh`, because the caller is usually `cargo
  test` and those would redirect the fixture's own wasm32 build into the
  caller's target dir. It deliberately does NOT scrub `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` — the
  dev shell needs it for Chromium. (2) It allocates TCP ports from the kernel
  rather than using the scripts' hard-coded defaults; 8080 in particular is
  routinely taken and a suite failing on that has told the reader nothing.
  There is no exit status meaning "skip". `CT_RECORDINGS_DIR` is the only way
  not to record, for a CI job that recorded in an earlier stage.

  records and `LoadRecording` refuses a recording whose two carriers disagree
  (spec §8) — so the edited recording was *refused*, not diverged, and the
  control had stopped reaching the path it names. It kept printing `ok` because
  the committed recording predated the in-stream carrier and had only a sidecar
  to edit; it surfaced on the first run against a recording made by the current
  producer. If you add a carrier for anything a negative control withholds, the
  control has to withhold it from every carrier.

- 2026-08-11 (Nim ORC's allocator is per-thread, and the region dies with the
  thread — `ct-test test run` SIGSEGV'd at 21+ workers): Nim ARC/ORC gives every
  thread its own `MemRegion`, and that region lives in the thread's TLS
  (`lib/system/mmdisp.nim`: `var allocator {.rtlThreadVar.}: MemRegion`). Every
  string/seq payload is stamped with the allocating thread's region as its
  owner. Cross-thread `dealloc` IS supported — `rawDealloc` sees
  `c.owner != addr(a)` and hands the cell over via `addToSharedFreeList` — but
  ONLY while the owning thread is still alive; that helper dereferences
  `c.owner`. So "protected by a Lock" is NOT sufficient for sharing GC-managed
  data with worker threads: you must also not let worker-allocated memory
  outlive its worker. `run_orchestration.runUnits` returned worker-built
  `RunUnitOutcome`s to its caller *after* `joinThreads`, and the caller's free
  then walked a dangling `c.owner`. It looked fine below 21 workers purely
  because glibc caches retired thread stacks (`stack_cache_maxsize`, 40 MiB
  default) so the dead TLS stayed mapped — silent corruption, not safety. The
  boundary is exact arithmetic, not folklore: Nim gives every thread a 2 MiB
  stack of its own (`ThreadStackSize` in `std/typedthreads` is
  `1024*256*sizeof(int) - 4096`; `ulimit -s` is irrelevant), so 40 MiB holds
  exactly 20 retired stacks and worker 21 is the first whose region is
  unmapped. Then the free segfaults, *after* the run has already printed a
  correct summary (stdout says success, `$?` says 139).
  Two things worth keeping:
  * `GLIBC_TUNABLES=glibc.pthread.stack_cache_size=0` unmaps every worker's TLS
    at join, so this whole bug class becomes deterministic at ONE worker instead
    of probabilistic at 21. Measured on the pre-fix build: exit 139 at
    `--threads 1`, and conversely a 1 GiB stack cache makes the pre-fix build
    survive 64 workers — proof that thread count never made it safe, only quiet.
    Reach for this tunable when stress-testing any threaded Nim code here; a
    clean run without it is close to no evidence at all.
  * `-d:useMalloc` is the independent cross-check: it removes the per-thread
    regions, and the pre-fix build then passes at 64 workers even with the
    cache disabled. If a crash survives `-d:useMalloc`, it is NOT this bug.
  * The fix pattern is in `runUnits`' `ResultHandoff`: workers park on a
    condvar after draining the queue, the spawning thread copies the results
    into its own region and frees the worker-owned originals while the workers
    are still alive, and only then releases and joins them. Any new worker pool
    that returns GC-managed data needs the same hand-off (or `-d:useMalloc`,
    which sidesteps per-thread regions entirely but only protects the binaries
    that are built with it, not library consumers).

- 2026-08-13 (ct-test discovery scoping + cost): `ct test discover --workspace`
  fans out to ~38 providers, and EVERY provider used to answer "which files are
  in this workspace?" with its own `walkDirRec(projectRoot)` plus an ad-hoc
  reject list (`js_common` skipped `node_modules/`, `go_test` skipped `vendor/`,
  `python_common` and `nim_unittest` skipped nothing). That produced ~16 full
  recursive walks during `detect` alone, several of which READ every matching
  file, and ~21 more during `discoverProject`.
  Measured against the reprobuild workspace on the pre-fix build: 53,349 items
  in 10m40s and 59 MB of JSON, of which 45,256 (84.8%) came from
  `references/{llvm-project,buildxl,nix,spack,mold,wild,…}` — vendored upstream
  source trees reported as that project's tests. After scoping: 8,093 items in
  2.1s and 9.8 MB. Same binary, same workspace; a ~300x wall-clock difference
  that is almost entirely "stop walking other people's repositories".
  The rule now lives in ONE place, `src/ct_test/workspace_scope.nim`:
  * `walkWorkspaceFiles(root)` is the drop-in for `walkDirRec` — new providers
    MUST use it, otherwise they re-open the hole for their own language.
  * The scope honours the workspace's OWN declaration rather than a ct_test
    ignore file: `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard`, run with
    cwd = root (so `--workspace <subdir>` scopes itself). That gets the whole
    `.gitignore` chain, stops at nested checkouts (a vendored tree is reported
    as ONE directory entry, never descended into), and — the part that is easy
    to break — keeps untracked-but-not-ignored files, so a test written seconds
    ago is still discovered. Fallback for non-checkouts is a PRUNING walk
    (prunes the descent, not the results) plus `VendorDirNames`.
  * The resolved set is memoized in a `{.threadvar.}` because `TestProvider`'s
    callbacks only receive `projectRoot: string`; `discovery.discover` calls
    `invalidateWorkspaceScopes()` once per invocation, so one traversal is
    shared by all 38 providers within a discovery and no state survives it.
  * Escape hatch is opt-OUT, not opt-in: `--scope unscoped` / `CT_TEST_SCOPE`.
    An `info` diagnostic (`discovery scope: N file(s) … excluded …`) reports
    what was dropped, so the exclusion is never silent.
  Regression guard: the last two cases in `src/ct_test/discovery_test.nim`.
  Mutation-verified against 7 mutations (drop the nested-VCS prune, the
  `VendorDirNames` prune in either mode, `--exclude-standard`, `--others`, the
  `--scope unscoped` branch, and the summary diagnostic).

- 2026-08-13 (ct-test Nim discovery: one shared lexical scanner): the
  nim-unittest provider had TWO hand-rolled character loops (a declaration
  scanner and a per-line comment stripper) and both treated every `'` as the
  opening quote of a character literal. Nim gives the apostrophe three other
  jobs — numeric type suffix (`0'u8`, `1.0'f32`, `0x1F'i64`), custom numeric
  literal (`12'MyType`), and accent-quoted operator names (`` `'u8` ``) — so
  ONE `sizeBytes = 10485760'i64` desynchronised the scan and everything up to
  the next apostrophe (typically the rest of the file) was skipped as string
  content. Symptom: `std/unittest import detected but no literal suite/test
  declarations were found` on files with ordinary `suite "…":` at column 0.
  Both scans now run over one token stream: `src/ct_test/nim_lexer.nim`.
  Things to know before touching it:
  * It is written against the Nim distribution's own `compiler/lexer.nim`,
    which IS on disk — `$(dirname $(readlink -f $(which nim)))/../nim/compiler/`
    — and is the ground truth for every rule. It is NOT usable as a dependency
    (nix store path that moves with the toolchain, compiler-internal API,
    `lexMessage` aborts the process on a malformed literal).
  * The load-bearing rule: lex numbers and identifiers FIRST. Once `lexNumber`
    has eaten the type suffix, a remaining `'` really is a character literal.
  * Fail-safe: anything Nim requires to close on its own line (single-quoted
    string, character literal) ends at the newline, and an apostrophe with no
    closer within `MaxCharLiteralBody` degrades to one punct token. A malformed
    file costs one token, never the rest of the file. Both properties are
    load-bearing and separately mutation-pinned.
  * `NimToken` is position-first ON PURPOSE: no per-token copy of the source
    slice. Use `sliceOf`, `identIs` and `.ch`. An earlier revision carried a
    `text: string` on every token and doubled workspace discovery wall time
    (5.6s -> 10.5s on the reprobuild workspace). If you add a field that
    allocates per token, re-measure with
    `time ct-test test discover --workspace <repo> --json`.
  * `nimUnittestFileCatalog` scans each file ONCE and hands the same tokens to
    `detectFrameworksInTokens` and `parseNimUnittestDeclarations`.
  Measured effect over 16 Nim repos in this workspace: 10,305 -> 10,901
  discovered cases and 2,468 -> 2,602 suites across 115 files; the "no literal
  suite/test declarations" warning went 24 files -> 1 (the survivor is a helper
  module that imports unittest and declares nothing — a true positive).
  MIRROR DEFECT, also fixed: `suite`/`test` inside a `when false:` block were
  being counted. The compiler never instantiates them, so no runner can produce
  them; they are now skipped with an `info` diagnostic naming the count. That
  is the only *semantic* rule in an otherwise purely lexical scan.
  Regression guards: `src/ct_test/nim_lexer_test.nim`, the `ct-test Nim
  unittest lexical regressions` suite in `nim_unittest_provider_test.nim`, and
  the fixture `fixtures/nim_unittest_project/tests/test_lexical_edge_cases.nim`
  — which is COMPILABLE, so `nim c -r` on it is an independent ground truth for
  what discovery must report (1 suite + 5 cases).

- 2026-09-08 (ct-test Nim framework detection: a statement is not a line): the
  framework scan in `frameworks/nim_unittest.nim` read ONE PHYSICAL LINE and
  called it the `import` statement. A bracketed clause continued on the next
  line —
      import std/[os, strutils,
                  unittest]
  — therefore never showed `unittest`. That is not a small mis-read: a file
  whose framework is not detected is never scanned for declarations at all, so
  it left the catalog whole. Measured on the reprobuild workspace: 46 sources
  and 200 cases were absent for this reason alone (1,394 -> 1,440 addressable
  sources, 8,028 -> 8,228 cases), with no file's count moving and no identity
  changing.
  The clause extent is now decided by the token stream (`importClauseEnd`): the
  statement continues while a bracket is open or the line ended on `,`/`/`/`.`,
  and ends at a line break or a `;`. That also picks up `import` inside a
  `when …:` branch and two clauses separated by `;`, and makes an `import`
  written inside a string literal a non-event. STILL NOT COVERED: `include`
  (the included file's imports are not followed) and `import unittest as u` /
  `except`, both by design.
  MIRROR DEFECT, also fixed — and the reason this sat unnoticed: the negative
  diagnostic was the bare sentence `no Nim unittest imports detected in file`,
  emitted identically for a file with no test import and for a file whose
  import the scan failed to read. 206 rows in one workspace report, 46 of them
  false, indistinguishable. A conclusion with no evidence attached cannot be
  audited: `noFrameworkMessage` now names the modules the scan actually read,
  and names any `include` whose imports it did not follow.
  Regression guards: the `ct-test Nim import clause scanning` and `ct-test Nim
  unittest detection diagnostics` suites in `nim_unittest_provider_test.nim`
  (each continuation form paired with a shape that must NOT be swallowed, so
  neither "join everything" nor "join nothing" passes), plus the fixture
  `fixtures/nim_unittest_project/tests/test_lexical_edge_cases.nim`, whose
  import now deliberately spans two lines.

- 2026-08-13 (ct-test silent output truncation): `execCaptured` bounds captured
  child output at `DefaultCaptureLimit` (16 MiB) and used to drop the fact that
  it had cut anything. `CapturedRun` now carries `outputBytes` (the true
  pre-bound count, from runquota_process's `stdoutBytes`/`stderrBytes`) and
  `truncated`. ANY consumer that parses a LIST-shaped output must check it: a
  prefix of a list is indistinguishable from a short complete list, so the loss
  is invisible where a truncated JSON/XML document would at least fail to
  parse. `workspace_scope.vcsInventory` refuses a truncated `git ls-files -z`
  outright — git emits paths sorted, so truncation drops a contiguous tail of
  the alphabet — and `auto` falls back to the pruning walk while `vcs` errors.
  Test seam: `setVcsInventoryCaptureLimit` / `clearVcsInventoryCaptureLimit`
  (lower the bound; do not try to materialise ~280,000 files).
- 2026-08-14 (#558): A `for` or an `if` inside an IsoNim `ui()` block is a ONE-SHOT STRUCTURAL construct. `processForStmt` / `processIfStmt` in `isonim/src/isonim/dsl/ui.nim:167-178, :249-250` emit a bare `nnkForStmt` / `nnkIfStmt` with NO `createRenderEffect` wrapper — unlike dynamic attributes (:366-379) and dynamic text (:278-292), which ARE wrapped. So the construct runs exactly once, when the node is built, and never again. `indexEach` does not save you: it calls `body(...)` once per index and thereafter only assigns `itemSignals[i].val`, which fires the row's attribute/text effects but not its structural loops. Any list whose length can change after mount, and any subtree behind a condition that can flip after mount, must use `indexEach` / `forEachKeyed` on a `ref =`-captured container, attached AFTER the `ui()` block. Diagnostic signature: a container whose `display` (a dynamic attribute, therefore reactive) updates correctly while its children stay frozen at their construction-time value — i.e. "the panel opens but is empty". This was #558's root cause in `views/isonim_state_view.nim`, and the adjacent origin-chain block had BOTH failure modes at once (a `for` over hops plus an `if chain.isSome` guarding the terminator). Note that when one `indexEach` covers heterogeneous rows, per-row class names must become reactive expressions too, because `indexEach` reuses a node positionally and a position can change role as the list grows.
- 2026-08-14 (#558): `common/common_types/*` is INCLUDEd, not imported, and `common/types.nim` binds `langstring = string` while `frontend/types.nim` binds `langstring = cstring`. Every type spelled in terms of `langstring` therefore EXISTS TWICE with incompatible layouts (`HistoryResult` is one). A `cast[seq[T]]` between the two compiles and is only ever sound because the JS backend erases the difference — it is a landmine on any native/headless path. When a ViewModel needs such a payload, give the VM its own backend-neutral row type and normalise at the single host boundary where the wire message is received, rather than casting. Removing that cast from `state_vm.nim` also dropped a heavy include-monolith from a headless VM.
- 2026-08-14 (test hygiene, from the pxor audit): several tests in this repo pass while the bug they name is present, and the pattern is always the same — they observe a FRESHLY CONSTRUCTED object instead of the long-lived one the app actually mounts. `test_state_value_history_toggle` called `renderStatePanel(...)` again after `updateHistory`, so the one-shot `for` ran after the data landed and the rows existed. Assert against the already-mounted panel. Related smells found in the same audit: a test whose only assertion is `check "flow-taken" == "flow-taken"`; two tests driving a `Signal` that nothing in production writes; and `retry()` in `src/tests/gui/lib/retry-helpers.ts:65-110` returning `void` and never throwing on exhaustion, so a `retry(...)` with no following assertion always passes.
- 2026-08-14 (#612): The frontend event bus (`frontend/communication.nim`) had
  NO teardown path until this fix: `subscribe` appended to `handlers[kind]`,
  `registerSubscriber` appended to `subscribers[kind]`, and nothing ever
  removed either. Every UI component gets a private mediator
  (`types.setupLocalViewToMiddlewareApi`) that registers itself as a subscriber
  of `data.viewsApi`, so a destroyed panel kept reacting to events forever.
  Panels are destroyed and re-created routinely — `layout.closeLayoutTab`,
  `renderer.resetLayoutState` (runs on every re-record / trace reload) and
  `session_switch.closeSession` all dropped components from
  `ui.componentMapping` without unsubscribing — so one emit was delivered once
  per accumulated generation. That is the real "Add to Scratchpad adds the
  value 4 times" mechanism; it is NOT a DOM listener leak (the `+` button is a
  fresh element every time the tippy popup is rebuilt). `Component.unregister`
  (`types.nim`) is now the counterpart of `register` and MUST be called at any
  new component-destruction site; it clears `api` so the component can be
  registered again. Diagnostic signature of this class of bug: a multiplier
  that is CONSTANT for a whole session (one per generation) rather than growing
  as the user steps.
- 2026-08-14: `utils.generateId` hands out the LOWEST FREE id, not a
  monotonically increasing one, so a re-opened panel normally gets the id its
  predecessor had. Module-global "already mounted" maps keyed by component id
  (`isoNimScratchpadMountedIds` and its siblings in trace_log / request_panel /
  filesystem) must therefore be cleared on teardown, or the next panel with the
  same id silently never mounts its IsoNim view. Likewise, a module-global
  `<panel>ComponentRef` must be re-pointed at the NEWEST registration —
  latching onto the first one (as `scratchpad.nim` did) leaves the ref aimed at
  a DOM container that no longer exists.
- 2026-08-14: `utils.openLayoutTab` has TWO reuse rules, and picking the wrong
  one makes a panel silently do nothing. Panels are singletons by default:
  asking for one that is already attached just re-focuses component 0 and
  RETURNS — no tab, no log, no exception. A panel asked for with
  `isEditor = true` is instead an editor-area *document* keyed by
  `Component.independentTabPath` (`opensAsIndependentTab` in
  `common_types/codetracer_features/frontend.nim`, shared by the JS and C type
  trees so the rule is unit-testable). The VCS panel's "View Diff" was routed
  through the singleton rule for months: the docked VCS panel is always
  component 0 and always attached, so every click re-activated it and returned,
  and the ENTIRE downstream path (tab titles in `layout.nim`, the `diff:`
  branch of `ensureVCSDataLoaded`, `VCSComponent.diffTarget`) was dead code
  that had never executed once — including a missing `isGitRepo = true` that
  would have rendered "Not a git repository" instead of the diff. Symptom to
  recognise: a button whose whole feature never ran, with nothing in the
  console. Note also that the `similarParent` grouping loop further down must
  skip singleton siblings, or an `isEditor` tab lands in the sidebar stack the
  singleton lives in.
- 2026-08-14: `VCSVM.unifiedDiffActive` means "this panel instance IS a diff"
  (a View Diff tab, or an agentic-session review diff), NOT "the Unified Diff
  view mode is selected". The view-mode toggle is `VCSVM.viewMode`; wiring the
  toggle to `unifiedDiffActive` replaces the commit history with a diff, which
  is what issue #561 originally reported.
- 2026-08-14 (#562/#593/#595/#606): `FlowMode::Call` must start the flow window at the ENCLOSING CALL ENTRY, for both trace kinds. `move_to_first_step` in `src/db-backend/src/flow_preloader.rs` is the SINGLE place that decides the window start — do not add a second widening at the `load_flow` pre-pass gate (:267-283), because `jump_to_call` + `StepIn` there followed by another `jump_to_call` in `move_to_first_step` rewinds twice and undoes the `StepIn`. From 2026-04-28 (`3d722b15`) to 2026-08-14 the materialized branch did NOT widen, so the window restarted at the current statement on every step and `load_view_update`'s forward-only walk produced a shrinking window. One regression, four reported bugs: the sliding-window flow scope (#606), a loop counter stuck at 0 (#593), a total that counted down as you stepped, and an apparent 2-iteration skip per arrow click (#595). The revert was pinned in place by a unit test asserting the truncated contract whose RR sibling asserted the opposite — when two tests of the same function assert opposite things for different backends, that asymmetry is the bug, not the design.
- 2026-08-14: `Db::jump_to_call` (`src/db-backend/src/db.rs:2722-2737`) resolves `step_id -> step.call_key -> calls[call_key].step_id`, i.e. the entry step of the EXACT enclosing call INSTANCE — not the function's first call. Rewinding for flow is also safe with respect to the user's position: `Handler::load_flow` (`dap_handler.rs:1122-1131`) builds a SEPARATE replay session.
- 2026-08-14 / superseded 2026-08-16: `FlowVM` (`src/frontend/viewmodel/viewmodels/flow_vm.nim`) used to send `ct/load-flow` and drop the response, so `iterationCount`, `steps` and `loadingState` were never written by production code (only by tests and `storybook_components.nim`); two "FlowVM loop iteration" tests that wrote `iterationCount` themselves and then asserted a setter certified #593/#595 as fixed while both were broken. It now CONSUMES the reply (`applyFlowUpdate`): it stores the window's loops and `location.rrTicks` and re-derives `selectedIteration` with `flow_loop_math.activeIterationForTicks`. Before writing a ViewModel test, still check that production code actually writes the signal you are asserting on.
- 2026-08-16 (#593/#595, the GUI half): the Omniscience loop counter reads `loopStates[loop].activeIteration` via `activeLoopIterationFor`, and THAT TABLE IS THROWN AWAY AND REBUILT ON EVERY DEBUGGER MOVE, twice over: `EditorViewComponent.loadFlow` (`ui/editor.nim`) constructs a brand-new `FlowComponent` with an empty `loopStates` whenever `rrTicks` differ (i.e. always), and `redrawFlow` -> `clear` -> `resetFlow` -> `clearLoopStates` empties it again on every redraw. So `createLoopStates` (`ui/flow.nim`) is not a top-up, it is the ONLY writer that runs before the control's DOM is built, and if it seeds `activeIteration` with the default 0 the counter reads 0 forever no matter where the debugger is — and the next arrow click computes `0 + 1` and jumps the user back to iteration 1. It now seeds from `self.location.rrTicks` through `activeIterationForTicks`. Corollary: never repaint a loop control with `redrawFlow()` after an optimistic `selectLoopIteration` — the clear discards the selection and re-derives it from the still-stale pre-jump location. Use `updateLoopControlDom(position)`.
- 2026-08-16 (#593/#595, the lifecycle half): because `loadFlow` builds a NEW
  `FlowComponent` per move (see the entry above), the OUTGOING one is not torn
  down — it is still referenced by its own pending `setTimeout` closures
  (`scheduleFlowRedraw`, the 0/100/500/1000/2000 ms
  `scheduleActiveLoopIterationValueRender` fan-out, `afterJump`'s 50 ms
  `redrawFlow`). Those fired ~100 ms into the NEW load and ran a full
  `redrawFlow()` against the PREVIOUS debugger position: they re-registered
  Monaco view zones that only the retired component's `loopViewZones` knows
  about (so the live component's `clear()` can never remove them — two loop
  controls in the editor) and repainted the loop counter with the old
  iteration. Concretely: click forward, the debugger DID move, the zombie
  repainted the old number, the next click read that number, recomputed the
  same target it had already jumped to, `needsFlowReload` was then false and
  the counter froze — "expected 3, received 2" forever. `loadFlow` now sets
  `FlowComponent.superseded`; `flowIsLive` gates `redrawFlow`,
  `recalculateAndRedrawFlow`, `onUpdatedFlow` and the two timer callbacks.
  It is deliberately "stop painting", not "tear down": until the replacement
  renders, the retired component owns the DOM the user clicks and holds the
  optimistic `activeIteration`. Any NEW deferred work in `ui/flow.nim` must
  check `flowIsLive` first.
- 2026-08-16: `selectLoopIteration` jumps to the iteration's FIRST BODY
  STATEMENT (`firstLoopBodyStepForIteration`), not to the `for`/`while` header.
  Both land on the right iteration — `FlowMode.Call` returns the whole call, so
  `rrTicksForIterations` is identical wherever the cursor sits and
  `activeIterationForTicks` maps a body tick onto its containing header
  interval — but the header target leaves the cursor on the loop condition,
  which is what `NoirSpaceShip.SimpleLoopIterationJump` / `LoopIterationSlider`
  detect (they assert the editor's active line is `shield.nr:5`, the first body
  line, after entering an iteration number).
- 2026-08-16: `FlowLine.loopIds` (`src/frontend/types.nim`) is initialised to `@[]` in `makeFlowLine` (`ui/flow.nim`) and in the twin literal in `ui/editor.nim`, and NOTHING ANYWHERE APPENDS TO IT. Every reader is therefore dead: `setFlowLineActiveIteration` never runs its body, so `FlowLine.activeLoopIteration` stays `(-1, -1)` and the `.active-flow-step` marker plus the per-iteration value refresh at the end of `updateFlowOnMove` never fire; `calclulateFlowLineTotalWidth` always returns 0. The loop counter and the slider handle do NOT depend on it (both read `loopStates[..].activeIteration`, via `activeLoopIterationFor` and `ensureLoopSlider`), which is why the panel works at all. Populating `loopIds` would switch several width/offset paths on at once and needs GUI verification.
- 2026-08-14: a Python `for` loop of N iterations reports N+1 iteration entries, because CPython executes the loop header one extra time (the visit that exhausts the iterator) and `process_loops` counts header visits. This is a real off-by-one in the DISPLAYED total, separate from the window-start bug, and is language/recorder-shaped rather than window-shaped.
- 2026-08-14 (#594): `EditorViewComponent.decorations` used to be a flat
  `seq[(DeltaDecoration, bool)]` in which the bool was ALWAYS `true`, so
  `styleLines`'s `filterIt(not it[1])` kept nothing and every call replaced the
  whole Monaco decoration set. That is fatal for any layer whose input is
  temporarily unavailable: `onCompleteMove` reloads the flow on every step,
  `loadFlow` installs a new `FlowComponent` with `flow == nil`, and
  `editorAfterRedraw` runs SYNCHRONOUSLY right there — so `flow-taken` /
  `flow-not-taken` computed empty and Monaco removed them. The bool is now a
  layer tag (`ui/editor_decoration_layers.nim`): the flow layer is RETAINED
  while `self.flow.flow` is nil and replaced only when real data arrives.
  Repaint sites that did not exist before: `EditorViewComponent.onUpdatedFlow`
  (data arrival) and `refreshFlowAfterActivation` (tab activation) — note that
  neither `redrawFlow` overload touches line styles, the EditorViewComponent
  one only computes `maxFlowLineWidth`.
- 2026-08-14 (#566): a Monaco view zone whose `innerHTML` you wipe takes any
  jQuery-DataTables instance built against a table inside it with it. The
  instance survives in `TraceComponent.dataTable.context`, `renderTableResults`
  only builds a table when that field is nil, and `traceChartTableDom` creates
  the container as `chart-table hidden` with `refreshTrace` the only code that
  un-hides it — so the grid VANISHES rather than going blank. `editorAfterRedraw`
  now dispatches on `ui/trace_redraw_policy.nim` (skip / refresh-in-place /
  rebuild) instead of rebuilding every expanded tracepoint on every move, and
  `refreshTraceViewZoneDom` releases the DataTable first and calls `refreshTrace`
  last. Rule of thumb: before wiping a subtree, ask which JS library still holds
  a reference into it.
- 2026-08-14 (#602): `Db::load_locals` (`src/db-backend/src/db.rs`) is
  POINT-IN-TIME for every language: it reports what the recorder wrote for the
  current step, nothing else. The JavaScript special case that unioned the whole
  frame's history (prefix-scan from `call.step_id`, last-write-wins `HashMap`) is
  gone; `is_javascript_frame` went with it (`is_javascript_module_frame` stays,
  it has a separate caller). Completeness is the RECORDER's job: since M37 the JS
  recorder emits the full in-scope local set on every step. Note the two
  recorders disagree on WHEN a step's snapshot is taken — the JS recorder's step
  for `const base = a + b;` already carries `base` (post-line), while the Ruby
  recorder's step for the same line carries the state before it ran (pre-line).
  Verify against a live dump before asserting a value on an assignment line.
- 2026-08-14: `TestRecording::create_db_trace` names its temp dir from
  `(language, format, version_label, pid, source-path hash)`. Two tests in the
  SAME test binary recording the SAME source therefore collide and race —
  symptom: "failed to rename trace dir" from whichever one loses. Fold the test
  name into `version_label` (see `tests/javascript_flow_dap_test.rs::trace_label`).
- 2026-08-14 (#608): `activeItemIndex` is a first-class GoldenLayout *stack*
  config field that round-trips verbatim through `StackItemConfig.fromResolved`
  (`node_modules/golden-layout/src/ts/config/config.ts:358`) and is RANGE-CHECKED
  with a `throw` in `Stack.init` (`items/stack.ts:169-171`). Any code that
  removes children from a stack config must remap it — the save-side sanitiser
  did not, so an ordinary replay session (editor tabs sharing their stack with
  "NO SOURCE" / "CALLS") wrote a layout file that could only be recovered with
  `just reset-layout`. The sanitise/repair logic now lives in the
  dependency-free `src/frontend/index/layout_config_repair.nim` (only
  `std/jsffi`) so it can be exercised under plain `nim js` + node.
  Related traps in the same area:
  * GoldenLayout signals a rejected config with a NATIVE JS `Error`, which a Nim
    `try/except` does not catch. Every `loadLayout` / `initLayout` call site
    needs a raw-JS `{.emit.}` try/catch — see `loadLayoutSafely` in
    `ui/layout.nim` and `callInitLayoutSafe` in `ui/session_switch.nim`.
  * Saved `size` strings are FRACTIONAL percentages (`RowOrColumn` recomputes
    them as `(size / total) * 100`, `items/row-or-column.ts:441`). Validate them
    the way `parseSize` (`config/config.ts:1249`) does, not with an integer
    regex, or a "repair" silently resets the user's panel proportions.
  * Pinning a panel to an auto-hide edge REMOVES it from the GoldenLayout tree.
    Anything that asks "is panel X in the layout?" must also consult the
    auto-hide state (`~/.config/codetracer/auto_hide_state.json`), or pinning
    FILES makes the loader declare the layout incompatible and delete it.
  * The GUI fixture copies the bundled default over `default_layout.json` on
    EVERY test setup (`src/tests/gui/lib/layout-reset.ts`), and the index
    process reads the layout file exactly once at startup. A layout-loading
    spec must therefore write `default_edit_layout.json` from a `beforeEach`
    that requests no fixture — writing after `ctPage.reload()` tests nothing.
- 2026-08-14 (#603): `services.editor.open` is NOT a list of visible source
  tabs. `tabLoad` (`utils.nim`) inserts every entry straight from the IPC
  payload, long before the Monaco editor component mounts and assigns
  `TabInfo.monacoEditor`, and it also inserts calltrace / instruction tabs keyed
  `path:functionName-key` that never get an editor at all. Anything that walks
  `open` and dereferences `monacoEditor` must nil-guard, or it throws a native
  `TypeError` on a perfectly ordinary session. That is what made Ctrl+R
  re-record do nothing at all: `saveFiles` threw *after* `pendingReRecord` had
  been armed and *before* a single `save-file` went out, so the queue was set,
  nothing was saved, nothing could drain it and nothing was reported.
  Related traps in the same area:
  * The decision model now lives in the dependency-free
    `src/frontend/file_conflicts.nim` (`requestReRecord` / `noteSaveOutcome` /
    `applyConflictAction`), exercised natively by
    `src/tests/gui/tests/welcome-screen/{file_conflicts,re_record_queue}_vm_test.nim`.
    Its two load-bearing invariants are "a failed save aborts" and "dirty
    buffers with nothing in flight aborts" — both are states the old code
    waited in forever.
  * A `writeFile` produces SEVERAL inotify `change` events, and up to three
    watchers are registered per file (`fs.watch` on the file, `fs.watch` on the
    directory, `fs.watchFile` polling). A counter that masks one event per
    self-write cannot work; `index/config.nim` uses a time-boxed
    `selfWriteUntilMs` window instead. Before that, saving a file raised the
    "File changed on disk" dialog for the user's OWN save.
  * Main-process IPC sends must carry the `CODETRACER::` prefix — the renderer
    subscribes to the namespaced channel only, so an unprefixed send is
    silently dropped (`traces.nim`'s `sendNotification` did this for every
    record-progress message).
  * `data.trace`, `data.services` and friends are Nim *templates* over
    `sessions[activeSessionIndex]`, so they do not exist as JS properties.
    Playwright specs must read `d.sessions[d.activeSessionIndex].trace`;
    assigning `data.trace` from `page.evaluate` is inert.

## The caption chrome is rebuilt by every `data.redraw()` (issue #555)

`renderer.redrawAll` — which `data.redraw` is bound to — calls
`renderer.sharedDirectRedraw`, installed by `ui/layout.nim`, and that calls
`ui/menu.nim`'s `requestMenuRender`. So EVERY `data.redraw()` anywhere in the
renderer reaches the menu.

The surprising part: `#isonim-debug-controls`, `#debug` and the session tab-bar
host are *not* static elements of `index.html` (no HTML file in the repo
mentions them). They are emitted by
`viewmodel/views/isonim_menu_shell_view.nim`, and `renderMenuShellInto` starts
with `clearChildren(container)`. A menu render therefore destroys the mounted
IsoNim debug toolbar every time, and `ui/debug.nim`'s
`requestDebugControlsRender` mounts a fresh one. A clean trace open was
measured re-mounting it ~45 times — the button flicker of #555.

`ui/menu_render_gate.nim` now gates the rebuild on a content signature of the
`MenuShellModel`. Two things it must not forget, both covered by
`src/tests/gui/tests/session-chrome/menu_redraw_storm_test.nim`:

* Object identity is useless here — `data.webTechMenu` builds a brand-new
  `MenuNode` tree on every call, so structurally identical menus are never the
  same objects.
* `MenuComponent` is per ReplaySession while `#menu` is one shared host, and the
  shell callbacks close over the component, so the gate is invalidated when the
  owning component changes as well as when the DOM host is emptied.

Still open in this area: `mountIsoNimDebugControls`
(`views/isonim_debug_controls_view.nim`) calls `createRenderEffect` with no
owner and no disposal, so every re-mount leaks a live set of reactive effects
bound to detached DOM.

## `array[N, bool]` becomes a `Uint8Array` in the JS backend

Nim's JS codegen represents an array literal of small scalars with a typed
array: `[true, false]` compiles to `new Uint8Array([true, false])`. Any JS
library that validates an option with `Array.isArray()` rejects it.

This is not hypothetical — it silently broke the Omniscience loop slider for
the entire lifetime of the feature. `ui/flow.nim`'s `ensureLoopSlider` passed
`"connect": [true, false]` to `noUiSlider.create`, whose `testConnect` does
exactly that `Array.isArray` check, so EVERY create threw
`noUiSlider: 'connect' option doesn't match handle count.`. `.noUi-base` was
therefore never injected, the `slide` handler was never wired, and because the
throw escaped through `resizeFlowSlider` the rest of that render pass was
abandoned too (issue #562). It now passes noUiSlider's `"lower"` string
shorthand, which means the same thing and survives the translation.

When handing a Nim array to a JS API, prefer a `seq`, a string shorthand the
library documents, or an explicit `{.importjs: "[#, #]".}` helper — and check
the generated `src/build-debug/ui.js` if the API is fussy about its argument
types.

## The VCS panel's infinite-scroll sentinel is a busy loop on small repos

`viewmodel/views/isonim_vcs_view.nim` rebuilds the whole panel subtree inside
one `createRenderEffect`, and the rebuilt subtree ends with a
`.vcs-load-more-sentinel` under a fresh `IntersectionObserver`. On any
repository whose history is shorter than the panel, that sentinel is inside
the viewport the moment it is attached, so it fires `onLoadMoreCommits`
immediately — which used to shell out to `git log` for a page that does not
exist and publish the unchanged commit list anyway, triggering the next
rebuild. Measured at ~60 `git log` processes and ~440 DOM rebuilds per second;
nothing in the panel could be clicked because every element was detached again
before a click could land. `VCSComponent.allCommitsLoaded` now stops the paging
once a short page comes back.

The general lesson: a render effect that recreates an observer, a timer or an
event source must not be able to reach its own trigger. Measure it with a
`MutationObserver` on the panel host — a Playwright "element is not stable" or
"element was detached from the DOM, retrying" failure is what a render storm
looks like from the outside.

## Menu rows carry two class vocabularies, and only one of them is styling

A row in the global menu (`viewmodel/views/isonim_menu_shell_view.nim`,
`menuItemClass`) renders BOTH the design-system component classes
(`ct-menu-item` + `--active` / `--disabled`, styled by
`styles/components/menu_item.styl`) AND a set of styling-free semantic hooks:
`menu-node`, `menu-element` / `menu-folder`, the per-entry identity class from
`node.nameClass` (`menu-folder-debug`, `menu-element-ruby-fibonacci`, built by
`ui/menu.nim`) and `menu-enabled` / `menu-disabled`. The second set is the
published selector contract of the GUI suite —
`welcome-screen/launch_config.spec.ts` and
`codetracer-specs/Testing/UI-Test-Catalog.md` § Launch Configuration Tests
drive the whole Debug > Launch Configurations flow through it.

A pure restyle onto `ct-menu-item` once deleted all of them, which left the
menu looking fine and made every one of those Playwright selectors match
nothing. If you restyle the menu, keep the hooks; and keep them out of
`menu_bar.styl`'s selectors — `#menu .menu-node` outranks `.ct-menu-item`, so
styling a hook silently overrides the component it is supposed to be neutral
about.

## Tests that grep production source break on refactors that change nothing

`session-chrome/new_trace_caption_chrome_test.nim` guards JS-only Electron
code by reading its source text, because a native `nim c -r` binary cannot run
it. That is a legitimate last resort, but it is not free: its edit-layout
sanitiser assertions were matching the *implementation text* of
`sanitizeEditLayoutConfig`, and issue #608's extraction of that logic into
`index/layout_config_repair.nim` failed the test while the behaviour was
intact. Where the module under test is dependency-free (`layout_config_repair`
imports nothing but `std/jsffi`), run it for real in the file's
`when defined(js)` branch — `test-vm-js` executes those with node — and leave
the native branch only the claims a native run can actually establish.

## The auto-hide edge strip only renders from `autoHideState.onChanged`

`__ctRedrawAll` does NOT redraw the auto-hide strips. `renderer.redrawAll`
calls `sharedDirectRedraw`, which `ui/layout.nim` installs to refresh the menu,
the status bar and the fixed search box — nothing else. `#auto-hide-strip-left`
/ `-right`, `#auto-hide-bottom-strip` and `#auto-hide-collapsed-icon-zone` are
mounted exclusively from the `autoHideState.onChanged` callback in
`initLayout`. Anything that mutates the pinned-panel set must go through that
hook, and a test that "forces a strip redraw" with `__ctRedrawAll` is measuring
nothing.

Two consequences worth remembering:

* `onChanged` rebuilds each strip from scratch (`renderAutoHideSideStripInto`
  clears the host and re-appends), so every spurious `onChanged` detaches the
  strip tabs. `locator.hover()` on a tab then fails with "element is not
  stable" / "element was detached from the DOM", and a `toHaveCount` poll can
  land in the emptied window. If a strip tab is unhoverable, look for an
  over-firing `onChanged`, not for a missing render.
* An *open* request must use `auto_hide.revealOverlay`, never `showOverlay`.
  `showOverlay` implements the strip tab's click gesture and toggles, so a
  caller that fires repeatedly (one per search-result batch, one per debugger
  step re-opening the current file) alternates show/hide and drives exactly
  that rebuild loop.

## Collapsed mode makes strip tabs disappear, and Xvfb always looks maximized

`updateCollapsedMode` (ui/layout.nim) decides collapsed mode from
`outerWidth >= screen.availWidth - 8 && outerHeight >= availHeight - 8`. Under
Xvfb the Electron window fills the virtual screen, so the answer is always
"maximized" and the side strips render as a 1 px `collapsed-strip-line` with no
`.auto-hide-strip-tab` at all. `__ctForceCollapsedMode(false)` is now a sticky
override for exactly this reason; before that it was silently overwritten by
the next `resize` event, mid-test.

In collapsed mode the replacement affordance is
`#auto-hide-collapsed-icon-zone` in the status bar
(`Planned-Features/Auto-Hide-Panes.md` §10, "the panel directory").

## The recent-traces list is startup-path state, not welcome-screen state

`Data.recentTraces` / `Data.recentFolders` are global (not session-forwarded)
fields, and the welcome surface of *any* session tab reads them through
`ui/welcome_screen.nim:syncLegacyWelcomeScreenIntoVM`. They used to be filled
only by `ui_js.onWelcomeScreen`, i.e. only when the process was launched into
the welcome screen — so `ct run <program>` left them empty forever and the tab
bar's "+" opened a blank Recent Traces panel (#568).

`src/frontend/index/recent_items.nim` now owns the decision of which startup
path gets the lists over which channel, and `index/startup.nim` routes every
exit of `init` through it. If you add a startup path to `init`, add it to
`StartupPath` too: `recent_items_startup_vm_test.nim` iterates the enum and
fails if a welcome-capable path delivers nothing.

`findRecentTracesWithCodetracer` and friends `app.quit(1)` on a failed
`codetracer trace-metadata` run; pass `quitOnError = false` from any path that
already has a live debugging session on screen.

## GUI-test `sourcePath` is relative to `test-programs/`, never to the CWD

`test.use({ sourcePath: "noir_space_ship/" })` is resolved by
`lib/fixtures.ts` as `path.join(testProgramsPath, sourcePath)`. Anything that
inspects the same string has to apply the identical rule —
`lib/lang-support.ts:isDbBased` used `path.resolve(sourcePath)` (i.e. the
process CWD), so every relative *folder* looked non-existent, its DB-marker
(`Nargo.toml`, `Forc.toml`, `Move.toml`, `program.json`, `aiken.toml`,
`flow.json`) was never found, the language was classified as RR-based and the
fixture skipped the test with "requires ct-native-replay". That silently hid
all 18 `noir-space-ship` specs plus the noir smoke/page-object/scratchpad
suites. Use `resolveTestProgramPath()` for any new classification helper.

## The context menu's shortcut hint is `.ct-menu-item-sublabel`

`viewmodel/views/context_menu_bridge.nim` renders each entry as
`.ct-menu-item-label` + optional `.ct-menu-item-sublabel`. The page object
`page-objects/components/context-menu.ts` used to look for
`.context-menu-hint`, which exists nowhere, so `getEntries()` returned
"Add value to scratchpad\nCTRL+<click on value>" as the action name for the
only menus that declare hints (flow values, `ui/flow.nim`). jstree menus keep
their own selectors via `createJsTreeContextMenu`.

## `common_types` types exist TWICE, and a generic proc is how you share code

`src/common/common_types.nim` is `include`d by both `src/common/types.nim`
(`langstring = string`, native) and `src/frontend/types.nim`
(`langstring = cstring`, renderer). Every type in it — `DeepReviewData`,
`Diff`, `Trace`, … — therefore exists as two *distinct* types, and a module
that names one has picked a backend. `state_vm.nim` records what happens when
both end up in scope: "every type in it exists twice and the two copies are
incompatible".

Two ways to write code once and run it against both copies:

1. **A generic proc in a ViewModel module.** `review_entry.reviewDatasetFrom[T]`
   projects a `DeepReviewData` into a plain ViewModel value; the renderer
   instantiates it over its copy, the headless tests over the native one, and
   both run the same source. Read `langstring` fields with `$`.
2. **A proc inside `common_types` itself.** Anything that maps between two
   `common_types` types belongs there — it is compiled once per copy for free.
   `codetracer_features/deepreview_from_diff.nim` (a trace's structured `Diff`
   → a review dataset) and `utils/text_representation.nim` are the examples.
   Convert with `langstring(x)`, which is `cstring(x)` or `string(x)` as
   appropriate.

A headless test *can* `import ../../../../common/types as ct_types` alongside
ViewModel modules (`vcs_vm_test.nim`, `deepreview_entry_test.nim` do); what it
must not do is drag `frontend/types` in as well.

## Review entry has exactly one routine — do not add a second

All three DeepReview launch paths (`ct review`, a trace with an
associated diff, the agentic handoff) publish `data.deepReviewData` and then
call `vcs.startDeepReviewNavigation(data)` → `review_entry.enterReview`, which
populates the VCS panel, the Agent Activity DeepReview section and the editor.
It is idempotent by design: the data half re-runs on every call (the agentic
launcher re-syncs constantly) while the entry half — focus the panels, open
the first file — is gated by `VCSVM.reviewEntered` and the selection is
carried across a refresh *by path*. `src/tests/gui/tests/deepreview/
deepreview_entry_test.nim` holds both a behavioural suite and a source
contract asserting the hosts still call it.

## `ct review` is DeepReview's whole CLI, and it is dispatched in two places

`src/ct/review_cli.nim` holds a *pure* planner, `planReviewCli(argv)`, that
resolves a `ct review …` line into a `ReviewPlan` with no filesystem and no
subprocess. That is what makes the dispatch assertable on both Nim backends
(`src/tests/gui/tests/deepreview/ct_review_cli_test.nim` runs under `nim c`
*and* `nim js`). Everything effectful — resolving the dataset on disk, shelling
out to the native collector — is in the file's `when not defined(js)` half.

The two callers are deliberate and asymmetric:

* `src/ct/codetracer.nim` intercepts `ct review collect|inspect` **before**
  confutils parses argv, because those verbs carry flags that are not ct's
  (`--repo`, `--diff-file`, `--preset`) and confutils rejects any unknown
  dash-prefixed token. Same technique as `ct-complete` and `ct record --`.
* `src/ct/launch/launch.nim` handles `ct review <PATH>` as an ordinary
  `StartupCommand.review`, so it keeps ct's global options (`--inspect`,
  `--remote-debugging-port`) that Playwright injects.

Both call the planner, so they cannot disagree about what a line means.

`reviewNeedsRawDispatch` must key on the VERB alone — `collect`, `inspect`,
`export` — and never on argument count. Claiming the line as soon as a second
token followed the path made `ct review DIR --remote-debugging-port=0` fail
with "exactly one dataset path", i.e. it took back the one property the split
exists to preserve. Extra positionals are confutils' job: it answers them with
"The subcommand 'review' does not accept additional arguments", the same way it
does for `ct edit`.

Retired, and they fail rather than alias: `ct --deepreview <PATH>` (diagnosed
deliberately in `codetracer.nim` — confutils' default names no replacement) and
`ct-native-replay deepreview {collect,export,inspect}` (the group was renamed to
a hidden `review-data` that `ct review` invokes; a hidden `deepreview` arm
survives only to print the pointer). The *frontend* `--deepreview <PATH>`
argument in `src/frontend/index/args.nim` is a different thing and is NOT
retired: it is the internal ct → Electron wire.

`ct review collect --output DIR` writes `DIR/review.json` as well as the binary
`.dr` chunks, because the GUI reads JSON and the `export` step that used to
bridge them is one of the retired commands. `ct review DIR` resolves the JSON
inside the directory.

- 2026-08-18 (RV-2): `ct review <PATH>` opens the EDITOR layout, not the
  debugging one. `index/startup.init`'s `withDeepReview` branch calls
  `index/config.loadReviewLayoutConfig` over
  `userLayoutDir / "default_edit_layout.json"` and forwards THAT in
  `CODETRACER::start-deepreview`; it no longer forwards the `layout` argument
  `init` was handed. A dataset launch loads no recording, so EVENT LOG,
  CALLTRACE, TIMELINE and TERMINAL OUTPUT used to come up present but empty.

  Two traps live here:

  * `editModeHiddenContentIds()` hides `Content.AgentActivity` and
    `Content.AgentActivityDeepReview` — DeepReview's THIRD PILLAR. A review
    therefore uses `reviewModeHiddenContentIds()`, which is the edit-mode set
    minus `reviewPillarContentIds()`. Never "just reuse the edit set" for a
    review: it deletes the Agent Activity panel and silently makes
    `deepreview_layout.focusReviewActivityPane` a no-op.

    That derived set is NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT, and the difference is
    the whole trap. It only stops the LOADER removing the panel. The same
    file is WRITTEN by `index/window.onSaveConfig` through
    `sanitizeEditLayoutJson`, whose hidden set *does* contain AgentActivity —
    so once a user has opened a folder in edit mode, `default_edit_layout.json`
    holds FILES + VCS and nothing else, and there is no panel for the hidden
    set to spare. `deepreview_layout.ensureReviewActivityPane` (run first by
    `focusReviewPanels`) puts an absent pillar back, in a slot of its OWN:
    dropping it into the FILES/VCS stack would hide the VCS panel, because
    the activity pane is focused last and two tabs of one stack cannot both
    be visible.

    General shape, worth remembering: one file shared by two modes with
    different panel requirements, where one mode's SAVE is the other mode's
    LOAD. Reasoning about the read path alone is not enough.
  * CodeTracer ships NO `default_edit_layout.json` (`src/config/` has only
    `default_layout.json`), so the edit-layout loader ALWAYS takes its
    fallback on a fresh install. The fallback reads the debugging layout and
    sanitises it, which is fine — but the loader's `resetLayoutToDefault`
    paths (corrupt / unrepairable / incompatible file) used to return the
    debugging layout RAW. They now go through
    `resetHiddenPanelLayoutToDefault`, which applies the caller's hidden set.
    Any new recovery path in that loader must do the same.

  Only the dataset launch changed. A trace with an associated diff
  (`vcs.startReviewForTraceDiff`) and the agentic handoff
  (`agentic_session_launcher.syncDeepReview`) enter the review from the
  renderer, on the layout the session already has, and keep the debugging
  layout — they have a recording, so those panels are populated.
  `renderer.saveConfig` still refuses to persist any layout while
  `deepReviewActive`, which is what stops a review's reduced layout being
  written over the user's `default_layout.json` (and what makes the
  materialised pillar above ephemeral — nothing is written to disk).

- 2026-08-18: GUI E2E tests share ONE `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` per Playwright worker
  (`lib/fixtures.ts` mkdtemps it at module scope). So anything a test leaves
  in `~/.config/codetracer` is input to every later test in that worker. The
  `ctPage` fixture resets all three pieces of the saved arrangement before a
  launch: `default_layout.json` (`ensureDefaultLayout`), `auto_hide_state.json`
  (`resetAutoHideState`) and — since a `ct review` launch reads it —
  `default_edit_layout.json` (`resetEditLayout`). Specs that seed one of those
  themselves must do it in a fixture-free `beforeEach` (which runs BEFORE the
  fixture) *and* opt out: `preserveAutoHideState` / `preserveEditLayout`.
  To launch over a layout a previous session would have written, use the
  `editLayoutPath` option instead of hand-writing the file.

- 2026-08-18 (RV-2): `ct review --help` / `-h` / `help` are intercepted by
  `reviewNeedsRawDispatch` and answered with `ReviewUsage`. Left to confutils
  they printed `ct review <reviewPath>` — the declared shape of the LAUNCH
  form only — and never named `collect` or `inspect`. `reviewNeedsRawDispatch`
  still keys on the exact token, so `ct review ./help` launches a dataset in a
  directory called `help`.

- 2026-08-18 (RV-4): `FlowMode::Diff` in the db-backend is NOT implemented.
  `flow_preloader::CallFlowPreloader::next_diff_flow_step` is a bare `todo!()`,
  so `FlowPreloader::load_diff_flow` panics ("not yet implemented") on its
  first call, and `diff::index_diff` — its only production caller, reached by
  `replay-server index-diff` — panics for the same reason. Anything that needs
  per-diff-line flow must drive `FlowMode::Call` per call instead, which is
  what `deepreview::collector` does (find the calls whose steps land on the
  diff's lines, ask for each of those calls' flow).

  Two things bit while doing that, both worth knowing before driving
  `FlowPreloader::load` from new code:
    * The anchor `Location` must have a NON-ZERO `rrTicks`. With
      `rrTicks == 0 && event == 0 && line > 0`,
      `should_seek_materialized_call_body_from_line_only_location` treats the
      request as line-only, sets a breakpoint on the line and continues to it —
      and if the line's only occurrence IS step 0, the breakpoint is never hit
      and the flow comes back with zero steps and no error.
    * `load_flow` widens any exact step to the ENCLOSING CALL ENTRY, so any
      step inside the call gives the same window. Picking the last matching
      step is therefore free and dodges the trap above.

- 2026-08-18 (RV-4): three on-disk layouts count as a materialized recording,
  and they are not interchangeable between loaders. `dap_server::setup` reads
  all three (a `*.ct` CTFS container, a legacy `runtime_tracing` `trace.json`,
  a legacy `trace.bin`); `diff::load_and_postprocess_trace` reads ONLY the
  container and rejects the other two outright. `nargo trace` writes a legacy
  `trace.json`, and the committed `codetracer-example-recordings/python/` are
  legacy `trace.bin`, so a helper built on `load_and_postprocess_trace`
  silently excludes them. `materialized_source::open_materialized_trace` is
  the shared opener for all three; use it rather than growing a fourth rule.

  Which layout a recorder writes is a per-recorder DEFAULT, not a property of
  the language: the Rust-backed `codetracer_python_recorder` writes a `*.ct`
  container unless `CODETRACER_TRACE_FORMAT` says `json`/`binary`
  (`codetracer-python-recorder/src/runtime/output_paths.rs`). So "Python
  recordings are legacy" is only true of the old committed fixtures — anything
  reading recordings has to handle all three.

- 2026-08-18: `codetracer_python_recorder` IS importable in this dev shell,
  but only from `python3.12`; the shell's default `python3` is 3.13 and has
  no such module. `ct record prog.py` therefore answers "Python module
  `codetracer_python_recorder` is not installed for interpreter …/python3" on
  a machine where recording Python works perfectly well — the db-backend test
  harness records with it routinely (`test_harness::find_suitable_python`
  probes `python3.12` FIRST for exactly this reason). Before concluding "the
  Python recorder is unavailable here", run
  `python3.12 -c "import codetracer_python_recorder"`.

- 2026-08-18 (RV-5 values): a Monaco DECORATION can carry DOM-ish annotations
  without a view zone or a content widget — `options.after` (injected text)
  renders `content` as a real inline span inside the line's own DOM, styled by
  `after.inlineClassName`. That is how a review draws its Omniscience value
  chips (`ui/unified_diff.monacoValueDecorations`, and `MonacoLineStyle`'s
  `afterContent`/`afterClass` for the full-file editor) with the debugger's own
  `ct-omni-name` / `flow-parallel-value-box` classes and no `FlowComponent`
  behind it. Three gotchas:
    * The range must be COLLAPSED at the end of the line's text. Injected text
      is placed at the range's end position, and a non-empty range would also
      apply the chip's class to the line's own characters. Set
      `showIfCollapsed: true` or the decoration vanishes on an empty line.
    * Set `inlineClassNameAffectsLetterSpacing: true`, or Monaco lays the span
      out as if it were plain monospace text and the chip's padding overlaps
      the code.
    * Monaco splits a LONG injected string across several rendering chunks, so
