  putting the Karax DOM shim into the SDK's `nim js` graph. Use `vmDebug` in
  the ViewModel layer; `cdebug` is fine everywhere in the renderer.
* `sdk/debugger_session.nim`'s `newDebuggerSession` builds the store but NOT
  the panel VMs: `session_vm.initializePanelViewModels` is called by
  `launch`/`attach`, because several panel VMs own effects that issue backend
  requests and there is nothing to ask about before a trace is open.
  `app_vm.createAppViewModel` grew an `initializePanels` parameter for this;
  it defaults to `true`, so existing call sites are unchanged.
* `headless_session.HeadlessDebugSession` is now a *host* around a
  `DebuggerSession` rather than an owner of its own graph. It still does its
  own DAP handshake, because it needs `DapStdioBackend.waitForEvent`
  (synchronous waits on a named event).
* Since 2026-09-23 the stdio adapter DOES deliver events to `onEvent`
  subscribers — until then it collected them and called none, so on every
  native host `FlowVM` never received `ct/updated-flow` and the flow overlay,
  the request-span tail and replay-status updates were dead. Delivery happens
  in `sendProc` AFTER the request's read loop returns (a handler may send;
  delivering mid-read would re-enter a half-read stream), in the
  `{"kind": <event>, "data": <body>}` envelope `RealBackendService` uses.
  Events a synchronous caller drains with `drainEvents` are still delivered:
  `undelivered` is a separate list from `eventQueue`. Events read by a direct
  `sendDapRequest` (e.g. `HeadlessDebugSession.stepIn`) are delivered on the
  next `BackendService.send`.

## The degraded-state catalogue is split across two packages (BlockTracer M2b)

`BlockTracer/Page-Descriptions.md` §14 lists thirteen degraded states and ends
with the rule that each is "a value of an enum on a ViewModel, not a branch in
a view". The table reads as one list; it is not.

* **Seven of the thirteen rows are chain-level** — pipeline behind the tip,
  object not found, trace awaiting generation, recorder unavailable for the
  VM, transaction below the history floor, reorganised away, CDN unreachable.
  They belong to BlockTracer's own ViewModels (`ChainVM`, `TraceStatusVM`,
  `GenerationJobVM`, ... — Front-End-Architecture.md §3). Modelling one of
  them in `src/frontend/viewmodel/` violates CodeTracer-Embed-SDK.md §3.2's
  last row and trips `ci/test/sdk-facade-boundary.sh`'s chain-token scan.
* **Six reach a pane** and live in `viewmodel/store/degraded_state.nim`:
  replay window expired, permanently unreplayable, browser cannot run the
  debugger, trace truncated, divergence detected, no verified source.
  That module's header carries the full row-by-row ownership table.

The split is 7-up/6-down for *modelling*, but it is NOT 7-up/6-down for
ownership, and reading it that way is the trap. Four of the six also have a
BlockTracer ViewModel named against them in Front-End-Architecture.md §3 —
`CapabilityVM`, `DivergenceVM`/`TraceStatusVM`, `SourceBundleVM`/`AddressVM`,
`ArtifactVM`. Those establish the condition from inputs that are chain- or
delivery-shaped (a capability probe, a manifest verdict, a provider chain, a
retention policy); the enums here are what a *pane* renders. The seam is
`ReplayDataStore`'s four setters plus the `CtReplayStatus` event: above
writes, panes read. Neither layer is redundant, and deleting `CapabilityVM`
because "the SDK already has `CapabilityRung`" would be the wrong conclusion.

Shape worth keeping if you extend it:

* ONE `PaneDegradation` enum plus a per-pane `set[PaneDegradation]`, not one
  enum per pane. §14 asks for "one canonical treatment rather than being
  reinvented per page"; five enums would be five places to re-decide what
  "truncated" means and to re-derive the precedence between two simultaneous
  conditions — the branch-in-a-view failure moved up a layer.
* The precedence is data (`DegradationPrecedence`), not control flow, so a
  test asserts the ordering directly.
* `DebugControlsVM.replayUsable` gates every `can*` memo. A step button that
  is enabled over a replay that cannot run IS the "retry that cannot succeed"
  §14 forbids, so the gate belongs in the memo and not in the view.
* Degraded state reaches the store over a `CtReplayStatus` backend event as
  well as through host-side setters, so `MockBackendService.emitEvent` can
  drive every §14 row headlessly. Unrecognised wire spellings deliberately
  leave the signal alone rather than falling back to the healthy default.

## `ci/test/sdk-facade-boundary.sh` needs bash >= 4 — it now handles this itself

It uses `mapfile`, which macOS's /bin/bash 3.2 does not have. The script now
re-execs into the first bash >= 4 on PATH, so no caller has to arrange it, and
if there is genuinely none it prints `NOT RUN   bash-version: ...` and exits
**2** — reserved for "could not run", as against 1 for "found something".

The old advice here was "run through `direnv exec <repo> bash -c '...'`, which
supplies bash 5", and it was **half wrong in a way that cost real time**. Being
inside the dev shell is not sufficient: `bash -lc` re-sources the profile and
puts /bin ahead of the nix store, so on this machine `direnv exec … bash -lc`
got **3.2** and `direnv exec … bash -c` got **5.3**, same shell, same moment.
The login flag was the variable, not the dev shell.

What it cost: `test_sdk_facade_boundary.nim` shells out and reads the exit
status, so under 3.2 it reported FOUR content failures — including
`VIOLATION consumer-facade-only`, its own negative control — for a checker
that had not run. That was both of `ci/lint/nim.sh`'s FAILEDs and the only red
in `just test-vm-unit` on a clean `cloud`, i.e. a gate that was red everywhere
locally and therefore read by nobody.

## The whole `lint-*` stage has been dying before it runs anything in CI

Measured on runs 33712110539, 33707534189, 33706050468 and 33689490588
(2026-09-02/03): `lint-nim`, `lint-bash`, `lint-rust` and `lint-nix` all fail
inside `nix develop`, before a single lint executes:

    error: Failed to fetch git repository https://github.com/anza-xyz/crossbeam
    fatal: could not read Username for 'https://github.com': No such device or address

`lint-nix` hits `https://github.com/mystenlabs/tidehunter.git` first; same
cause, different input reached first. The dev shell cannot be evaluated
without credentials for these flake git inputs, and the job's token is
installed as an owner-scoped `http.<url>.extraHeader` that authenticates only
`metacraft-labs`.

So: **the SDK facade gate has no CI verdict at all** — it is not "green in CI
and red locally", it has not run in either place. Do not cite `ci/lint/nim.sh`
as coverage until this is fixed. (The isonim submodule `##[error]` earlier in
the same job is a red herring: it is downgraded to a warning and the step
continues.)

## The ViewModels and the wasm32 replay engine speak different DAP dialects

`src/frontend/viewmodel/backend/dap_dialect.md` is the measured, reproduced
record — eight disagreements, four of which trap the WASM worker. Read it
before wiring any pane to `WorkerBackendService`. The three that bite first:

* **Handshake order.** `sdk/debugger_session.nim:377-383` sends `initialize ->
  configurationDone -> launch`; the browser engine needs `launch` before
  `configurationDone`, because `handle_message_browser`'s `configurationDone`
  arm (`dap_server.rs:2743-2775`) is the only place the `Handler` is ever
  constructed and it reads a folder only `launch` populates. Get it wrong and
  all three requests answer `success: true`, then every later request is
  dropped with a bare `warn!` (`dap_server.rs:2809`) — no response, so the
  future hangs forever. The NATIVE path tolerates both orders; the browser
  path's copies of both tolerances are inert because they are `&&`-chained
  onto `ctx.to_stable_sender`, which is `None` in the browser.
* **`SystemTime::now()` traps on wasm32.** No cfg guard, no shim; `vfs.rs`
  works around it for the VFS only. `ct/load-locals` (so the State pane cannot
  work at all), `ct/originChain`, any step reaching a trace boundary, and
  `disconnect` all die. `probe_engine_defects.mjs` reproduces all four.
* **`drainPlatformCallbacks()` cannot see a real transport.** On JS,
  `onComplete` uses the drainable queue only for `__syncResolved` futures —
  i.e. `newCompletedFuture`, i.e. `mock_backend`. Anything built with
  `newPromise` and resolved later (`worker_backend`, `real_backend`) is
  observed by a `.then` microtask a synchronous drain cannot pump. This site
  has now been wrong twice for two different reasons; commit `caaea86c` fixed
  the synchronous half only.

## Two dispatch tables, and neither is in `dap_handler.rs`

`dap_handler.rs` has no `match` on a command string at all — it holds the
handler methods. The tables are `dap_server.rs:2043` (`handle_request`, the
full command set) and `dap_server.rs:2676` (`handle_message_browser`, which
handles only initialize/launch/configurationDone/disconnect and delegates the
rest to `handle_request`). Grepping `dap_handler.rs` for command-looking
strings matches filesystem paths (`meta_dat/sources`, `origin-patterns`), a
replay-task name (`"flow"`) and a default literal (`"source"`) — none of them
commands.

## The browser's Build/Run path, and four things that were quietly in its way

- 2026-09-01: `ui/web_noir_build.nim` is the call path from a gesture to the
  Noir wasm toolchain. Everything either side of it existed and was tested
  (registry, worker protocol, transport, the merged NS9 bundle); nothing
  called it. Marshalling is `platform/noir_build.nim`, pane-driving is
  `viewmodels/noir_build_producer.nim`, and both are pure so `vm-unit` and
  `vm-unit-js` run all of it. Four obstacles were found by measurement and
  each is worth knowing before touching this area:
  * **`ui/shortcuts.nim`'s hard-coded `ctrl+b` shadows a CONFIGURED shortcut.**
    `default_config.yaml` binds `build: "CTRL+B"`, the YAML loop binds it, and
    then a later `Mousetrap.bind` in the same proc replaces it — Mousetrap
    overwrites rather than chains. The YAML entry has been dead on every
    platform. It is now excluded from `-d:ctWeb`, and the web arm installs
    `data.actions[ClientAction.build]` instead, which `bindShortcut` reads AT
    PRESS TIME. A `Mousetrap.bind` from `ui_js.nim`'s web arm does NOT work:
    `onNoTrace` is `{.async.}` and calls `configureShortcuts()` again in a
    later microtask, so it rebinds over anything installed there — measured,
    with `Mousetrap.trigger('ctrl+b')` from the page also reaching nothing.
  * **The generated entry document had the auto-hide STRIPS and not the
    OVERLAY.** BUILD, PROBLEMS, FIND IN FILES and REQUESTS are standalone
    auto-hide panes (`ui/layout.nim`): their DOM lives in
    `#auto-hide-standalone-host` at `left:-9999px` and revealing one means
    reparenting it into `#auto-hide-overlay-content`. `web_deployment.nim`'s
    skeleton omitted `#auto-hide-overlay`, so `doShowOverlayImpl` logged
    "not found in DOM" and returned — none of the four could ever be opened in
    a browser. The skeleton now carries the overlay, the backdrop and the three
    docked hosts, copied structurally from `src/frontend/index.html`.
  * **`build.syncLegacyBuildIntoVM` wiped the pane on every reveal.**
    `ui/layout.nim`'s `onPanelShown` calls it whenever the BUILD overlay is
    shown, and it is a BULK REPLACE from `self.build` — the Electron
    producer's mirror, permanently empty in a browser. Measured: a compile
    reporting `rows=1 paneRows=1` (one view model, holding the row) and
    `#build` holding none 50 ms later, the row appearing only on the next
    gesture. It now returns early when the legacy record is empty and the view
    model is not.
  * **`wasm_worker.finish` dropped a `failed` message's TEXT on the `start`
    path.** `run` gets it as a `PlatformError.message`; `start` has no
    `settle`, so the three module-load faults
    (`not-delivered`/`not-served`/`broken`) reached a streaming caller as a
    bare exit code 1. It is now delivered on stderr before the exit.
- 2026-09-01: `mode: "debug"` SILENCES WARNINGS. `compiler/wasm/src/vfs.rs`'s
  `debugging_compile_options()` sets `silence_warnings: true` (nargo's own
  choice — the instrumenter imports `__debug` functions the user did not
  write). Measured over one program carrying both an unused expression and an
  unresolved path: `program` mode reports 2 diagnostics, `debug` reports 1. So
  Build asks for `program` and Run asks for `debug`, and they are not
  interchangeable in either direction — an uninstrumented artifact traces to
  ONE event and ZERO steps while both wasm modules report `ok`.
- 2026-09-01: `VfsResponse`'s `warnings` and `diagnostics` are ABSENT, not
  empty, when there are none (`skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty"`), and a
  RESOLVE refusal (`git-dependency-refused`, `missing-manifest`) carries ZERO
  diagnostics and one positioned message in `manifest`/`line`/`column`. A pane
  that rendered only `diagnostics` paints nothing for the two most likely
  first-run mistakes.
- 2026-09-01: `views/isonim_build_view.nim` assigns `BuildOutputLine.htmlText`
  to `innerHTML` — deliberately, because the desktop's producer passes ANSI
  runs through `ansi_up`. Anything else writing that field must escape: Noir
  diagnostics quote the user's own source, and §1b.3 links can carry a project.
- 2026-09-01: the BUILD pane's ■ has a NON-REACTIVE `disabled` class.
  `views/isonim_build_view.nim`'s header computes it once with
  `stopButtonClass(vm)`; measured over a real compile in a browser it stayed
  `build-ctrl-btn build-stop-btn disabled` for the whole run while the header
  text beside it updated from `▶` to `build succeeded`. The button still WORKS
  — `cancelBuild` is wired to the click, not to the class — but "wait until the
  ■ is live" is a condition that never becomes true, on either platform, so no
  harness can synchronise on it. Not fixed here (it belongs to the pane's
  view); `ci/test/noir-build-in-browser.sh` records why it has no Stop arm.
- 2026-09-01: `immutable` was being served on STABLE filenames, and a CDN
  believed it. Every file the deploy publishes under `/assets/`
  (`wasm-worker.js`, `noir_wasm.wasm`, `noir_tracer_wasm.wasm`) carried
  `public, max-age=31536000, immutable` from `staticAssetHeader`, but none of
  the three has a digest in its name. After deploy `2fb3afa6` both custom
  domains served `assets/wasm-worker.js` at 14150 bytes — the PREVIOUS build —
  with `cf-cache-status: HIT` and `age: 131292` (36.5 h), while
  `web-codetracer.pages.dev` served the correct 35525; still stale an hour
  later, and it would have stayed so for a year. The two `.wasm` modules
  carried the identical header and escaped only because they answered
  `cf-cache-status: DYNAMIC` — nothing in our code made them safe.
  How it survived review: the class's own doc comment claimed `/assets/` was
  "content-addressed", and `test_platform_web.nim` selected it with
  `/assets/app.9f2b1c.js`, a hashed filename NO assembly step has ever
  produced. The class was validated on a fictional address while the three real
  ones went unchecked. Fixed by deriving the class from the published names
  (`assetIsContentAddressed` / `staticAssetGlobClass`), so the glob revalidates
  today and becomes `immutable` on its own if digests ever land.
  A header fix does NOT cure an already-poisoned edge object: the entry is held
  under the OLD max-age and never revalidates, so clearing it needs a one-time
  cache purge.
- 2026-09-01: the deploy's "Verify the DEPLOYED artifact" step checked SEVEN
  hard-coded paths against a tree of 535 published files — 1.3% coverage. The
  wasm-worker staleness above was caught only because that file happened to be
  one of the seven. Among the 528 unchecked was
  `public/dist/frontend_bundle.js`, whose absence is the documented cause of a
  deployed page that painted nothing. Now every published file is swept on both
  hosts, and the number of verdicts is asserted against the number of files —
  because the first draft printed only disagreements, which made silence mean
  success, and silence is also what a worker that never ran produces (an awk
  `ORS="\0"` is the EMPTY separator, since NUL terminates the C string; every
  worker failed and the sweep reported zero disagreements over four files).
- 2026-09-01: deploy run 33547074249 annotated that there is NO workspace lock
  for codetracer (neither `locks/<project>/codetracer/<sha>.xml` nor
  `.toml` in metacraft-manifests for `2fb3afa6` or `df60154d`), and that three
  siblings — `codetracer-native-recorder`, `codetracer-trace-format`,
  `codetracer-trace-format-nim` — are pinned by the explicit entry `<name>=dev`
  rather than by a 40-hex SHA. The same commit can therefore build different
  artifacts depending on WHEN it is built, which undermines the deploy guard's
  byte comparison (it compares served bytes to just-built bytes, not to the
  commit) and any claim that a deployed artifact corresponds to a revision. Not
  fixed here; the lasting fix named by the annotation is to declare those
  siblings in codetracer's project manifest and publish the lock.
- 2026-09-01: what the all-files sweep found on its first real run, and the
  distinction worth keeping. Cloudflare Pages does NOT upload a path named
  `.git`, so `libs/codetracer-design-system/.git` — a 57-byte submodule
  gitlink that `cp -rL` pulls into the publish tree — answered 9174 bytes on
  both hosts: the SPA fallback, i.e. the request fell through the 200-rewrite
  to the entry document. Nothing is disclosed. But it is NOT a blanket dotfile
  rule: `libs/codetracer-design-system/scripts/.gitignore` IS uploaded and IS
  served, with its real 19 bytes. Pages excludes `.git` and `node_modules` by
  name, not dotfiles as a class, so the manifest mirrors exactly that. An
  over-broad exclusion is how a real asset quietly stops being checked.
  Separately: the gitlink has no business in the publish tree at all; the
  staging step's `cp -rL` should not be carrying it. Not fixed here.
- 2026-09-01: the deploy's verification checked TWO hosts while the deployment
  declares THREE, and the missing one was independently broken. `origin` plus
  `languageOrigins` in the descriptor name `ide.codetracer.com`,
  `noirstudio.dev` and `www.noirstudio.dev`; the step named the first and the
  Pages alias. All three custom hostnames held separately-cached stale copies
  of `assets/wasm-worker.js` with DIFFERENT ages (139374s, 48357s, 48081s), so
  a purge of one zone would have turned the gate green while two hostnames
  stayed stale. The host list is now derived from the descriptor and the count
  of hosts swept is asserted against the count declared — a host that drops out
  of the list fails rather than passes. Consequence for the purge: two zones
  (codetracer.com, noirstudio.dev) but THREE URLs if purging by URL rather than
  by zone.
- 2026-09-01: a counted assertion does not save you from an empty set. The
  all-files sweep asserts verdicts == manifest entries, and that agrees
  perfectly at zero: testing the step on macOS, `find -printf` is an unknown
  primary, the manifest came out EMPTY, and the step printed
  "ok: 0/0 published files match what was uploaded" for every host and exited
  0. Nothing was wrong and nothing was checked. Two fixes, both needed: the
  manifest is now built with `-exec sh -c ... +` (portable, so the same block
  runs on the workstation), and the floor is asserted by NAME — `index.html`
  and `ui.js` must appear in the manifest, because a manifest built from the
  wrong directory has neither and a bare `> 0` would not notice.

## Watch expressions: seven places, and the wire was always carrying them

- 2026-09-04: `CtLoadLocalsArguments.watchExpressions` has existed on both
  sides of the wire for as long as the State pane has, and the frontend has
  always populated it. There was never a missing feature to design — there
  were seven disconnected pieces, each of which reads as success from the
  others' point of view. Anyone touching this area should know all seven:
  * **`db.rs` dropped the field with a `warn!`** ("watch expressions not
    supported for db traces currently"). The request carried the expression
    and the response carried no row for it AND no reason, so the pane could
    not tell "not evaluated" from "no such variable".
  * **`state_vm.nim`'s `currentVariables` memo returned a literal empty seq
    for `stWatches`**, with a comment promising it would be filled in "when
    the backend supports them". The tab could show nothing whatever the
    backend answered.
  * **The WebRenderer panel in `isonim_state_view.nim` had no tab strip.**
    The Locals/Globals/Watches buttons existed only in the *MockRenderer*
    panel in the same file, which is what the headless view tests render —
    so `stWatches` was reachable from `vm.selectTab` and from no gesture in
    any shipping product. A test that switched tabs through the ViewModel
    passed against a product no user could navigate.
  * **The typed expression and the answered request were on different
    objects.** The live input form (`wireWatchInputForm`) calls
    `StateVM.addWatch`; the reply the product paints comes from
    `StateComponent.loadLocals` -> `CtLoadLocalsResponse` ->
    `registerLocals`, and `loadLocals` read `StateComponent.watchExpressions`
    — a field written only by `submitWatchExpression`, which has NO CALL
    SITES anywhere in the repo. `ui/state.nim` now sources the list from the
    ViewModel and installs `onWatchesChangedProc` to re-ask.
  * **`replay_data_store.requestLocals` keyed its dedup on the COUNT of
    watch expressions**, so swapping one watch for another at the same step
    was suppressed and the pane kept answering a deleted expression.
  * **`headless_session.requestAndLoadLocals` hardcoded
    `"watchExpressions": []`** while the same file exposes `addWatch` /
    `removeWatch` — the identical crossed wiring on the SDK surface.
  * **The page objects located `#watch`; the view renders `#watch-0`.**
    Both `layout_page.ts` and `variable-state-pane.ts` had a locator that
    has never matched an element. Nothing caught it because no spec ever
    typed into the box.
- 2026-09-04: **do NOT route watch evaluation through
  `tracepoint_interpreter`**, which is what `db.rs`'s TODO pointed at. It is
  gated behind the `syntax-highlight` feature (it compiles expressions with
  tree-sitter) and `src/db-backend/build_wasm.sh` builds the browser engine
  with `--no-default-features --features browser-transport`. In that build
  `TracepointInterpreter::evaluate` is the stub in `tracepoint_interpreter/
  mod.rs` that returns `vec![]`. Using it would ship a feature that works on
  the desktop and silently answers nothing in a browser — the same defect
  one layer down. `watch_expression.rs` therefore depends on nothing but the
  trace's own value records and answers identically in both builds.
- 2026-09-04: a REFUSED watch travels as `ValueRecord::Error { msg }`, not
  as a new wire field. The Nim `Value` already has an `Error` kind whose
  text representation is its `msg`
  (`common_types/utils/text_representation.nim`), so a refusal renders as a
  visible row on every existing frontend without any of them learning a new
  case. `Value.isWatch` — declared in `common_types/language_features/
  value.nim` and styled `.value-watch` since before any backend wrote it —
  is what separates a watch row from a local; the Rust `Value` gained the
  matching `is_watch` field.
- 2026-09-04: watch rows are appended to `locals` AFTER the sort and dedup
  in `Db::load_locals`, deliberately. Sorting them in would scatter them
  through the list in name order, and the dedup (`a.expression ==
  b.expression`) would DELETE a watch whose expression equals a local's
  name — which is the most ordinary watch anyone types.
- 2026-09-05 (CTUI-0, the CodeTracer TUI at `src/frontend/tui/`): **anything
  that imports `isonim_tui` hands the linker a grammar archive path that comes
  from isonim-tui's own source — say which one you mean with
  `-d:isonimTuiGrammarArchive=<abs path>`.**
  `isonim-tui/src/isonim_tui/syntax/treesitter_ffi.nim` emits that path as a
  `{.passl.}`, and it DEFAULTS to
  `<isonim-tui>/build/grammars/libisonim_tui_grammars.a`, computed from
  `currentSourcePath` at compile time. Remove that file, omit the define, and
  the link dies with `ld: cannot find <path>`; nothing else in the error names
  isonim-tui, tree-sitter or the TUI. Building codetracer's own archive from
  `libs/tree-sitter-*` (which `scripts/build-tui-grammars.sh` does, from all
  ten vendored grammars) does not by itself change which path is used — the
  define does. `just build-tui` and both `tui*` lanes pass it; verified by
  moving the sibling archive aside and linking with and without it (0 vs 1).
  A symlink at the baked path is still planted as a fallback, because Nim
  SILENTLY IGNORES a `-d:` naming a constant the sources do not declare, so an
  older isonim-tui would otherwise fail at link with no diagnosis. Beware what
  that symlink costs: isonim-tui's `just grammars` tests the path with
  `[ -f ]`, which follows the link, and will replace it with a TWO-grammar
  archive — `test_tui_build_prerequisites.nim` counts the members at that path
  for exactly this reason.
- 2026-09-05: the tree-sitter RUNTIME (`-ltree-sitter`, appended by that same
  `{.passl.}`) is on neither the linker search path nor `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` in
  this repo's shells. `scripts/build-tui-grammars.sh` resolves it four ways and
  records the answer in `build/grammars/tui-link-flags.txt`; the route that
  works outside a dev shell is the `tree-sitter` CLI's own nix store prefix,
  which ships `bin/`, `lib/` and `include/` in one output. Do not hard-code a
  store path — it is correct on one machine until the next GC.
- 2026-09-05: `tree-sitter generate` (bare) loads `grammar.js` through node and
  fails on a host without one; `tree-sitter generate src/grammar.json` uses the
  already-compiled grammar and needs only the CLI. `libs/tree-sitter-nim` is
  the one vendored grammar whose `src/parser.c` is gitignored and generated;
  the other nine commit theirs.
- 2026-09-05: **`tree-sitter generate` run inside a grammar checkout overwrites
  `src/tree_sitter/parser.h`, which that checkout TRACKS.** Measured, twice, in
  `libs/tree-sitter-nim`: the CLI rewrites `src/parser.c` AND
  `src/tree_sitter/parser.h` on *every* run (both mtimes advance on a second
  consecutive run); `src/grammar.json` is NOT rewritten (its mtime is unchanged
  across runs under 0.25.3 and 0.25.10 alike — an earlier note here said it was,
  and that was wrong). The header it writes is whichever one the CLI on PATH
  carries, so whether the run leaves DIRT depends on the version:
  tree-sitter **0.25.3** (this host's `~/.nix-profile`) adds
  `typedef struct TSLanguageMetadata TSLanguageMetadata;` and leaves a permanent
  ` M libs/tree-sitter-nim`; **0.25.10** (the dev shell's) writes a header
  byte-identical to the pinned one and leaves the checkout clean. So the
  hazard is real but VERSION-DEPENDENT, and it is the plain-PATH tree-sitter —
  the one `just tui-prereqs` gets outside `direnv` — that trips it. Dirt there
  is not cosmetic: the workspace pre-push gate refuses uncommitted changes in a
  repo's develop-set closure, so it blocks every push from that checkout.
  Generate with
  `-o <dir>` instead: it writes `parser.c`, `node-types.json` and a matching
  `tree_sitter/{parser,array,alloc}.h` into `<dir>` and does not touch the
  submodule at all. `scripts/build-tui-grammars.sh` puts that under
  `build/grammars/generated/<grammar>/` and compiles with that directory FIRST
  on the include path, so parser.c and its runtime header come from one run.
  Restoring the header afterwards would be worse: a parser.c generated against
  a header you then reverted is an ABI mismatch found late or never.
- 2026-09-05: **a Nim import has far more spellings, and far more PLACES to
  sit, than the one this repo writes; both import extractors now normalise the
  spec and split the line into statements before grading.** The two are
  `nim_imports` in `ci/test/sdk-facade-boundary.sh` (which every "must not import
  X" check in the repo is built on) and `importSpecs` in
  `src/frontend/tui/tests/test_tui_facade_boundary.nim`, deliberate mirrors of
  each other — compare their output byte for byte over all 998 tracked Nim files
  before believing a change to either. Five facts, all confirmed by compiling
  against nim 2.2.8 AND then using a symbol from the imported module, not
  remembered:
  1. THE SPEC MAY BE A STRING LITERAL. `import "a/b"` is joined onto each
     `--path` root exactly as `import a/b` is; so are `import "a/b" as c`,
     `from "a/b" import c`, `from "a/b" as c import d`, `include "a/b"`,
     `import a, "b/c"`, `import "a/b"/[c, d]`, `import "a"/b/c`,
     `import a/"b"/c`, `import r"a/b"` and `import """a/b"""`.
     `import "a/b/[c]"` is the one that does NOT compile — inside quotes the
     brackets are part of a filename — so neither extractor expands it.
  2. A STRING LITERAL SELF-TERMINATES, so the space before the next keyword is
     OPTIONAL: `import "a/b"as c`, `import "a/b"except d` and
     `from "a/b"as c import d` all compile. Split on a SPACED keyword and the
     extractor yields `a/bas c`, which is nothing.
  3. `/` IS AN INFIX OPERATOR: `import a / b / c` and `import "a" / "b" / "c"`
     compile and mean `a/b/c`. **26** files in this repo already write imports
     that way (`src/frontend/ui/ui_imports.nim`, `src/common/paths.nim`,
     `src/frontend/types.nim`, …), and at HEAD their specs resolved to nothing.
     The number is measured — diff the extractor's output over all 998 tracked
     Nim files before and after — not counted by eye; an earlier note here said
     "four", and the spec repo said "about twenty", from the same change.
     `import a /b/ c` does NOT compile — nim wants the spacing symmetric — so
     only the symmetric form is handled.
  4. `;` SEPARATES STATEMENTS, so `import a; import b` is two imports, and so
     is `echo 1; import a`. This is the one that hid an import in TOTAL
     SILENCE rather than yielding a wrong spec: read as one statement the line
     normalises to `a;importb`, one module nobody has, so both imports vanish —
     including the permitted one, leaving a file that appears to import nothing.
     Split on a `;` outside a string literal AND outside brackets, because
     `when (let x = 1; x > 0): import a` compiles.
  5. `when` / `elif` / `else` MAY CARRY THEIR STATEMENT ON THEIR OWN LINE.
     `when not defined(js): import a` is idiomatic, compiles, and is ONE line —
     so the indented-continuation handling, which covers the MULTI-LINE
     `when defined(js):` spelling of exactly the same thing, never sees it. That
     is what made this look covered: both extractors' comments cited the
     multi-line form, so a reader checking "are conditional imports handled?"
     found yes. `elif`, `else`, `when(c):` and `else:import a` compile too.
     Choose the colon that ends the condition by WHAT FOLLOWS IT, not by
     counting brackets: `when F(a: 1).a == 1:` and `when {1: 2}.len > 0:` both
     compile.
  Quotes also change what a character MEANS: `#`, `,`, `[`, `]` and whitespace
  inside a literal are filename characters, and nim requires only the BASENAME
  of a module path to be a valid identifier, so `import "h#d/../<forbidden>"`
  compiles. Comment stripping, keyword splitting, bracket counting and the
  statement split are therefore all quote-aware in both extractors. **If you
  write a third extractor, do all of this**; a rule that learns about quotation
  marks separately is how a guard grows one hole per rule.
  Nim REJECTS these, so nothing has to handle them (checked, because "it might
  compile" is how the list above kept growing): `if` / `block` / `static` /
  `for` / `case` / proc bodies carrying an import ("only allowed at top level"),
  `when a: when b: import c` ("nestable statement requires indentation"),
  `when a: (import b)`, and `import"a/b"` — the space AFTER the keyword is
  mandatory even though the keyword after a quoted spec may be tight.
  THREE SHAPES ARE REFUSED LOUDLY rather than guessed at — awk logs them and the
  script reports `VIOLATION import-specs-analysable`, the Nim mirror raises
  `UnanalysableSpec`:
    * a spec containing a BACKSLASH: `import "a\x2Fb\x2Fc"` imports `a/b/c`, and
      decoding Nim's escape rules (which differ between the plain, raw and
      triple-quoted literal forms) in two languages is a place to be silently
      wrong;
    * an import running into, or resuming after, a BLOCK COMMENT that does not
      close on the same line. One that opens and closes on a line is removed,
      nesting and all (`import #[c]# a/b` compiles); cross-line comment state is
      NOT tracked, because a block-comment opener inside a multi-line string
      literal would then swallow every import after it;
    * a one-line `when`/`elif`/`else` that visibly carries an import the lexer
      could not read out of it. The cause is a CHARACTER LITERAL in the
      condition — `when '#' == '#':`, `when ';' == ';':`, `when '"' == 'x':` all
      compile and land the comment cut, the statement split or the quote parity
      inside the literal. Teaching the scans about character literals means
      teaching them that the suffix in `1'u8` is not one, so they assert on
      themselves instead.
  ONE PLACE IS STILL A SILENT MISS, and it is bounded BY THE PLACE rather than
  by the trick — bounding it by the trick ("a character literal holding a `#` or
  a `\"`") was tried and is measurably too narrow. THE PLACE is a NON-conditional
  statement sharing its line with an import through a `;`; anything in that
  leading statement which unbalances the comment cut, the quote parity or the
  bracket depth carries the import past all three. All of these compile, import
  usably, and are read by neither extractor: `echo '#'; import a/b`,
  `echo '"'; import a/b`, `echo '('; import a/b` (and `'['`, `'{'` — the bracket
  depth rises so the `;` is never a separator), `echo "a\"b"; import a/b` (an
  ESCAPED quote in an ordinary string, not a character literal at all), and the
  closing triple-quote of a multi-line string before the same `;`. A character
  literal holding a `;` or a CLOSING bracket IS read correctly. Extending the
  self-check to every line was measured and rejected: six real prose comments in
  this repo carry a `;` before "from" and eleven more carry a `:` before
  "import"/"from", and all seventeen would go red for nothing — before counting
  the dozens of lines in `test_tui_facade_boundary.nim` that document the forms
  themselves. Closing the family properly needs a self-check keyed on `;` plus
  `import`/`include` on ANY line, which none of the six prose lines would trip;
  that is a design change, not a wording one. Emitting raw
  text, or guessing, would be a MISS, which is the one outcome a boundary lint
  may not produce.
  History, so the shape of the bug is on record: at `f274fa68` the extractors
  left the quotes on, so `import "tui/host/native_host"` planted in the real
  `src/frontend/tui/app/tui_app.nim` compiled and left both guards green while
  six unquoted spellings went red. The first fix stripped `r` before any `"`,
  which ate the last letter of every quoted spec ending in `r` — measured over
  998 tracked files it turned `"../../../../isonim-docs/src/ssr"` into
  `.../src/ss`, and `src/frontend/viewmodel` alone holds 21 `r`-final modules
  (`request_tracker`, `front_end_adapter`, `reducer`, …). The `r` of a raw
  string literal belongs to the quote that OPENS one; track quote parity.
  The pass after that shipped a caveat saying "what remains is ONE form, a
  backslash, and it fails loudly". That was false too: at the same commit,
  `when true: import <internal>` planted alone in the real `tui_app.nim` left
  the guard at `6 check(s), 0 failing` while the extractor yielded NOTHING for
  the line, and `import <a>; import <b>` yielded the single spec
  `<a>;import<b>`, losing both. **Both were pre-existing at `f274fa68`.** If you
  touch either extractor, do not write a completeness claim you have not put in
  front of the compiler; a narrower true statement is worth more.
- 2026-09-05: the repo-wide facade guard (`ci/test/sdk-facade-boundary.sh`) does
  NOT police `src/frontend/tui/app/` -> `src/frontend/tui/host/` imports, in any
  spelling: its consumer check only reports specs resolving under
  `src/frontend/viewmodel`, and `host/` is not there. Its only TUI rule is
  `tui-layers`, about which directories carry a `.sdk-consumer` marker. The
  app/-must-not-reach-host/ rule belongs entirely to
  `src/frontend/tui/tests/test_tui_facade_boundary.nim`. So a host import
  planted in `app/` reddens ONE guard, not two — expect that when checking a
  change to either.
- 2026-09-05: `ci/lib/run-nim-test-lane.sh` reads an assertion count from
  `const ExpectedAssertions = <n>` **on a line of its own**. Written inside a
  `const` block it is invisible to the lane and the file is reported as having
  declared none. Emitting `CHECKS: <n>` at runtime is better still: the runner
  prefers it, and it is a count a run verified rather than one read off source.
- 2026-09-05 (CTUI-1): **DAP `threads` in `replay-server` enumerates PROCESSES,
  not threads.** `src/db-backend/src/dap_handler.rs::threads` answers from
  `ReplaySession::list_processes` and surfaces one DAP `Thread` per process.
  `list_processes` has two implementations: the trait DEFAULT at
  `src/db-backend/src/replay.rs:238` returns ONE synthetic entry
  unconditionally — the arm every CTFS/db-backed trace takes, i.e. every trace
  the Python, Noir, Ruby and JavaScript recorders produce — and
  `src/db-backend/src/recreator_session.rs:1068` forwards `GetProcessInfo` to
  the rr worker, whose answer is parsed out of `rr ps`
  (`codetracer-native-backend/src/multiprocess/process_tree.rs:180`), a
  PID/PPID/EXIT/CMD table. Measured: a Python program with four workers past a
  `threading.Barrier` records fine, its calltrace carries four sibling `worker`
  frames, and DAP `threads` still answers `[{"id":1,"name":"<thread 1>"}]`. So
  "how many threads does this trace have" is not answerable through the front-end
  surface today, whatever the recorder did. Do not write a thread test against a
  trace and assume >1; it will pass vacuously at 1 or be written to expect 1.
- 2026-09-05 (CTUI-1): recorded trace fixtures for the TUI live under
  `test-logs/tui-fixtures/<name>-<md5-of-the-program>/`, produced on demand by
  `src/frontend/tui/tests/fixtures/fixture_provider.nim` from `test-programs/`.
  `/test-logs/` is now gitignored WHOLESALE — before this only `*.log` was, so a
  recorded fixture directory showed up as untracked. Superseded cache entries
  are not pruned: editing a fixture program leaves the old `<name>-<md5>/`
  beside the new one.
- 2026-09-05 (host, not the tree — three ways this workspace stops `ct` from
  building, each diagnosed far from its cause):
  * a stray `~/.tup` makes `tup` treat `$HOME` as the project root, and
    `just build-once` then dies parsing an unrelated repo's Tupfile. This repo's
    tup root is `src/` (that is what `src/Tupfile.ini` marks); `tup init --force`
    run IN `src/` fixes it, and `.tup/` is gitignored.
  * `src/db-backend/build.rs` regenerates the MCR emulator's C by spawning
    `direnv exec <codetracer-native-recorder> bash ct_emulator/build_native_api.sh`.
    Under cargo's environment that nested shell failed with
    `cannot open file: results`; run the same script by hand once
    (`direnv exec ../codetracer-native-recorder bash ct_emulator/build_native_api.sh`)
    and the cargo build then succeeds. `CODETRACER_DB_BACKEND_SKIP_DIRENV=1` is
    the documented alternative.
  * `ct` will not compile when the `io-mon` sibling predates
    `src/io_mon/depfile.nim` (`src/ct_test/incremental/io_mon_capture.nim:83`
    imports it). `scripts/require-siblings.sh` reports io-mon as an OPTIONAL
    missing sibling and says the build "continues" — it does, and then fails at
    the `ct` link step several minutes later. `config.nims` reads `$IO_MON_SRC`,
    but it adds that path BEFORE the `../io-mon/src` sibling and Nim resolves
    later `--path` entries first, so the env var cannot override a colliding
    module in the sibling checkout.
- 2026-09-05: **a `HeadlessDebugSession` that steps in a loop and never calls
  `drainEvents()` grows without bound.** Every `continue`/step makes
  replay-server push events the session buffers until somebody asks for them,
  and the buffer is never trimmed on its own. Measured over the `wide_state`
  fixture's 67-stop recursion (locals include a 600-member dict): RSS climbs
  ~39 MB per stop, 126 MB -> 2.85 GB, linear, with replay-server itself flat at
  ~155 MB — so the memory is on the Nim side, not the backend's. Adding one
  `discard session.drainEvents()` inside the loop holds the same run at
  ~164 MB. `continueForward` synchronises on its own events, so draining after
  it returns discards nothing a later call needs.
- 2026-09-05 (CTUI-4, the source-access seam). Seven things that were surprising
  enough to cost real time, each measured rather than reasoned about:
  * **`replay-server` did not implement the DAP `source` request.**
    `dap_server.rs`'s `handle_request` had no `source` arm, so it fell through
    to `dap_command_to_step_action` and answered
    `command source not supported here` — while `dap_types.rs` already carried
    `SourceArguments` and `SourceResponseBody`, referenced from nowhere else.
    `Handler::source` now serves it (bundled srcviews, then `<trace>/files/`,
    then the recorded path) and returns `sourceGeneration` in the body, which
    is what lets a client refuse a revision it did not ask for.
  * **A DAP `source` response also names WHERE its bytes came from, and you
    must not treat `success: true` as "this is the recording's copy".**
    `SourceResponseBody.sourceOrigin` (`dap_types::SourceOriginKind`) is
    `payload`, `working-tree`, or — on the refusal — `unavailable`, and
    `SourceArguments.allowWorkingTree` (absent means `true`) turns the
    last-resort read of the replay host off. This exists because the first
    version of the arm resolved the bundle with `bundled_source_path` alone,
    missed the project-relative layout below for every Noir recording, and
    answered from the replay host's working tree while reporting an ordinary
    success. Both the payload lookups now go through
    `expr_loader::resolve_bundled_source` (exact mapping, then a longest-first
    suffix walk), and both are labelled. If you add another resolution step,
    label it too — an unlabelled step is the same bug again.
  * **`EditorVM.cursorLine` is the editor CARET and nothing writes it from the
    debugger position.** Its only writer is `EditorVM.setCursor`. A pane that
    scrolls by `cursorLine` alone sits on line 1 for a whole session while the
    execution pointer walks off the bottom. Use
    `SourceVM.followExecutionPointer` / `SourceVM.executionLine`
    (`store.debugger.val.location.line`) for stop-driven scrolling.
  * **The trace folder's `files/` payload has two layouts.** A `ct`-imported
    CTFS container (Python recorder) writes
    `files/<absolute path, root stripped>` — `ctfs_sources.safePayloadPath`'s
    answer. The Noir recorder writes PROJECT-RELATIVE paths (`files/src/main.nr`)
    while the engine reports the same file at an absolute path. A reader has to
    try the writer's mapping and then walk the recorded path's suffixes. There
    are TWO readers of this layout — `sdk/source_provider.nim`'s
    `resolvePayload` and the engine's `expr_loader::resolve_bundled_source` —
    and applying the finding to only one of them was CTUI-4's blocking defect.
    The container's own bundled srcviews (extracted to
    `$TMPDIR/codetracer-bundled-sources/<hash>/`) use the SAME two layouts and
    are resolved by the same walk; on `noir_space_ship` they are what answers,
    ahead of `<trace>/files/`.
  * **A RECORDED PATH IS UNTRUSTED INPUT, and a bundle root is only a boundary
    if every step checks it.** Recorded paths come out of containers this
    process did not write, so they may carry `..`. Both readers of the `files/`
    layout resolved one through the OS — in OPPOSITE steps: the engine's exact
    `bundled_source_path` mapping was unguarded while its suffix walk skipped
    `..`, and `resolvePayload`'s exact step called the guarded
    `safePayloadPath` while its walk did not. Measured on both: a recorded
    `/../../secret.txt` was served from outside the trace, labelled
    `sourceOrigin: "payload"` / `soTracePayload` ("the recording's own copy"),
    to clients built with `allowWorkingTree = false`. Because BOTH sides had a
    hole, the two providers agreed and `checkBothProvidersAgree` could not see
    it — agreement between two implementations is not a containment check.
    `expr_loader::is_within_bundle_root` and `source_provider`'s
    `isWithinPayloadRoot` are now the single answer on each side, applied at
    every step and by the WRITERS too (a container naming `res://../../x` would
    otherwise have the srcviews extractor place its bytes there). Symlinks out
    of a bundle are in scope and refused. If you add a step that maps a
    recorded path under a root, route it through those, not through a bare
    `join`.
  * **`Path::is_file()` is unconditionally `false` on
    `wasm32-unknown-unknown`** — measured, not read: a `cdylib` built for that
    target and run under node answers `0` for both `Path::new("/").is_file()`
    and `.exists()`, because the target falls to std's `unsupported` fs pal
    whose `stat` returns `Err(Unsupported)`. Consequence for anything gated on
    it: `resolve_bundled_source` can never resolve in a browser, at EITHER
    bundle root, even when `load_bundled_sources_from_vfs` has just put the
    container's own raw source views in the VFS. So `Handler::source` in a
    browser answers only from step 3 — `working-tree`, or a refusal under
    `allowWorkingTree: false`. `expr_loader::source_is_reachable`
    (`exists() || vfs`) is the probe that works on both targets; `is_file()`
    is not, and the difference is invisible on a native run.
  * **`async_compat.onComplete` defers its callback even on an ALREADY-COMPLETE
    `asyncdispatch` future.** Anything built on `BackendService.send` therefore
    answers asynchronously on the C backend too, and a caller that reads its
    `var` straight after the call gets the zero value. For an enum whose first
    member means success that is indistinguishable from an empty answer — which
    is why `SourceFetchStatus`'s consumers seed their result with
    `sfsProviderUnavailable`. `drainSourceCallbacks()` (or
    `async_compat.drainPlatformCallbacks`) is the pump.
  * **No ViewModel owns the CALL STACK, and `CalltraceVM` is not it** (CTUI-6,
    2026-09-06). DAP `stackTrace` is in `backend/dap_commands.nim`'s allow-list
    and has no ViewModel caller — a grep over `src/frontend/viewmodel/` finds
    two test files. `store/types.nim` has `CallLine` (a CALLTRACE row, with a
    `depth`) and `DebuggerState` (one `Location`), and no frame type at all.
    The two are different data: measured on `noir_space_ship`, a stop whose
    `stackTrace` reports 4 frames returns 81 `ct/load-calltrace-section` rows,
    most of them siblings and children of calls that already returned. A pane
    that wants frames must convert the engine's own `stackTrace` body
    (`tui/app/call_stack_binding.framesFromStackTrace`). What `CalltraceVM`
    genuinely owns and is worth binding is the SELECTION: `selectEntry` writes
    one signal and issues no backend command.
  * **`EditorVM.activeFileName` is a MEMO over
    `store.debugger.val.location.file`, not a settable "active tab".** So
    `SourceVM`, whose `revision` derives from it, CANNOT be pointed at another
    file: showing an outer stack frame's source needs its own
    `SourceLineRequest` through `sdk/source_provider`, not a re-point. And
    `SourceVM.executionLine` is a LINE with no file attached — a pane showing
    another file must suppress the execution pointer or it marks a line that
    never ran (`tui/app/call_stack_binding.sourcePaneModelForFrame`).
  * **isonim-tui has no OSC 8 / hyperlink concept at all** (grep over
    `isonim-tui/src/`, 2026-09-06): `Cell` carries no link id and `encodeAnsi`
    emits SGR and runes only. A front-end that wants hyperlinks emits them
    itself, INLINE with the frame — a second pass moves the cursor off the
    frame barrier `dual_snap.waitForCompleteFrame` depends on, and OSC 8 is not
    retroactive (libvterm fills the grid from the cursor at OPEN to the cursor
    at CLOSE). See `tui/app/views/hyperlinks.nim`.
  * **`waitForCompleteFrame` is not a barrier AFTER sending input to a snapshot
    app.** The cursor is already parked on the bottom-right cell from the
    previous frame, so the call returns immediately and the assertions read the
    pre-input screen. The child must NAME the new frame — `testing/
    test_app_runtime.nim` increments its step counter on any input-driven
    repaint so the parent can wait for `<label>-stepN`.
  * **`OriginChainVM` IS populated on a real replay session — it is NOT a
    fifth unfilled ViewModel field.** Measured on `noir_space_ship` through a
    real `replay-server`, 2026-09-06: `ct/originChain` is a real dispatch arm
    (`dap_server.rs` -> `Handler::origin_chain`), and the chain comes back on
    the `ct/updated-origin-chain` EVENT that `respond_origin_chain` emits
    beside the response — exactly one event per query. `remaining_shield` at
    tick 283 (`shield.nr:6`) answers one `okComputational` hop at tick 219,
    confidence 0.9; `mass` answers four. Three of six locals at that stop have
    a classified hop. `onShowOrigin` DISCARDS `send`'s future by design, so the
    event is the only place the answer is readable from a host — that is the
    documented event-driven path, not a workaround.
  * **An origin hop's `location` names the step that WROTE the value; its
    `step_id` names the step at which the write became OBSERVABLE. They are
    different steps on a line-entry recorder, and that is deliberate.**
    In `db.rs`'s `origin_chain_inferred`, step "(2) Resolve the source line",
    pass one takes the line of `last_change_step`; when that line does not
    parse as an assignment naming the target, pass two retries the immediately
    preceding step in the same frame — the case of a recorder that snapshots
    variables at line entry, which Noir's and Python's are. Pass two now keeps
    that step (`origin_step`) and the hop's `location` is built from it, so
    `location.line` and `source_text` always describe ONE statement.
    NAVIGATE BY `location` — `location.rrTicks` is the coordinate every
    front-end seeks to (`OriginChainVM.onSeekToHop`, the TUI's `o`), and
    `location.rrTicks` / `path` / `line` / `stackTrace` are mutually
    consistent. Do NOT navigate by `step_id`: it is the engine's cursor and
    `cross_process_origin::find_receive_marker_for_tail` correlates recorded
    marker firings against it with a ±1-step window. On a materialized trace
    `Location::rr_ticks` IS the step index (`trace_reader.rs::load_location`),
    so `location.rrTicks != step_id` on such a recorder — that inequality is
    the fix, not a bug.
    THIS WAS A LIVE DEFECT UNTIL 2026-09-06: pass two assigned only
    `line_text` and bound the step it found as `_prev_step`, which it then
    DISCARDED, so `location` kept naming the later step and `o` / the desktop's
    origin jump landed ONE RECORDED STEP AFTER the write. Measured then:
    `remaining_shield`'s `sourceText` was `remaining_shield += regeneration;`
    (line 12 of `shield.nr`) at `location.line` 14 (`status_report(…)`), tick
    220. Measured after the fix: `shield.nr:12`, tick 219 — the write.
    Guarded by `origin_dap_test.rs::test_origin_hop_location_names_the_writing_
    step_not_its_successor` (with `…_unchanged_for_post_write_snapshot_
    recorders` as its control) and, end to end on a real trace, by
    `tui/tests/test_value_origin_jump.nim`, whose inequality tripwire is now
    the corresponding EQUALITY.
  * **`keyName(" ")` is `"Space"`, and that made a space untypeable at a text
    prompt until CTUI-10.** The shadowing rule in `tui/app/input/keymap.resolve`
    used to ask `isPrintableKey`, which is false for a five-letter name, so a
    space in COMMAND/SEARCH resolved to `krNone` and was lost. Use
    `keymap.isTextKey` / `KeyResolution.character`, never `KeyResolution.key`,
    when inserting into a buffer.
  * **`unicode.strip` returns an ALL-whitespace string unchanged; `strutils.strip`
    returns `""`.** Measured on nim 2.2.8. Any file that imports both — seven
    of the ten Tier-2 TUI suites do, for `Rune` — gets `unicode.strip` on an
    unqualified call, so a blank terminal row reads as 100 characters. Qualify
    `strutils.strip` in any helper that trims a terminal row.
  * **A snapshot app may park the cursor off the frame barrier.**
    `testing/test_app_runtime.FrameEpilogue` writes bytes AFTER the frame — its
    one use is a CUP into a `:` prompt so `cursorPosition` can be asserted —
    and a parent driving such an app waits on `dual_snap.waitForCursorAt`
    rather than `waitForCompleteFrame`.
  * **CTUI-11: the TUI binary now RUNS.** `build/bin/codetracer-tui <trace>`
    opens a recording and paints the §3.1 screen. `main.nim` is still the
    wiring; the loop's state is `tui/app/runtime.nim`, the tty is
    `tui/host/terminal_driver.nim` and the live session is
    `tui/host/tui_session.nim`.
  * **`replay-server` EXITS 2 AND WRITES NO DAP for a folder it cannot open.**
    Measured directly. `DapStdioBackend.waitForEvent` is a blocking read with a
    message budget and no clock, so a front-end that had already claimed the
    alternate screen hung there with no input loop running.
    `tui/host/native_host.traceFolderProblem` `stat`s for the three trace
    shapes (`trace.bin`, `rr/`, a `.ct` container) BEFORE the tty is claimed. A
    shape-valid folder that stalls the handshake still stalls.
  * **`nim-libvterm`'s `synchronizedOutput()` is a LIVE FLAG, not a latch**, and
    **`TermAssert.assertSynchronizedRender` cannot fail** — its "not observed"
    arm is `discard` (`TermAssert/src/term_assert.nim:640-647`). To assert DEC
    2026 pairing, feed the emitter's own bytes to a fresh
    `nim_libvterm.newScreen` (true after the open, false after the close, and an
    unpaired stream latches true) and assert `false` on the live session.
  * **`isonim-tui`'s inline styles now understand `indexed:N`.** `ckIndexed` was
    always ENCODABLE (`text/ansi.fgParams` emits `38;5;N`) and had no spelling
    in `compositor.parseColorOrDefault`, so a truecolor→256→16→mono ladder could
    not have four distinct rungs. Additive: every string that reached that point
    previously fell through to `namedColor`.
  * **An EMPTY shell has zero styled spans.** `shell.paintPane` draws CTUI-3's
    plain `TITLE ────` row with the default style and only delegates to a
    pane's own painter when that pane's model has content. A style test built
    from `newShellModel` alone makes "no colour" true for free.
  * **The token framer, the timed read and `frameBytes` live in
    `tui/host/terminal_driver.nim`**, and `testing/test_app_runtime.nim`
    imports them. The direction is forced: no module under `testing/` may appear
    in `main.nim`'s import closure. The framer handles SS3 (`ESC O P`..`S`),
    which the old copy did not — §4.2's `F1` was unreachable until CTUI-11.
  * **`osproc.outputStream` IS BUFFERED ON POSIX** — `createStream(p.outHandle,
    fmRead)` is `open()` + `newFileStream()`, a C `FILE*`. A `select` in front
    of it sleeps on data `fread` has already taken off the pipe: measured with
    `ioctl(FIONREAD)` returning 0 while a blocking read on the same descriptor
    returned instantly. The UNBUFFERED `FileHandleStream` in
    `nim/lib/pure/streams.nim` is real and is `osproc`'s **Windows** arm.
    `viewmodel/backend/stdio_backend.nim` therefore reads
    `process.outputHandle` into its own buffer. If you ever put a readiness
    check in front of a stream you did not write, find out where the bytes are
    first — `codetracer-specs/Testing/Verification-Harness-Traps.md` §8.
  * **A signal kills the TUI rather than being translated into an exit code.**
    `nim-termctl`'s handler restores the terminal and lets the signal through,
    so `waitpid` reports `WIFSIGNALED` and `nim-pty` reports `-WTERMSIG` (`-2`
    for SIGINT, `-15` for SIGTERM) — not the shell's `128 + N`, which is the
    same wait status in a different encoding. `isonim-tui`'s own signal case
    asserts `130` because ITS fixture app installs a handler calling
    `quit(130)`.
  * **The strip cache never hits in the shipped TUI, and it is not the cache's
    fault.** `compositor.stripForEntry` keys on `LayoutEntry.nodeId`;
    `terminal_driver.composite` calls `resetNodeIds()` (so ids repeat) but
    builds a FRESH `Compositor` every frame, so the cache it would have hit is
    thrown away. Measured: 0 hits / 20,320 without `resetNodeIds()` per frame
    on a persistent compositor, 98.33% with it. CTUI-14 diffs at the byte level
    (`host/ssh_tuning.dirtyRuns`) instead and did not move the diff base,
    because every Tier-2 frame barrier rests on it.
  * **`TermAssert.effectiveEnv` applied its BLOCKLIST to its OVERRIDES**
    (`if k in blocked: continue`), so `.envRemove("X").envSet("X", v)` left
    `X` ABSENT. FIXED in `TermAssert` 2026-09-06 (an explicit `envSet` wins),
    but keep blocking **or** setting and never both: this workspace pins no
    `TermAssert` revision, so a lane can be built against a checkout that
    predates the fix.
  * **Two more `TermAssert` defects, both unfixed and both silent.**
    `sendKey` strips `shift+` and never uses it — measured, `sendKey("shift+f10")`
    and `sendKey("f10")` put the identical `^[[21~` on the wire, and `ctrl+`
    has the same hole for a NAMED key. And `assertSynchronizedRender` contains
    no `raise` at all (its "not observed" arm is a bare `discard`), so it
    passes over a child that emits zero DEC 2026 sequences — measured against
    `echo`. Both are traps 9 and 10 of
    `codetracer-specs/Testing/Verification-Harness-Traps.md`.
  * **`--headless` renders ONE settled frame, so it takes `--goto` and refuses
    `--record-keys` / `--replay-keys`.** The refusal is a usage error naming
    the conflict, not silence: all three used to parse into `tckHeadless` and
    `runHeadless` read none of them. When a MODE cannot honour a flag, refuse
    it — `app/cli.PlannedOptions`'s rule is about a list and this arrived
    through a mode, which is why the list could not catch it.
  * **A benchmark's `samples=` belongs to the ENTRY, not to the run.**
    `benchmarks/tui_benchmarks.nim` used to stamp the suite's loop budget onto
    all twelve entries, four of which are one observation of one spawned
    process. `record` now takes `samples` and `shape` with no defaults so a new
    metric cannot inherit somebody else's provenance by omission.
  * **`conditionGap` has no default either, and `cgNone` is why.** A benchmark
    row can beat its number under circumstances its target is not stated for —
    `tui/steady-state-rss` (no fixture is 50,000 ticks) and
    `tui/strip-cache-hit-rate` (the shipped driver rebuilds the compositor
    every frame, so its real rate is 0%). Both emitted a bare `verdict=met`,
    with the gap only in `extra`'s prose. `cgNone` is the POSITIVE claim that
    the condition held, so defaulting it hands that claim to every metric added
    later; the artifact now emits `met-CONDITION-NOT-external` /
    `-internal`, and every `verdict=` value is one whitespace-free token
    (`reported (not gated)` was read as `reported` by a space-splitting parse).
  * **A timing check on the DIFFERENCE of two runs cannot use an exact bound.**
    `tests/real_terminal/test_real_pty_lifecycle.nim` opens the same wedged
    session under a 1500 ms and a 6000 ms handshake budget and asserts the
    exit-time gap. It read `gap >= 4500` on the argument that "a loaded host
    makes this LARGER, never smaller" — false: each run pays its own overhead,
    so the gap is `4500 + (overhead_long - overhead_short)` and the bracket has
    either sign. It failed at 4499 ms with overheads of 26 and 25 ms, after
    passing three times. Now `abs(gap - 4500) <= 500`, which is STRONGER (the
    one-sided form accepted an arbitrarily large gap), with each run's own
    overhead separately asserted inside the same tolerance so it cannot hide a
    regression. See trap 12 in
    `codetracer-specs/Testing/Verification-Harness-Traps.md`.
  * **`tests/test_benchmark_artifact.nim` reads the COMMITTED
    `bench-results/benchmark_results.json`.** It derives entry count, names,
    condition-gap kinds and comparison directions from `tui_benchmarks.nim`'s
    own `record(...)` call sites, so a metric added without regenerating the
    artifact reddens the `tui` lane. Four of CTUI-14's defects were in the
    artifact and none could redden a suite, because nothing read it — if you
    commit a generated file, give it a reader.
  * **`TermAssert` can keep the raw byte stream**: `newTuiTest(…).transcript()`
    plus `transcriptBytes` / `transcriptDroppedBytes`. Off by default, capped at
    8 MB. It is the only way to assert a PAIRED alternate screen — the
    terminal's live alt-screen state after the child exits is `false` whether
    the child left correctly or never entered, the same trap DEC 2026 has.
  * **`src/common/paths.nim`'s tool paths are ACCESSORS (`bashExe()`), not
    `let`s, and the parentheses are load-bearing.** They used to be a
    module-level `let` block of 38 `findTool`/`findExe` values (39 searches —
    `mcrRecorderExe` tries `ct-mcr` then `ct_cli`), which Nim runs before
    `main`, so every `ct` invocation searched `PATH` for all of them and
    used at most one. `findExe` stats one path per `PATH` entry per absent tool,
    so the cost scaled with `len(PATH)`: 5,445 `newfstatat` calls (5,400
    failing) and 16.3 ms on a 193-entry nix dev shell `PATH`, which is what
    broke PLAT-1's 10 ms `--ui` handoff gate. Now `lazyToolPath` captures the
    env-var override eagerly (a hash lookup, not a syscall) and defers +
    memoises the `PATH` search: 46 calls, 2.6 ms. Adding a tool path means
    adding a `lazyToolPath`, never a `let` — `paths_test.nim`'s "no tool is
    searched for before main" reads `toolSearchCount()` at module-init time and
    reddens `common-units` if you do.
  * **To measure a start-up change, keep the OLD binary and make it a third arm
    of ONE run.** The `--ui` handoff benchmark is a difference of two medians,
    and the first attempt at showing the `paths.nim` fix took the "before" from
    a run made before the change and the "after" from a run made after it. The
    control arm moved from 1.54 ms to 5.47 ms between them with the load
    average LOWER in the second, so ~4 ms of unattributable host drift landed
    in a 10 ms gate, and the improvement was understated as 7.0 ms. Copying the
    pre-change `ct` aside, then interleaving {direct, pre-change, post-change}
    with the order shuffled per round, pairs every difference against the same
    instant of load and gave the real figure: 16.30 ms → 2.58 ms, median
    per-round delta −13.34 ms over 200 rounds. The build is byte-reproducible
    (`just build-once` twice gives an identical `ct`), so the old arm is cheap
    to reconstruct: revert the sources, build, copy the binary out, restore.
  * **A parameterless Nim proc is NOT transparently a value.** `echo bashExe`
    passes the *proc* to `varargs[typed]` rather than calling it, so a
    let-to-accessor conversion that relies on paren-less calls fails in some
    contexts and silently type-checks in others (`@[a, b]` builds a `seq[proc]`
    quite happily). Spell the call. The audit for a conversion like this is
    `grep -P '(?<!\w)(id1|id2|…)\b(?!\s*\()'` over the whole repo, which is
    what caught the remaining sites; the compiler alone will not.
  * **`ct` loads `--env-file` / `--env0-file` in `launch.nim` AFTER
    `paths.nim` has initialised**, so an env file that sets
    `CODETRACER_RUBY_EXE_PATH` and friends has never affected those values.
    The lazy conversion deliberately kept it that way by capturing the
    overrides eagerly — but this is a latent bug on the macOS `open` path,
    which is the reason env files exist at all. **`PATH` itself is the one
    exception, and it changed**: the tool search now runs after `loadEnvFiles`,
    so an env file carrying the user's `PATH` (which the macOS `open` dump
    does) now steers the recorder lookups where before they ran against
    launchd's `PATH`. That is the behaviour those flags were written for, but
    it is a change and not a no-op — do not describe the lazy conversion as
    value-preserving without this caveat.

## Value presentation is ONE pipeline now (PLAT-2, 2026-09-07)

There used to be seven implementations of "recorded value -> string" in this
tree.  There is one: `src/common/value_presentation/`.  If you are about to
render a value on any surface, do NOT write a formatter.

  * `presenter.present(v: PValue, budget: Budget, lang, measure) -> Presentation`
    is the only entry point.  `Presentation.root.text` is the one-line
    rendering; `.children` is the tree; `.class` is the semantic class a
    front-end colours by; `.attribution` says which presenter answered and
    what else matched.
  * Values arrive through ONE OF TWO ADAPTERS and never any other way:
    `value_presentation/json_adapter.toPValue` for the wire (this is what the
    TUI, the headless app and the Embed SDK see) and
    `common_types/utils/value_presentation_bridge.toPValue` for the desktop's
    `Value`.  The bridge is `include`d into `common_types`, not imported,
    because `Value` exists TWICE under different `langstring` bindings — see
    that file's header.
  * The desktop reaches all of it through `src/frontend/ui/presented_value.nim`
    and nothing else.  That is enforced:
    `ci/test/value-presentation-boundary.sh` (ten checks, wired into
    `ci/lint/nim.sh`) fails on any surface that names a deleted formatter,
    that does not import the pipeline, or that clips a presentation after
    receiving it.
  * TRUNCATION IS A BUDGET, NOT A POST-FILTER.  Each surface declares one in
    `surfaces.nim`; the presenter returns what fits.  Do not add a
    `truncate(present(v))` — the gate has a case for exactly that, in both
    orders, and its contract suite proves it fires.
  * THE PIPELINE IS `func` THROUGHOUT.  A presenter that reads the CLOCK, the
    FILESYSTEM or a module-level `var` does not compile.  This is what
    replaced `textRepr`'s read of the `CURRENT_LANG` global; the language is a
    PARAMETER with no ambient default, and `ui/presented_value.sessionLang`
    is the one place the session's language is read.

    THE ENVIRONMENT IS THE EXCEPTION, and this file said otherwise until
    2026-09-07.  `std/envvars.getEnv` carries `ReadEnvEffect` as a TAG rather
    than as a side effect, so it COMPILES inside a `func`:

        import std/envvars
        func f*(s: string): string = s & getEnv("HOME")   # nim check accepts

    What keeps it out of the pipeline is the IMPORT LINT — `envvars` is in
    `ci/test/value-presentation-boundary.sh`'s `FORBIDDEN_PIPELINE_IMPORTS`.
    The contract suite has an arm that plants the import and watches the gate
    fire, and another that asserts the compiler's acceptance, so the split
    cannot quietly become wrong again in either direction.
  * `$value` ON A `Value` IS A MULTI-LINE DIAGNOSTIC DUMP, AND NOTHING WILL
    STOP YOU WRITING IT.  `common_types/utils/text_representation` still
    defines `$` / `text` — `"Sequence(Seq [Field; 4]):\n  100\n…"` — for `echo`
    and error messages.  A pane reached it exactly once (`ui/state.nim`'s
    `valueDisplayText` fallback) and that call site is gone.  A gate rule
    banning `$value` inside a surface WAS written, run, and REMOVED: all five
    hits were false positives in `ui/value.nim` (`$value` over a float in a
    chart histogram; `$value.typ.langType`, a type NAME).  `$` is not lexically
    separable, so the boundary gate declares this as a bound instead of
    claiming it.  If you are adding a fallback anywhere that renders a value —
    `ui/state.nim` especially — reach for `ui/presented_value.nim`.  Nothing
    else is watching.
  * THE GATE IS A NAME BLOCKLIST OVER A HAND-WRITTEN FILE LIST.  Read its
    header before trusting a green run: four bypass shapes are declared as
    bounds rather than caught — a surface that keeps the import and formats by
    hand under a fresh name, a formatter in a module the `SURFACES` list does
    not name, a cut placed more than three lines below the presentation, and
    `$value`.  The second is not hypothetical: `ui/calltrace.nim` carried
    `safeCallArgText` through the whole of PLAT-2's first pass while the gate
    reported ten checks and zero failures.  ADDING A SURFACE TO THIS PRODUCT
    MEANS ADDING IT TO `SURFACES` BY HAND.
  * A useful side effect worth knowing: `isonim_tui.displayWidth(s)` reads a
    threadvar (the ambiguous-width policy).  Use the `func` overload
    `displayWidth(s, ambiguous)` from anything that has to be pure —
    `tui/app/formatters/type_formatters.terminalMeasure` and
    `tui/app/views/inline_annotations.cellWidth` are the two, and both pin
    `type_formatters.TerminalAmbiguousWidth` (= `awNarrow`).

    A LATENT SPLIT THIS CREATES.  isonim-tui's OWN render stack — compositor,
    layout, `Content` wrapping, the widgets — still calls the threadvar
    spellings, so it honours `setAmbiguousWidth` at runtime while CodeTracer's
    measure does not.  In a process that called `setAmbiguousWidth(awWide)`,
    the library would lay a value out at one width while the presenter
    budgeted it at another, and a value column that overflows by one cell
    corrupts every column after it.  Nothing calls the setter today, so this
    is a note and not a bug — but if you ever want `awWide`, thread the policy
    through isonim-tui's render stack rather than unpinning the measure here.
  * THREE VALUE-ADJACENT TRUNCATIONS ARE NOT ON THE BUDGET, on purpose.
    `event_log_vm.summarise` (a marker's `summary:<n>`, which cuts a string
    the instrumented program chose, not a `Value`),
    `origin_chain_types.abbreviateExpr` (a SOURCE EXPRESSION, under a user
    preference) and `inline_annotations.fitAnnotation` (a JOIN of several
    already-budgeted values, clipped to room known only after the code line is
    painted).  Each carries its reason in its own doc comment.  The first two
    used to index by BYTE over UTF-8 and now count runes; do not reintroduce
    that.

PLAT-3 — THE ABSTRACT VIEW VOCABULARY (2026-09-07)
  * THERE IS ONE VOCABULARY ENUM AND IT LIVES ON THE VALUE SIDE.
    `common/value_presentation/vocabulary.PresentationKind` carries all
    SIXTEEN of PLAT-3's entries (`pkText` … `pkMarkdown`);
    `common/view_vocabulary/vocabulary.ViewKind` is an ALIAS of it, not a
    second enum.  PLAT-2 opened the enum with five entries and PLAT-3 added
    eleven, renaming nothing.  If you find yourself declaring a second `Text`,
    stop.
    The PRESENTER's range is still the five a recorded value can inhabit;
    `value_presentation_test`'s "the presenter's range is still PLAT-2's five"
    case is what keeps the widening of the enum from widening what a value
    becomes.
  * `applyKey` IS THE ONLY DEFINITION OF WHAT A KEY DOES, and the terminal
    binding DELIBERATELY DOES NOT CALL IT.  `frontend/view_vocabulary/
    terminal_binding.nim` fires a real `keydown` at a real isonim-tui widget
    and reads the answer out of the widget's own fields, which is what makes
    `tui/tests/test_view_vocabulary_cross_medium.nim` an independent check
    rather than a function compared with itself.  Do not "simplify" it by
    routing the terminal arm through `applyKey`; the suite would still be
    green and would no longer mean anything.
  * `isonim_tui.TabsWidget` WRAPS AND NO OTHER WIDGET DOES.  `moveRight` on
    the last tab sets the first.  ListView, OptionList, Tree, DataTable,
    RadioSet, ContentSwitcher and MarkdownViewer all CLAMP — read one by one,
    NOT grepped: `grep -n wrap widgets/*.nim` returns 34 lines and all but two
    are text wrapping or the word `wrapper`, so that grep proves nothing.
    Outside `widgets/`, `command/palette.nim` moves `selectedIdx` modularly
    and `focus/manager.nim` wraps Tab traversal, so the claim is about
    widgets, not the library.  The vocabulary's `Tabs` does
    not wrap; the divergence is ASSERTED in the cross-medium suite and
    `mappings.terminalMapping(pkTabs)` is `msPartial` because of it.  If you
    change either side, that case is where you will hear about it.
  * `unittest.check` INSIDE A PLAIN `proc` CANNOT FAIL THE TEST.  Nim's
    `unittest` injects `testStatusIMPL` as a local in the `test` template AND
    declares a global fallback, so an assertion helper written as a `proc`
    compiles, prints `Check failed`, and the test still reports `[OK]`.
    Measured here: the cross-medium suite's Tabs case printed a failed
    comparison and passed.  Write assertion helpers as TEMPLATES.
  * THREE VOCABULARY ENTRIES HAVE NO GPUI TAG AT ALL — `Table`, `Modal`,
    `ProgressIndicator`.  `isonim-gpui`'s `tagMap` has 35 keys and none of
    `table`/`tr`/`td`/`dialog`/`progress` is among them; `mapTag` passes an
    unknown tag through unchanged.  `mappings.GpuiTagMap` is a COPY of those
    keys (isonim_gpui dlopens a Rust cdylib at module init, so it cannot be
    imported from a unit test) and `view_vocabulary_test` verifies the copy
    against isonim-gpui's source with a count assertion.
  * PLAT-2's ATTRIBUTION NOW HAS A PRODUCT CALL SITE: the TUI variables
    pane's title row (`views/variables.provenanceOf` / `provenanceBadgeOf` /
    `fitProvenance`).  A narrow pane shows the SHORT form or nothing, never a
    clipped one — `builtin.reco…` names a presenter nobody can grep for.  The
    desktop has no equivalent affordance yet.
  * THE TRANSIENT LAYOUT MACHINE IS A SEPARATE MODULE ON PURPOSE.
    `headless_app/layout_interaction.nim` (PLAT-5) imports
    `headless_app/layout_model.nim`, so a `Layout` field of type
    `Interaction`/`DropTarget` is an IMPORT CYCLE Nim rejects — that is the
    structural check, not a review convention.  `Layout` is passed BY VALUE
    but `Layout.tree` is a `ref`, so a routine that looks pure can still
    write through it; the "cancelled drag is byte-identical" case compares
    `$saveLayout(...)` for exactly that reason, and two mutation arms
    (P3/P5 in `run-plat5-mutations.py`) plant that leak.
  * `commit` DOES NOT DECIDE WHAT "NOTHING HAPPENED" MEANS.  It hands the
    command to `apply` and yields `some` only on `loApplied`, so its `none`
    and `loNoOp` cannot drift.  That is why `lcSplit`'s move arm has its own
    `loNoOp` (via `equalTrees`) in `layout_model` rather than a shape
    comparison in the transient layer.
  * `lcSplit` HAS A `splitMovesPane` FLAG.  False (every PLAT-4 caller) means
    the new pane must NOT be in the tree; true means it must be, and the
    split MOVES it.  It exists because `commit` yields ONE command and
    "drag a tab onto a pane's edge" had no single-command spelling.  A
    DOCKED source still has none — `lcSplit` refuses a pane in `docked`, so
    `dropTargetsFor` offers a docked pane no split target at all.
  * THE TERMINAL LAYOUT BINDING (PLAT-6) IS `tui/app/layout/binding.nim`, and
    the two directions of its hit-test READ ONE TABLE.  `pointerAt` (cells ->
    `LayoutPointer`) and `cellsFor` (`DropRegion` -> cells) both measure edge
    bands with `edgeBandCells` against `dropAreaOfPath` — the node's rectangle
    MINUS its tab strip, because a strip is a drop region of its own and a cell
    that meant both would make the two directions disagree.  Consequence for a
    mutation arm: mutating the SHARED helper changes both and the round-trip
    case stays green, correctly.  To kill it, mutate ONE direction.
  * `tabRow` LIVES IN `tui/app/layout/tab_strip.nim`, NOT IN `views/shell.nim`,
    AND IT CALLS `tabSpans`.  It walks the span table and fills to each span's
    `startCol`, owning no gap and no cursor of its own, so "which tab is at
    column 34" is one answer BY CONSTRUCTION.  (PLAT-6 landed it as a second
    traversal that only shared `tabLabel` and `TabGapCells`; its follow-up made
    it structural.)  `views/shell.nim` re-exports the module (and
    `PaneRuleGlyph`), so every CTUI-3 call site still resolves and the goldens
    are byte-identical.
    CONSEQUENCE FOR A CHECK, and it is the same one the hit-test's shared
    `edgeBandCells` has: comparing the painter with the span table can no
    longer see a defect IN the table, because both move together.
    `test_layout_binding.nim` therefore also carries `specStrip` — §3.1's label
    rule written out a second time, calling nothing in the module under test.
    M5 (painter stops following the table) dies in the differential half; M26
    (`TabGapCells` widened) dies only in the absolute one.
  * PLAT-6'S LAYOUT BINDING IS AN OPT-IN: `codetracer-tui --layout-binding`.
    `main.nim` calls `app/runtime.enableLayoutBinding`, and `runPromptLine`
    then routes the twelve layout verbs (`:dock`, `:move-tab`, `:resize`, …)
    into `binding.runLayoutCommand` — a PREFIX on the `:` path, deliberately
    not a seventeenth row in §4.3's published table, which
    `test_gdb_command_surface.nim` parses out of `CodeTracer-TUI.md`.
    WITHOUT THE FLAG NOTHING CHANGES: `layoutBinding` is nil, `shellModel`
    carries the session's own `LayoutNode`, and those words are unknown
    commands.  It is an opt-in rather than the default because a binding's tree
    is a CLONE of the session's, so enabling one by default would give the
    terminal two layout authorities; flipping it needs `HeadlessSessionSlot` to
    hold a `Layout` rather than a `LayoutNode`.
  * BOTH HALVES OF THE LAYOUT GESTURE SURFACE ARE ROUTED, behind the same
    `--layout-binding` opt-in: `runPromptLine` takes the twelve `:` verbs and
    `handleToken` -> `routeMouseReport` takes a decoded SGR-1006 report into
    `binding.onMouse`.  With the flag OFF `decodeMouse` is not even called and a
    mouse report takes the path it always took — `keyName` answers "" and
    `keymap.resolve` reports `krNone`.
    THE LAYOUT BINDING IS THE ONLY MOUSE CONSUMER IN THIS FRONT-END, and that
    was measured rather than assumed: `call_stack_keys.applyMouse` and
    `timeline_keys.applyMouse` are each reached only from their own module's
    `applyKey` / `applyToken`, and those only from `tests/apps/app_call_stack
    .nim` and `tests/apps/app_timeline.nim`.  (An earlier note here called that
    precedence question "the part that is NOT small"; there was no contest at
    all.)  `lasNoGesture` is the seam a pane's own mouse contract belongs at
    when one is wired.
  * A MOUSE GESTURE'S FOCUS SYNC HAS A RETURN LEG THE `:` PATH DOES NOT NEED.
