    A typed verb cannot move `LayoutBinding.focus`; a mouse press can, so
    `routeMouseReport` rebuilds the ring and then carries the binding's answer
    back into it.  (The gap recorded here on 2026-09-07 — the REBUILD on the
    mouse path having no arm, so deleting `rt.rebuildFocus()` there left `Tab`
    offering a pane a drop had just docked away with every case green — is
    CLOSED: `test_layout_command_routing.nim`'s "a mouse DROP rebuilds the focus
    ring, so Tab cannot offer a docked pane" is the assertion and M37 is the
    arm, with S10 as its behaviour-preserving control.)
  * A REARRANGED TERMINAL LAYOUT IS PERSISTED, PER RECORDING, BEHIND THE SAME
    `--layout-binding` OPT-IN.  `main.nim` calls
    `host/layout_store.restoreLayoutForSession` before the first frame and
    `persistLayoutForSession` after the input loop.  The split is
    `host/capabilities` -> `app/theme/capabilities` again:
    `app/layout/persistence.nim` decides the file's NAME, what an unreadable
    document MEANS and whether there is anything to write, and does NO I/O — no
    `getEnv`, no file, no fd, no `std/os` in its imports; `host/layout_store.nim`
    owns the environment, the read, the atomic write (`<path>.new` then
    `moveFile`) and the remove.  Four rules worth knowing before touching it:
      - the document is `<state root>/tui-layouts/<slug>-<sha1 prefix>.json`,
        keyed by the recording's CANONICAL path.  Not in the trace folder (a
        recording is shared, archived and may be read-only); state rather than
        config; per recording so one arrangement never silently applies to
        another.  Slug AND digest, because `/a/calc.ct` and `/b/calc.ct` share a
        basename and a directory of bare hex is unauditable;
      - AN UNREADABLE DOCUMENT IS REPORTED BY KIND ON THE STATUS LINE AND LEFT
        ALONE.  The message leads with the kind because `statusBarText`
        truncates the tail, and the file is QUARANTINED — a document from a
        NEWER build is `UnknownVersion` here, and overwriting it would destroy a
        user's arrangement for having opened an older binary once;
      - A RESTORED DOCUMENT SETS `userModified`, so it freezes the responsive
        profile.  Which is why AN UNTOUCHED SESSION WRITES NOTHING: persisting a
        profile default would pin the Compact tree on a wide terminal for ever.
        `:reset-layout` clears the flag and the exit DELETES the document;
      - with the flag off `restoreLayoutForSession` refuses before it computes a
        path, so the state directory is not touched, not even by a `stat`.
    `CODETRACER_TUI_LAYOUT_DIR` overrides the state root; that is how the two
    persistence suites point a real spawned binary at a directory of their own.
    THE "file would not OPEN at all" PATH IS THE ONE ONLY
    `runtime.markLayoutDocumentUnreadable` DEFENDS — the other failure arms set
    the flag inside `adoptLayoutDocument` — and neutering it DELETES the
    document (the plan becomes `lpiRemove`) while the report stays exactly the
    same, still saying "the file was left alone".  So the case that grades it
    asserts THE FILE, never the message: `test_layout_persistence.nim`'s "a
    document that will not OPEN is quarantined and survives byte-identical",
    with a real `setFilePermissions(doc, {})`, a positive twin that puts the
    permission back, and a LOUD skip (banner + `unittest.skip()` + a second
    exact assertion count) on a host where root or the filesystem will not deny
    the read.  M45 is the arm, S18 its control.
    KNOWN GAP, recorded 2026-09-08: THE PRECEDENCE is still unmeasured.
    `layoutPersistPlan` tests `quarantined` BEFORE `userModified`, which is what
    stops an older build that could not read a newer-schema document from
    overwriting it after the user rearranges a pane.  Weakening that branch to
    `quarantined and not userModified` survives the whole 66-arm harness, and
    the measured consequence is a `version: 99` document replaced by a
    `version: 2` one.  The grading case plants an unreadable document and never
    gestures, so it cannot reach the combination.
    The `app/` -> `host/` split IS enforced now, by
    `ci/test/tui-layer-split-boundary.sh` (wired into `ci/lint/nim.sh`); note
    its subject is the DECISION HALF OF A DECLARED PAIR and not all of `app/`,
    because `app/input/keymap.nim` legitimately imports `std/os` and calls
    `readFile`.  Its rule and its positive control must go through ONE scanner —
    they were two copies of a regex until 2026-09-08, and breaking only the
    rule's copy left every check green over a planted violation.
  * `LayoutBinding.focus` AND `TuiRuntime.focus` ARE TWO DIFFERENT FOCUS
    NOTIONS, and `runPromptLine` synchronises them before every layout command.
    `PaneFocus` is what `Tab` / `Ctrl+w` move; `LayoutBinding.focus` is what
    `:dock` and `:move-tab` act on.  Forgetting the copy makes a verb act on
    whatever pane the binding was constructed with, which is a defect no screen
    assertion names — `test_layout_command_routing.nim`'s "the pane a verb acts
    on is the one Tab moved to" is what catches it.
  * `run-plat6-mutations.py` DECODES THE SUITE'S OUTPUT WITH
    `errors="replace"`.  A MUTATED suite prints diagnostics about bytes it did
    not expect, and these suites quote painted rows — which carry U+2500 and
    U+2591..U+2593 — into their failure messages, so a mutated run's output is
    not guaranteed to be well-formed UTF-8.  Under Python's default strict
    decode, `subprocess.run` raises and the arm ends in a TRACEBACK rather than
    in one of the harness's three verdicts, which is the "no verdict at all"
    state the three-verdict rule exists to keep visible.  (Found when a
    tab-strip mutation made the case quote a partial rune, back when it sliced
    rows by BYTE index at cell offsets; `sliceCells` fixed that particular
    source, and the decode rule stands regardless.)  Same shape for any harness
    that reads a child's stdout as text.
  * A NEW `.nim` FILE ANYWHERE UNDER `src/frontend/tui/app/` — INCLUDING UNDER
    `app/tests/` — REDDENS `app/tests/test_gdb_command_surface.nim`.  It asserts
    `ExpectedAppModules` exactly, and that brittleness is deliberate: the count
    bounds the set its "called from exactly one file" rows are measured over.
    Re-count in the same change.  The same file also asserts an exact count of
    `const CellStyle` literals under `app/`, so a new module that paints must
    move `ExpectedStyleLiterals` too — which is why `binding.nim`'s decorations
    carry a GLYPH per kind and no style at all.
  * `testing/dual_snap.newestSourceTime` STAMPS `app/` **AND `host/`**.  A
    Tier-2 child binary is rebuilt when anything under either moves, and `host/`
    is in the stamp because `tests/apps/app_layout_persist.nim` calls
    `host/layout_store`.  Leave it in: without it a mutation arm against a
    `host/` module is graded by a child built BEFORE the mutation, which is a
    comparison of two different programs and fails — or passes — for a reason
    that has nothing to do with the subject.
  * `layout_interaction.nodeAtPath` HANDS OUT A LIVE `ref` INTO THE COMMITTED
    TREE.  Use `nodeInfoAtPath`, which returns a `NodeInfo` value with no
    reference field; `binding.nim` names `nodeAtPath` nowhere and its test
    asserts that with a source scan plus a `fieldPairs` walk with a counted
    control.  What is NOT closed: `Layout.tree` is a public `ref`, so any holder
    of a `Layout` can still write through it.
  * A TERMINAL CAN ONLY MOUSE-DOCK TO THE TOP AND BOTTOM until something is
    docked left or right.  A drop docks when it lands outside the tree area, and
    the only such cells are the dock strips and the header/status rows — there
    is no column left of column 0.  `:dock left` / `:dock right` reach the other
    two edges.
  * THE THREE RESPONSIVE PROFILES ARE DELIBERATELY DIVERGENT FROM
    `defaultReplayLayout()`, and a user's arrangement FREEZES the profile:
    `binding.resize` re-selects the profile always but rebuilds the tree only
    while `userModified` is false.  `:reset-layout` is the way back.  Recorded
    in Layout-ViewModel §8.2/§8.4 and CodeTracer-TUI.md §3.2.

PLAT-8 verification repairs, 2026-09-09 — four stdlib facts that cost time
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
  * `os.expandFilename` IS NOT THE SAME FUNCTION ON TWO PLATFORMS, and its doc
    comment ("Follows symlinks") describes only one of them.  POSIX: `realpath(3)`,
    which resolves every symlink.  Windows: `GetFullPathNameW`, which normalises
    `.`/`..` and does NOT resolve symlinks or junctions.  So a security check
    written as "canonicalise, then compare textually" is a real containment on
    Linux and a no-op on Windows.  `plugin_io.canonicalPath` uses it and says so;
    the write-up of that repair first claimed cross-platform coverage and had to
    be corrected.  Verification-Harness-Traps §8's shape: two `when` arms, one
    plausible sentence.
  * `std/posix` DOES NOT DECLARE `O_NOFOLLOW` on any platform — checked across
    `posix_linux_amd64_consts`, `posix_other_consts` and `posix_freertos_consts`,
    which all carry `O_CLOEXEC` and not this.  Import it from the header
    (`{.importc: "O_NOFOLLOW", header: "<fcntl.h>".}`) rather than writing the
    number: it is 0o400000 on Linux and 0x100 on the BSDs and macOS.
  * `osproc`'s `poDaemon` IS `POSIX_SPAWN_SETPGROUP` WITH A PGROUP OF 0, AND
    NOTHING ELSE, on the `posix_spawn` path — no `setsid`, no stdio change.  That
    makes the child its own process-group leader atomically at spawn, which is the
    only race-free way to make `killpg` reach a child's descendants from the
    parent (a parent-side `setpgid` can lose to `execve`).  BUT
    `startProcessAuxFork` IGNORES IT ENTIRELY, so a build where
    `useProcessAuxSpawn` is false silently gets no process group.  Always read the
    group back with `getpgid` and compare it with the pid before `killpg`: if they
    differ the child shares YOUR group, and `killpg` would SIGKILL the whole
    application.
  * A CHILD'S CHILD CANNOT BE REAPED BY YOU.  `rawKill` reaps its own child with
    `peekExitCode` (a `waitpid`), so `/proc/<child>` is gone immediately after a
    teardown.  A grandchild reparents to `init` or the session subreaper when its
    parent dies, and THAT reaps it, on its own schedule — so `/proc/<grandchild>`
    lingers in state `Z` for an unbounded moment.  A teardown test may assert the
    grandchild is dead (`Z` or gone); asserting `/proc` is gone races the reaper.

PLAT-8 source admission, 2026-09-09 — five nim facts the allow-list rests on
---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  * `import` IS NOT `export`, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE MEMBERSHIP RULE.  A module a
    plugin imports hands it that module's EXPORTED symbols plus any module that
    module `export`s WHOLE — never the modules it merely imports.  `std/times`
    imports `std/posix` on the POSIX arm and re-exports none of it, so
    `times.fork` does not resolve; `std/intsets` IS `export packedsets`, so
    admitting the first commits an allow-list to the second.  Measured with
    `declared(alias.name)` in `src/common/plugin_source_admission_test.nim`, not
    read off the source.
  * `import std/x as y` HIDES THE NAME `x`.  After `import std/os as d_os`,
    `declared(os.getEnv)` is FALSE.  That is what makes a probe file able to
    import both the modules it admits and the modules it refuses without the
    refused ones polluting the "no bare module name is reachable" assertion.
  * `declared(m.name)` ANSWERS FALSE FOR AN UNDECLARED `m` RATHER THAN FAILING
    TO COMPILE, so a corpus of qualified names can be asked of any module alias.
    Use QUALIFIED lookup: unqualified `declared(readFile)` answers about
    `system`, which exports `readFile`, `open`, `close` and `write` into every
    scope in the language, and every module then looks dangerous.
  * A `'` AFTER AN ALPHANUMERIC IS A TYPE SUFFIX, NOT A CHARACTER LITERAL.
    `4096'u64` and `1'f32` are ordinary integer/float literals.  Any scanner
    that treats `'` as a char-literal opener swallows from there to the next
    apostrophe — measured on a trap-13 sweep, where
    `updateDebuggerPosition(21'u64, …)` merged 102 lines into one and put a whole
    suite's `check`s inside a helper `proc`'s body, i.e. a FALSE POSITIVE that
    looked exactly like a real finding.
  * `nim` READS `config.nims` FROM THE PROJECT FILE'S DIRECTORY AND ITS
    ANCESTORS.  A generated probe compiled out of `/tmp` therefore fails with
    `cannot open file: isonim/core/signals` while the identical file under the
    repository compiles: this repo's `config.nims` is what puts the siblings on
    the path.  Stage generated sources under `build/` (gitignored) rather than
    `/tmp` when they import anything of ours.
  * COREUTILS' `timeout` IS A MULTI-CALL BINARY HERE.  `findExe("timeout")`
    returns `/nix/store/…/bin/coreutils`, and `osproc.startProcess` sets
    `argv[0]` to the command string it was given — so the child runs as
    `coreutils` and answers `unrecognized option '--hints:off'`, naming your
    ARGUMENT rather than the program.  Pass the bare name with `poUsePath`.

- 2026-09-09 (PLAT-8, the `system` residual): **`system.nim` ends with
  `when not defined(nimPreviewSlimSystem): import std/syncio; export syncio`.**
  That one line is why "what a plugin can call with no import" is not a list
  anybody in this repo can keep. Consequences that cost three passes to learn:
  * THE SURFACE IS `std/syncio`'s WHOLE EXPORTED API — 36 entries on nim 2.2.8,
    not the ten the residual used to name. `open`, `reopen`, `lines`,
    `readBuffer`, `readBytes`, `readChars`, `writeBuffer`, `writeBytes`,
    `writeChars`, `writeLine`, `getFileHandle`, `getOsFileHandle`,
    `getFileSize`, `getFilePos`, `setFilePos`, `endOfFile`, `flushFile`,
    `setInheritable`, `setStdIoUnbuffered` are all reachable in a module whose
    entire import list is `import codetracer_plugin`.
  * `reopen(stdin, path, fmRead)` READS ANY FILE WITH NO `open`. `stdin` is
    already a `File`, so denying `open` alone closes nothing. Likewise
    `for l in lines(path)` is a whole file read in one identifier.
  * `compiles(lines("x"))` IS **FALSE** while `for l in lines("x")` compiles and
    reads the file — an iterator is not callable as an expression. Any
    `compiles`-shaped reachability probe answers the question you spelled, so
    ask iterators in the `for` spelling: `compiles((for x in it(): discard))`.
  * THE ENUMERATION IS DERIVED, NOT TYPED: `ci/lib/system-io-surface.sh` sweeps
    `std/syncio.nim` and `system/compilation.nim` (plus their `include`
    closures) off the compiler on PATH, and the gate's check 23 requires every
    derived name to be on `PluginDeniedSyncIo` or `PluginSystemSurfaceExempt`.
    Validated against `nim jsondoc`: the sweep reproduces the compiler's view of
    `syncio` exactly, 36/36. It reads every `when` branch on purpose, so it
    over-reports (`nimrtl`) rather than under-reporting.
  * `hostOnly` IS READ OFF THE SAME LINE AS THE NAME. `table_names … host-only`
    matches `, true)` in the record it just matched `("name"` in, so an entry
    split across a continuation loses the flag SILENTLY — the symptom is
    `sdk-does-not-block` firing on a line the table meant to exempt.
  * A `'` ANYWHERE IN THE `table_names` awk PROGRAM — INCLUDING IN A COMMENT —
    ends the single-quoted shell word, and bash then reports a syntax error at
    a token forty lines away that nobody wrote.
  * THE FFI PRAGMA DENYLIST AND THE IMPORT ALLOW-LIST ARE ONE COMPOSED DEFENCE.
    A macro-built pragma (`nnkPragma.newTree(ident("importc"))`) puts the word
    on no line of source and yields ZERO pragma spans; what refuses it is
    `std/macros` not being on the allow-list. Admitting any reflective module
    retires the pragma denylist with it.

THE `system` SURFACE SWEEP NEEDS NIM'S LOCATION, NOT A RUNNING NIM (2026-09-11)
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  * `ci/lib/system-io-surface.sh` reads the stdlib's SOURCE off disk; the only
    thing it ever needed a running compiler for is WHERE that source is, because
    the nix layout (`<prefix>/nim/lib`) and the source layout (`<prefix>/lib`)
    disagree. So a `nim … dump` that cannot be RUN — starved, OOM-killed, or a
    release that stops printing the line — used to be indistinguishable from
    "this compiler has no standard library", and check 23 of
    `ci/test/plugin-reactive-boundary.sh` then reported it as an unaccounted
    surface, i.e. as a finding about THIS repository. Measured: a host at load
    ~1200 with 2 GB free (1250 orphaned processes from a runaway in a SIBLING
    workspace, `~/m/dev/reprobuild`) took `test_plugin_source_admission.nim`'s
    `VIOLATION` count from 2 to 3 that way, and the transcript sent two readers
    hunting for a PATH defect that was not there. The suite's environment is
    fine: it inherits PATH through `startProcess`, and the suite is green from
    the lane's exact environment at load 1200.
  * The repair is `system_surface_lib_from_exe`: the SAME nim (`command -v`), a
    second DERIVATION of its library directory, returned only once a root has
    been looked at. It is the shape `src/db-backend/build.rs` already uses for
    the same compiler. On the pinned 2.2.8 `<prefix>/lib` is a symlink to
    `<prefix>/nim/lib`, so `pwd -P` makes the two derivations string-identical
    and the agreement control a plain comparison.
  * FAILING CLOSED IS UNCHANGED, and it is the property to check first when
    touching this file: with no nim, or with a nim beside no library carrying
    `std/syncio.nim`, the sweep still derives nothing and check 23 still
    refuses. PLAT-8's arm S7 is what holds that down — it is graded by the
    contract suite's `no-nim` stub, which shadows the real nim on PATH, so the
    fallback resolves to the STUB's directory and finds nothing there.
  * `shfmt -s` AND `shellcheck` DISAGREE ABOUT BACKTICKS IN A SHELL LITERAL, and
    both run as pre-commit hooks. `shfmt -s` rewrites a double-quoted literal
    that needs no expansion into a single-quoted one; a single-quoted literal
    containing a BACKTICK PAIR is then SC2016 and shellcheck exits 1. A string
    carrying an apostrophe is immune, because shfmt cannot single-quote it.
    Worth knowing before writing ``"… \`nim dump\`"`` into a `ci/` script.

## Terminal image rendering (PLAT-14, 2026-09-13)

- `src/common/terminal_graphics/` is the tier renderer: `tiers.nim` (§2.1's
  seven tiers, ordinal == the spec's tier number so `weakerOf` is `max`),
  `cell_render.nim` (one Oklab argmin for every cell tier), `aspect.nim`,
  `emit.nim` (THE bytes), `media.nim` (which §5.2 media a resolved terminal
  can draw). The DECISION lives in `src/frontend/tui/app/theme/
  image_capability.nim` beside `resolveCapabilities`, and the empirical probe
  in `src/frontend/tui/host/image_probe.nim`.
- **`TermAssert.assertImageMatches` cannot fail on its first run.** It writes
  the golden and returns when the golden file does not exist
  (`TermAssert/src/term_assert.nim:697-710`), which is exactly the situation a
  new test is in. Assert `imageData(...)` pixels directly instead. Its sibling
  `assertSynchronizedRender` has a bare `discard` where its failure should be.
- **`nim-libvterm` really decodes Kitty PNG payloads** (stb_image is vendored
  and compiled in), so a Tier-2 suite can assert decoded RGBA from the
  terminal's side. `sess.images()` also registers the PROBE's own 1x1 query
  image, so a case that counts images must not run in probe mode.
- **`nim_termctl.emitSixel` raises** — there is no Sixel encoder in the
  workspace. Anything that "detects sixel" must refuse to select it.
- Nim's `unittest.check` prints `<operand> was <value>` for dotted and call
  expressions too, and an enum with string values prints the STRING
  (`prScreenNeverPasses` prints as `screen-never-passes`). Mutation-harness
  `because` needles have to be derived from a transcript, not typed.

## The image capability's OVERRIDE path, and the fence that is not evidence (2026-09-14)

- **`GraphicsProbe.answered` is the DA1 fence and is NOT evidence about
  graphics.** `host/image_probe.probeGraphics` sends the Kitty query with
  `CSI c` behind it, and every terminal and every multiplexer answers `CSI c`.
  The module's single predicate for "a graphics reply came back" is
  `measuredProtocol`. Three rules in `image_capability.nim` turn on this and
  each was written independently; two of the three tested the fence, and the
  verification pass of 2026-09-14 is what found them. If you add a fourth,
  call `measuredProtocol`.
- **`--image-tier=protocol` cannot name a protocol**, because there is no
  spelling of the flag that does. `emittableProtocol(advertised, probe)` is
  the one question — an advertisement in `EmittableProtocols`, else a
  measured reply, else `ipNone` and the pin loses. Never synthesise one: the
  old code ended `else: ipITerm2`, and `probe.iterm2` is documented
  always-false, so that arm was an unconditional guess.
- `ImageCapability.protocol` is NEVER `ipSixel` on any path. `emitProtocolImage`
  raises for it, so selecting it turns a picture into an exception — and the
  override used to reach past `prSixelHasNoEncoder` and do exactly that.
- **`unittest` prints the SUBSTITUTED AST and escapes as `\e`, not `\x1b`.**
  Two `because` needles typed from the source scored `MIS-ATTRIBUTED` over
  mutations that killed their cases exactly as intended: `cap.refusal was
  prNone` (the enum prints its STRING value, `none`) and `not
  asciiBytes.contains("\x1b[48;2;")` (printed with `\e`). Verification-Harness
  -Traps §17a; derive every needle from a transcript.
- **The `tui` lane's "cannot compile here" is a missing `-I`, not a missing
  toolchain.** `fatal error: tree_sitter/api.h: No such file or directory` is
  what three milestones (PLAT-21, PLAT-22) priced decisions against. The header
  is in the SAME store path `build/grammars/tui-link-flags.txt` already names —
  those flags carry `-L` and `-rpath` and no `-I`, by design, because the nix
  dev shell supplies it. Outside the dev shell:
  `export CPATH="$(sed 's/.*-L\([^ ]*\)\/lib.*/\1/' build/grammars/tui-link-flags.txt)/include"`
  — or just read the store path out of that file and append `/include`. With it,
  `just build-tui` succeeds (rc 0, 14,430,672 bytes). Measured 2026-09-16.
  **TWO VARIABLES, AND THEY ARE FOR DIFFERENT THINGS — do not conflate them.**
  `CPATH` is for anything that COMPILES `isonim_tui/syntax/treesitter_ffi.nim`
  (the TUI binary; `nim check` never needed it). The tui SUITES do not pull that
  module in and compile with `CPATH` unset — what they need is
  `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` (see the next entry), because they open a real recording.
  Run `test_cross_renderer_panes.nim` with `CPATH` and without
  `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` and you get 10 `[FAILED]` cases and a SIGSEGV, not 240
  checks. With `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`: `test_cross_renderer_panes.nim` 240 checks,
  `test_inline_annotations.nim` 32 checks, both rc 0.
- **`src/build-debug/bin/replay-server` cannot resolve `libmcr_emulator.so`**
  through an rpath or `CT_LD_LIBRARY_PATH` — `ldd` says `not found`. Anything
  that spawns it needs the emulator's build directory on `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`:
  `src/db-backend/target/debug/build/replay-server-*/out`. Without it the
  SHIPPED `codetracer-gpui` exits 1 with
  `DapStdioBackend: the debug adapter closed its output stream mid-header`, and
  `run-plat22-mutations.py` used to score 5 of 15 on a clean tree; it now
  refuses with that reason instead (PLAT-22 residue 13, closed by PLAT-23).
- **`codetracer-gpui` has NO EVENT LOOP, and its default invocation is a silent
  success.** `main.runOpen` calls `createWindow` → `show()` → `requestRepaint()`
  → `destroy()` with nothing between them, and the shim built without
  `--features gpui-backend` documents `show` as "just updates the internal
  state". So `codetracer-gpui <trace>` with no flags prints nothing, opens
  nothing and exits **0** in ~0.13 s. Use `--report-plan` to see anything at
  all. Do not read a green exit as a rendered window. (PLAT-23, 2026-09-16.)
- 2026-09-09 (SB-1): **`except CatchableError` / `except Exception` DO NOT
  CATCH A FOREIGN JS EXCEPTION on the `nim js` backend.** jsgen emits a
  `catch (e)` that tests `e.m_type` and RE-THROWS when the test fails, and
  anything thrown by `require('fs')`, `require('child_process')` or any other
  node API has no `m_type`. Only a BARE `except:` catches it, and
  `getCurrentExceptionMsg()` then returns the real message
  (`ENOENT: no such file or directory, scandir '…'`). Measured with a
  four-line program, not inferred.
  This is not academic: `viewmodel/host/desktop_electron.nim`'s `jsGuard`
  template had exactly the two typed arms and no bare one, so **every**
  `pkNotFound` / `pkAccessDenied` / `errorText()` in that file was dead code
  for its whole life, and the facade's "failures are values" contract was
  false on the instantiation the product actually ships. It surfaced only
  when the status bar's certificate indicator listed a directory most
  workspaces do not have. If you add a `try` around a host call in a
  `nim js` module, add a bare arm.
  AND NOTHING GUARDS THE FIX — checked, not assumed. With the bare arm
  removed and the frontend rebuilt, `status-bar-render-stability.spec.ts`'s
  `verify_clean_console_on_trace_open` still passes, because the certificate
  indicator's own store adapter (`certificate_indicator_source.nim`) wraps
  every facade call in a bare `except:` and swallows the re-thrown node
  exception before it can reach `window.onerror`. Measured in the running
  app: the indicator then reads *unverifiable* for an ordinary
  store-less workspace with an EMPTY console error bucket — a silently wrong
  verdict rather than a loud one. `desktop_electron.nim` is `{.error.}` off
  the JS backend and is imported by no test, so any real guard for it has to
  run inside Electron and assert the facade's OUTCOME (`fs.listDir` on a
  missing directory returns `pkNotFound` rather than throwing).
- 2026-09-09 (SB-1): a CAPTURE-FREE closure literal assigned inside a
  `std/unittest` `test` body is `jsgen.nim(1239) env is missing
  [AssertionDefect]` under `nim js` — the file compiles on the C backend and
  dies in the JS lane. Declare the proc at module level and assign the name
  instead. Two of the status-bar suites hit this; both now say so at the
  site.
- 2026-09-09 (SB-1): `ci/test/frontend-reachability.sh` matches BY NAME
  across all of `src/frontend`, so adding a symbol anywhere marks every
  unreached export of the same name as reached, and the count silently drops.
  (This remark used to continue "and the ratchet is an EQUALITY, so the lane
  demands you lower the ceiling". That half stopped being true on 2026-09-12:
  `--max` is a CEILING again, so a drop no longer reddens anything. The
  masking below is the part that still bites.) A drop is therefore not
  automatically progress: check whether a name went away or merely arrived
  somewhere else. SB-1 masked
  `viewmodel/identity/token.nim`'s `SignatureVerifier` (renamed the new type
  to `CertificateSignatureVerifier` to un-mask it) and still masks that
  file's `issuedAt`, which cannot be avoided — `issued_at` is the test
  certificate standard's own field name.
- 2026-09-09 (SB-1): a refresh driven by "after a render pass" is not a
  trigger in an idle Electron renderer, because an idle status bar does not
  render. `ui/certificate_indicator.nim` uses a `setInterval` now. If you need
  periodic revalidation of anything, do not hang it off a render.
  MEASURED, with `window.__ctStatusRenderStats()` (status.nim exposes it):
  across 60 s of untouched idle in edit mode the status bar performed TWO
  render passes, both inside one 10 s window and none in the other 50. Driving
  the same change (an edit to a tracked file deep in the tree, which no wired
  watch can see) the shipped timer moved the label after 15.1 s — one
  `RevalidateIntervalMs` exactly — and the render-hook design after 29.2 s,
  and only because a notification happened to draw. So it is not literally
  "never fires"; its latency is governed by unrelated render activity, which
  in a quiet window is unbounded.
- 2026-09-11 (M-LX-7): THERE ARE TWO extension→language maps in db-backend and
  they do not agree. `src/lang.rs::lang_from_context` is the one that decides
  which value loader the native replay worker uses, and it knows `.adb`/`.ads`,
  `.f90`/`.F90`, `.cr`, `.jl`, `.lean`, … `src/expr_loader.rs::
  ExprLoader::get_current_language` is a SEPARATE, older map that decides which
  tree-sitter grammar parses the file, and it knows far fewer. A language in the
  first but not the second computes `Lang::Unknown` there, misses `NODE_NAMES`,
  and `process_file`'s `if NODE_NAMES.contains_key(&lang)` guard then extracts
  ZERO expressions — the flow comes back with steps and no variables, which
  reads exactly like a broken extractor. Ada and Fortran are in that gap today.
  If you add a language, add it to BOTH.
- 2026-09-11 (M-LX-7): `log_expressions`'s documented "no tree-sitter grammar →
  load variable names from the trace data" fallback is reached for those
  languages and, for MCR traces, returns nothing: an Ada session stopped inside
  `Calculate_Sum` logs `no tree-sitter var list for line Position(30)` then
  `loaded 0 variables from trace data`. So the fallback is not the safety net it
  looks like on MCR traces.
- 2026-09-11 (M-LX-7): `add_mcr_source_breakpoint` (codetracer-native-backend)
  answers the DAP `setBreakpoints` request with `verified: true` even when LLDB
  resolved ZERO locations — it downgrades to a *pending* breakpoint and logs
  `created pending source breakpoint N`. `ct-dap-client`'s
  `validate_set_breakpoints_body` checks `verified` and nothing else — not the
  location count — so a breakpoint with zero locations passes that gate and the
  failure surfaces much later as an empty flow at `<unknown>:0`. Late resolution
  IS a designed outcome (`mcr_refresh_pending_breakpoints` re-checks and logs
  `pending source breakpoint N resolved with M location(s)`); the defect is that
  `verified: true` is asserted BEFORE that happens, so the client cannot tell
  "resolved" from "hoping". When a flow test reports `Stopped at <unknown>:0`,
  grep the worker log for `pending source breakpoint` before suspecting the
  extractor. Note also that this path forces BASENAME matching
  (`utils::lldb_breakpoint_file` reduces any path with a `/` to its file name).
- 2026-09-11 (M-LX-7): a hard-coded `breakpoint_line` in a flow test is a
  liability — `rust_mcr_streaming_flow_test` pointed at line 12 for two days
  after a ~390-line preamble was prepended to the fixture, and the symptom was
  indistinguishable from a variable-extraction defect. That test now derives the
  line from the source text and panics if the anchor is missing or ambiguous;
  prefer that shape for new ones. The other EIGHT language arms
  (c/cpp/ada/d/fortran/go/nim/pascal) still hard-code theirs and carry the same
  hazard.
- 2026-09-11 (M-LX-7): `flow_preloader.rs`'s flow walk pushes
  `new_location.line` into a `FlowViewUpdate` whose `location` is the WINDOW's
  file, and NEVER compares `new_location.path` against `self.location.path`. So
  a step that legitimately reports a line in another file — a Nim `=destroy`
  hook inlined from `system.nim`, an inlined header function in C++ — is filed
  as that line number of the file you asked about. This is how the nim MCR arm
  reports "steps at lines 394 and 398" of a 23-line fixture: those are real
  `system.nim` lines. Symptom to recognise: step lines beyond the end of the
  file, together with locals that belong to a different translation unit
  (`colontmpD_`, `nimErr_`, `FR_`, `gEnv`). `ExprLoader::get_source_line`
  already warns on the out-of-range row and nothing acts on it.
- 2026-09-13 (cow namespace indexes): `memwrites.tc` / `linehits.tc` are now
  built bottom-up in one pass (`CowNamespaceWriter::bulk_load`), not one
  `insert_and_commit` per key. **The failure mode here is NOT the one the Nim
  writer has**, and the difference is worth knowing before you go looking for
  it: Nim's `cow_btree` holds superseded pages "pending free" and never
  reclaims during a build, so a per-key Nim build blows the IMAGE up (1.18 GB
  at 100k keys) and bytes/key climbs. The Rust writer reclaims eagerly into the
  free list at every commit (see its module doc), so the Rust images stayed
  modest and bytes/key stayed FLAT — 89 bytes/key at every cardinality from
  10k to 200k. What grew instead was encode TIME, because
  `insert_and_commit` calls `collect_reachable` on the old AND new root and
  then does an O(pages) `contains` per page: 0.13 s at 10k keys, 2.1 s at 50k,
  10.6 s at 100k, 72.5 s at 200k. So an image-size check alone would have
  called the Rust side healthy. Measure the encode, not just the artefact.
- 2026-09-13: both encoders used to run a whole THROWAWAY per-key build over
  every key purely to learn how long the finished index would be, because
  descriptors carry `(payload_offset, len)` into a payload appended after the
  page-aligned index. That doubled the cost. A one-pass index's length is a
  pure function of the key count — `CowNamespaceWriter::bulk_load_image_len`.
  If you add another namespace with an appended payload, use that rather than
  building the tree twice. `bulk_load` refuses to return an image whose length
  disagrees with it, because an under-estimate misplaces every payload offset
  while still producing a structurally valid `NSB1` image.
- 2026-09-13: the same sizing-pass-plus-per-key shape survives in THREE test
  fixture helpers (`linehits_namespace.rs` `image_with_entries`,
  `mod.rs` `cow_linehits_image`, and the deliberate reference builders in the
  two encoders' test modules). Those are kept on purpose — they mean the
  readers are still exercised against a per-key-packed image, which is a real
  second packing, not dead weight. Do not "fix" them to `bulk_load` without
  leaving something that reads a per-key build.
- 2026-09-13: a bulk-built and a per-key-built namespace of the same keys are
  reader- and value-equivalent but **never byte-identical** (different commit
  id, the other root slot, and the per-key one keeps the pages its spine
  copies left behind). Any equivalence check between the two must be on decoded
  content. `codetracer-trace-format-nim/tests/check_nsb1_namespace.nim` is the
  canonical adjudicator for "a foreign writer emitted a real namespace" — it
  takes an image plus a `<key> <descriptor-hex>` / `!<absent-key>` manifest and
  judges it with the production Nim `loadCowBTree`/`lookup`.
- 2026-09-14: the bottom auto-hide pane for `Content.SearchResults` is titled
  **FIND IN FILES**, not "SEARCH RESULTS" — renamed in `ui/layout.nim`'s
  `standaloneAutoHidePanels` by 529c8dd1 ("feat: Add find in files component").
  The DOM did NOT follow: the component id is still `searchResultsComponent-0`
  and the CSS class is still `.search-results`, so a spec can hold a correct
  id-based locator and a dead title-based one at the same time. That is what
  happened — `bottomStripTab(page, "SEARCH RESULTS")` matched nothing, both

  asserting `toHaveCount(1)` for the same missing tab. The titles now live as

  never with a literal. Note the tab COUNT stayed 4 the whole time, so
  `DEFAULT_BOTTOM_TAB_COUNT` went on agreeing with the app while the titles
  beside it did not — a count is not evidence that the names are alive.

- 2026-09-14: A STALE RENDERER BUNDLE CAN SURVIVE A "SUCCESSFUL" SOURCE REVERT,
  and it looks exactly like a product bug. `just build-once` runs tup, and tup
  ABORTS ON THE FIRST FAILED JOB — so when the `db-backend` step dies (e.g. the
  `cargo build --locked` failure a stale `src/db-backend/Cargo.lock` produces),
  webpack never re-runs and `src/public/dist/frontend_bundle.js` keeps whatever
  the LAST complete build baked in. The Nim source can be clean, `git diff`
  empty, and the running Electron app still render the previous contents.
  Measured the hard way: a `.search-results` → `.search-results-MUTANT` class
  edit stayed live in the app across a source revert and two `build-once`
  invocations, and the resulting spec failures read convincingly as an
  order-dependent mount defect ("passes alone, fails when not first in the
  worker") because the passing runs predated the poisoned bundle.
  DO NOT verify bundle freshness by grepping the bundle for a class or id: the
  isonim DSL does not emit them as contiguous literals, so `grep -c` returns 0
  for a class that is demonstrably rendered. Both `fif-panel search-results`
  and the mutated form return 0. The only reliable check is to read the LIVE
  DOM (`ctPage.evaluate(() => el.outerHTML)`); the `#auto-hide-docked-bottom-content`
  dump is what finally showed the mutant class still present. Confirm
  `build-once` exited 0 before trusting any GUI e2e result.
- 2026-09-14: A COMMAND LINE CANNOT BE SPLIT WITHOUT KNOWING WHICH SHELL WROTE
  IT, and `viewmodels/evidence_call_vm.nim`'s `splitCommandLine` now says so in
  its signature (`dialect: CommandLineDialect = cldAuto`). `\` ESCAPES on a
  POSIX shell and SEPARATES on cmd.exe/PowerShell, and the two readings are
  mutually exclusive, not merely different — the proof is a pair of inputs each
  unambiguous on its own: `-o C:\out\ --diff` needs `\ ` to be a separator plus
  a delimiter or the two arguments glue into one, while `CT_LOG=/home/a\ b/log`
  needs `\ ` to be an escaped space or one argument splits into two. Any single
  context-free per-character rule trades one of these for the other, which is
  how the previous "escape only before a delimiter" rule was reached and why it
  left four residuals behind.
  The dialect is a RUNTIME parameter, deliberately not `when defined(windows)`:
  the producer is an agent session and the reader is whoever opens it later, so
  they need not be the same machine (the reported case is a Windows agent's
  dataset opened in a Linux CodeTracer), and both CI lanes being Linux means a
  `defined(windows)` arm would execute zero assertions while looking covered.
  `cldAuto` is what production passes because NOTHING RECORDS THE AGENT'S OS —
  not `AgentActivityMessageEntry`, not ACP's `tool_call.title`, not Agent
  Harbor's shell events. Giving the splitter a real dialect is therefore a
  change to the PRODUCERS, not to the recogniser. Auto decides per TOKEN, never
  per line, because one line legitimately carries both readings.
  THE TRAP INSIDE AUTO, worth the space because the obvious rule is wrong and
  its wrongness is silent. It is tempting to say a token carrying a literal
  backslash cannot be POSIX-escaped, since the POSIX reading would have to call
  it a pointless escape (`\o` in `C:\out`). That holds for QUOTES — `'` and `"`
  are not legal Windows path characters, so `\"` in such a token is a trailing
  separator meeting a closing quote. IT DOES NOT HOLD FOR WHITESPACE. A token
  can be Windows-shaped AND POSIX-escaped at once:
      C:\Program\ Files\out\r.json
  is what git-bash/WSL/any shell-quoting harness emits for the commonest spaced
  Windows path there is — single separators, only the space escaped — and its
  `\ ` is a genuine, necessary escape. Withdrawing the escape on the
  literal-backslash evidence alone truncates it to `C:\Program\` and orphans
  the rest: a wrong-but-plausible dataset path, the exact failure this module
  exists to prevent. It is locally IDENTICAL to `-o C:\out\ --diff`, which
  needs the opposite reading, so only LOOKAHEAD separates them: what follows
  the escaped whitespace is more path in one case and a flag in the other.
  `continuesTheSamePath` is that lookahead — a flag is the one argv token that
  can never be a path continuation. STATED LOSS: a trailing separator followed
  by a POSITIONAL argument (`-o C:\out\ report.json`) reads as one token. The
  two classes cannot both win; the rarer one loses, and `-o`/`--output` is
  followed by a flag or by end of line in every spelling this recogniser
  accepts.
  `cldWindows` is the SHELL reading, not MSVCRT's `CommandLineToArgvW`
  backslash-run rule. That rule turns `"C:\dir\"` into `C:\dir"` — a string no
  Windows path may contain, since `"` is not a legal path character. It is a
  known CRT trap rather than an intent, and reproducing it would name a
  wrong-but-plausible dataset, which is the whole failure class this module
  exists to prevent. cmd.exe and PowerShell both read it as `C:\dir\`.
  Also: `commandBaseName`'s `.exe` test folded case while the name comparison
  did not, so `ct.EXE` was accepted and `CT.EXE` refused. Half a fold is not a
  rule. The name test now lives in `namesCtBinary` and folds case there.
- 2026-09-14: A path containing an apostrophe (`C:\Users\O'Brien\review.json`)
  is mis-split by `splitCommandLine` in ALL THREE dialects: the `'` opens a
  single-quote region, and because nothing closes it the apostrophe is
  SWALLOWED and the remainder appended — `C:\Users\OBrien\review.json`, not a
  truncation. Pre-existing at HEAD and unchanged; fixing it needs a "`'` quotes
  only at a token boundary" rule that no reported case asks for.

- 2026-09-15: A `flake.lock` node can be INTERNALLY INCOHERENT — `lastModified`
  carrying one commit's date while `rev` names another — and nix rejects such an
  input outright ("mismatch in field 'lastModified'"), but ONLY when it actually
  FETCHES it. Two things stop that happening: a warm store reuses the cached
  entry without re-resolving, and a workspace `.envrc` overrides the
  metacraft-labs inputs with sibling paths so the github node is never fetched
  at all. Consequence: a hand-edited pin is green locally, green on warm
  runners, and fatal on a cold one — which reads as flake and sends the
  investigation at whatever the PR happened to touch. Two guards now exist and
  NEITHER subsumes the other: `ci/test/flake-lock-metadata-test.sh` asks GitHub
  about the 31 direct `github` inputs (needs a token, CI only, ~18 s), and
  `scripts/test-flake-lock-node-dates.sh` reads `git show -s --format=%ct` out
  of the sibling checkouts already on disk (42 nodes including the `type: git`
  ones and the transitive metacraft-labs ones, no network, ~0.5 s, in
  `just test`). A commit sha commits to its committer date, so where both can
  answer they cannot disagree.
- 2026-09-15: Three nodes in `flake.lock` carry a `rev` and NO `lastModified`
  and NO `narHash` — `codetracer-src`, `nim-shm-gset-src`,
  `reprobuild-test-adapters-src`, all `"flake": false` source inputs from
  reprobuild's closure, in that shape since cb3fff2e6. This is NOT rot: a node
  with no timestamp has nothing for its rev to disagree with, and nix verifies
  it by narHash at fetch time. Do not "repair" them by filling the fields in by
  hand — that is precisely how 4d15c1ea introduced the incoherence above. If a
  pin has to move without a full re-lock, dropping `lastModified`/`narHash` is
  the SAFE hand-edit; leaving a stale `lastModified` behind is the unsafe one.
- 2026-09-15: `repro.nim` DOES NOT COMPILE on `dev`, and has not for at least as
  long as dd971ae36. Measured two ways: in CI, `test-ui-tests (macos,
  aarch64-darwin)` -- which is the ONLY pull-request job that compiles this
  repo's own provider -- dies with `repro.nim(1247, 31) Error: type mismatch`,
  byte-identically on PR 754 and on dev@dd971ae36 (runs 34934002629 and
  34907519270); locally, `repro build --list-targets` dies at `repro.nim(1766,
  40) Error: type mismatch`, again byte-identically on a pristine dev worktree,
  because the `repro` on PATH is 0.1.3 while flake.nix pins reprobuild
  3a7ef0d7. Consequences worth knowing before you edit that file:
    * The compile ABORTS at 1247, so anything below it is never type-checked by
      anything, anywhere. An edit near the gate registrations (~1780) cannot be
      validated by CI or locally; do not believe a green PR about it.
    * `reprobuild-linux-smoke` / `-macos-smoke` do NOT read this repo's
      repro.nim -- `ci/reprobuild/linux-smoke.sh` builds in a `mktemp -d`
      scratch project. Their passing says nothing about it.
    * `windows-installer-build`, which runs `repro build windows-installer` in
      the repo root, is gated to `main`. So the first thing that would compile a
      repro.nim edit end-to-end is a RELEASE build.
  A gate added to `ci/lint/bash.sh` + the `just test` aggregate is fully
  verifiable today; the matching `repro.nim` test-gate registration is not, and
  was deliberately left out of the lock-node-dates gate for that reason.
- 2026-09-16 (merge of the bundled-source containment work with the
  hot-reload version work): **`expr_loader::bundled_source_path` now takes a
  `generation`, and the containment wrapper takes it too.** The two changes
  landed on different branches and are not independent: the guard has to be
  applied to the VERSIONED destination that will really be written or read,
  not to a generation-0 stand-in for it, or a second-version file would be
  reached by a path nothing checked. So:
  * `bundled_source_path(root, source_path, generation)` is still the RAW,
    UNGUARDED mapping. It is public only so a test can name the raw key; every
    raw call site in this repo is inside a `#[cfg(test)]` module, and
    `cargo check --all-targets` is what keeps that true.
  * `contained_bundled_source_path(root, source_path, generation)` is the form
    every production caller uses — both srcviews extractors (native and VFS)
    and `get_source_line_v2`'s bundled branch.
  * Generation 0 is the historical destination byte for byte, so
    `resolve_bundled_source` — which answers a DAP `source` request and has no
    version ordinal to key on — passes 0 and behaves exactly as before.
  The GDH-M7 falsifier arm in `get_source_line_v2` goes through the contained
  candidate too. Routing it around the guard would have made the arm test a
  code path the product does not ship.
- 2026-09-17 (event-log / point-list store plumbing): **A CACHED FIXTURE UNDER
  `test-logs/tui-fixtures/` GOES STALE SILENTLY WHEN THE TRACE FORMAT CHANGES,
  AND THE SYMPTOM IS A HANG RATHER THAN AN ERROR.** The cache key
  (`fixture_provider.fixtureCacheRoot` + a digest) is computed over the
  PROGRAM'S OWN SOURCES plus a hand-maintained `FixtureCacheSchema` constant
  (`fixture_provider.fixtureKey`), so nothing in it changes when a trace-format
  writer or reader does — and adoption only checks that a `.ct` file EXISTS
  (`isUsableTraceDir`), never that it opens. `test_fixture_corpus.nim`'s header
  claims a fixture is re-recorded "whenever the recorder changes"; that half is
  not implemented.

  **THE CULPRIT COMMIT, corrected 2026-09-17 after verification.** This entry
  first named `ac413d7` in `codetracer-trace-format` ("assemble events from the
  split streams, and retire events.log"). That attribution is WRONG and the
  breakage runs the other way: ac413d7 made the reader dispatch on the ABSENCE
  of `events.log` and kept both legacy paths, so a container that HAS one still
  opens. What it broke is newly-written legacy bundles (`cargo test --test
  ctfs_split_only_full_db_test` fails on `events.log bundle must carry
  events.log`).

  The commit that actually strands cached containers is **`b891a0f` in
  `codetracer-trace-format-nim`** ("funcs.dat and types.dat carry the spec's
  record shape", 2026-09-15 16:34 — 27 minutes after ac413d7, which is how the
  two got conflated). It moved those two interning tables from bare bytes to
  varint-length-prefixed records in writer and reader together, so any
  same-revision build is self-consistent and only PRE-EXISTING containers are
  misread. The signature is
  `funcs.dat record is truncated: declares a NNN-byte name with only N bytes
  left` — measured on four of the five committed request-panel fixtures.

  **THE SYMPTOM IS A HANG RATHER THAN AN ERROR** — verified by driving a real
  `replay-server dap-server --stdio`. `CTFSTraceReader::open` fails, the failure
  is logged at `info!` (`dap_server.rs:471`, invisible without `RUST_LOG`),
  `setup` falls through to the rr replay-worker path (`dap_server.rs:641`),
  the worker start fails `NotFound` — and `launch` has ALREADY answered
  `success: true` unconditionally at `dap_server.rs:2643`, because setup runs
  asynchronously on `task_thread`. The failure reaches only `error!` at
  `dap_server.rs:3300`/`3459`; no DAP message is ever emitted and NO `stopped`
  event arrives, so the client blocks for ever in `waitForEvent` at 0% CPU.
  `dap_server.rs:2546-2553` documents this hazard class in its own words.

  Cold-cache suites were never affected: the ~30 `*_dap_test.rs` flows and the
  Playwright GUI suites re-record every run. The exposure is containers that
  PREDATE b891a0f — committed `tests/fixtures/*.ct` (refreshed only by hand-run
  `regenerate*.sh`) and any warm fixture cache. The worst case is
  `src/tests/gui/tests/noir-space-ship/noir_space_ship_test.nim`, which adopts
  any directory under `~/.local/share/codetracer/` whose NAME contains
  `noir_space_ship`, with no content check at all.

  The fix is `rm -rf test-logs/tui-fixtures/<name>-*` and let the corpus
  re-record. If a real-stack suite hangs at `openLive`/`openHarness` with the
  child idle, suspect this before anything else.
- 2026-09-17: **THREE BINARIES MUST AGREE ABOUT `meta.dat`, AND TWO OF THEM ARE
  NOT REBUILT BY `cargo build`.** `ct record` fails with
  `meta.dat: unsupported version N` when the RECORDER writes a newer version
  than the reader in the Nim half. The reader lives in
  `src/ct/trace/ctfs_sources.nim` (`SupportedMetaDatVersion`) and is compiled
  into BOTH `src/build-debug/bin/ct` AND `src/build-debug/bin/db-backend-record`
  — `ct record` shells out to the second, so rebuilding only `ct` leaves the old
  message coming from a binary whose name never appears in the error. `tup upd`
  in `src/build-debug` currently dies on
  `tup error: Unable to use files from another variant (/build-debug) in this
  variant (/build-release)` after refreshing `replay-server`, so the Nim
  binaries have to be built by hand with the flags in `src/Tuprules.tup`
  (`NIM_COMMON_FLAGS` + `NIM_REPO_PATH_FLAGS` + `CODETRACER_FLAGS`; `ct` also
  needs `-d:ctEntrypoint`, `db_backend_record.nim` does not).

- `index/startup.nim`'s `init(dataArg: var ServerData, ...)` does `var data =
  dataArg` and its comment claims "on the JS backend, ServerData is a reference
  type, so mutations propagate back". IT DOES NOT. `ServerData` is an `object`,
  and Nim's JS backend emits `nimCopy` for that assignment (see
  `HEX3Aenv_*.data0 = nimCopy(...)` in `src/build-debug/index.js`), so every
  write to `data` inside `init` lands in a COPY. The one that bites is the edit
  branch's `data.workspaceFolder = folder`: after `ct edit <folder>` the global
  `data.workspaceFolder` is still `nil`, so anything reading it — launch
  configs (`getLaunchConfigsForWorkspace`, `onRecordWithLaunchConfig`),
  `loadTrace`'s filesystem categorisation, `pathContentRootFor` — behaves as if
  no folder were open. The Welcome-screen path is unaffected because
  `traces.nim`'s `onInitEditMode` assigns the global directly, which is why the
  same folder behaves differently depending on how it was opened. Measured
  2026-09-16 while building the HCR launch path, which works around it by
  reading `data.startOptions.folder` (never copied) in edit mode.
  `loadTrace` in `traces.nim` carries the identical comment and the identical
  assumption.

- `calls.dat` stores call arguments ONE ENTRY PER ARGUMENT, each with its own
  interned name — `CallStreamRecord.args: Vec<CallArg>` where
  `CallArg { varname_id, value }` and `value` is streaming CBOR. The on-disk
  framing is `args_count`, then per argument `varint varname_id, varint
  value_len, value bytes`; the authority for that is the encoder/decoder pair
  and module doc in `codetracer-trace-format`'s
  `codetracer_trace_writer/src/call_stream.rs` (`CallStreamRecord::{encode,
  decode}`). NOTE (verified 2026-09-17): the SPEC has not caught up —
  `codetracer-trace-format-spec/trace-events.md` §"Call Stream Records
  (`calls.dat`)" on `origin/dev` still describes `args` as a single
  "streaming CBOR (or empty)" field, and the string `varname_id` appears
  nowhere in that repo. Do not cite the spec for this layout; cite the code.
  It did NOT always: before `codetracer-trace-format` a797cb8 the writer
  collapsed the whole argument vector into ONE entry under a synthetic
  `varname_id` of 0, and `ctfs_trace_reader/call_stream_source.rs` decoded that
  blob as a `Vec<FullValueRecord>`. Against the Nim writer, which always filled
  the framing per argument, that kept only the FIRST argument and threw away
  every name.
  The adaptation to `FullValueRecord` is the INTERNING ID, not a string lookup:
  `FullValueRecord.variable_id` indexes the same `varnames.dat` table
  `varname_id` does, and text is resolved much later by `Db::variable_name`. So
  `variable_id: VariableId(arg.varname_id as usize)` is the whole of it — the
  identical construction `mod.rs`'s `open_new_format_rust` /
  `open_new_format_nim` already perform on `reader.call_arg(key, i)`. Do not
  invent a second mechanism, and do not answer a CBOR decode failure with an
  empty arg list: an argument dropped there is indistinguishable in the
  Variables view from a call that captured nothing, which is exactly the loss
  a797cb8 exists to end. `ctfs_trace_reader::decode_interned_cbor_value` is the
  one decoder both `values.dat` step values and `calls.dat` args go through; its
  failure answer is a visible `ValueRecord::Raw` placeholder under the real name.

- TWO copies of the call-arg decode are live, and the tests do not cover them
  equally (measured 2026-09-17 by planting a `panic!` in `decode_args` and
  seeing which tests reddened):
    * `call_stream_source::decode_args` serves the SEEKABLE call tree —
      `TraceReader::seekable_call`, which `Calltrace::new` uses, plus
      `follow_stream_source` (live/follow) and `from_bytes` →
      `open_new_format_rust` (the browser/WASM replay path).
    * `open_new_format_nim`'s INLINE copy (this file, in the `call_arg(key, i)`
      loop) populates the materialized `db.calls`, which is what
      `TraceReader::call`/`call_count` return on the default desktop open —
      `open_new_format` dispatches to the Nim reader whenever the `nim-reader`
      feature is on, and it is on by default.
  Consequence for anyone trusting the integration suite here:
  `tests/ctfs_call_args_roundtrip_test.rs` reads `reader.call(key)`, so it
  exercises the MATERIALIZED copy and NOT `decode_args`. A `panic!` planted in
  `decode_args` leaves that test green. The seekable copy's only coverage today
  is the unit tests in `call_stream_source.rs`. If you change one copy, change
  or re-measure the other; do not read a green roundtrip test as evidence about
  the seekable path.

- The Event Log has TWO backend routes for the SAME recorded events, and they
  are not interchangeable (measured 2026-09-17 against a real `replay-server`
  on `noir_space_ship`, 70 events):
    * `ct/event-load` — `Handler::event_load` (`src/db-backend/src/dap_handler.rs`)
      reads only `start`/`count` from the arguments and, when BOTH are absent or
      zero, answers a fixed 20-row PREFIX. It applies no kind filter and no
      search. It also has a side effect no other event-log route has (the only
      other caller of it in the tree is `Handler::correlation_markers`, which
      is a different feature): `ensure_events_loaded`
      populates the `EventDb` that the other route pages over, so a
      `ct/update-table` on a session that has never seen a `ct/event-load`
      answers an empty table.
    * `ct/update-table` — `EventDb::update_table` (`src/db-backend/src/event_db.rs`)
      answers the window the pane asked for, applying `selectedKinds`, the
      substring search and `start`/`length` SERVER-SIDE, and returns the real
      `recordsTotal`/`recordsFiltered` the DataTables Scroller virtualises its
      height from. This is why the route cannot be collapsed into the first one:
      a client-side re-filter of a 20-row prefix is not the same pane.
  `EventLogVM`'s auto-load effect sends `ct/event-load` with
  `{page,pageSize,searchQuery,...}` — NONE of which that handler reads — so on
  the desktop it is in effect "give me the first 20". Both routes write
  `ReplayDataStore.eventLog.rows`; `EventLogWindowSource` is the precedence rule
  that stops the prefix from replacing the page the user is scrolling.

- `TableRow` and `ProgramEvent` are each TWO unrelated Nim types, not one.
  `common/common_types/codetracer_features/events.nim` is `include`d (not
  imported) into `common/types.nim`, which binds `langstring = string`, and into
  `frontend/types.nim`, which binds `langstring = cstring`. So a decoder in
  `viewmodel/store/` cannot be typed against either without picking a host. The
  shared decoders (`eventLogRowFromTableRow`, `eventLogRowFromProgramEvent`) are
  GENERIC over the row for exactly this reason — one source text, instantiated
  by whichever host holds the value — and that is also why a test cannot import
  the product's `TableRow` and has to pass a field-compatible carrier instead.

- `EventDb::refresh_global` SORTS `global_table` by `(step_id, …)`, while
  `ct/event-load` answers `cached_events` in the recorder's own order. The sort
  is stable, and on `noir_space_ship` the two orders agree row for row (asserted
  in `src/frontend/tui/tests/test_event_log_table_route.nim`). They are not
  guaranteed to: a recording whose `direct_location_rr_ticks` is not
  monotonically non-decreasing in recording order would make
  `EventLogRow.eventIndex` mean a position in a different sequence depending on
  which route produced the row. That suite is where it will be caught.

- `ProgramEvent.source_digest` and `.source_generation` are ALWAYS EMPTY on
  both event-log routes today. The two places a recorded event is built —
  `Db::to_program_event` (`src/db-backend/src/db.rs`) for every CTFS/db-backed
  trace, and the `to_program_event` beside `Handler::event_load`
  (`src/db-backend/src/dap_handler.rs`) for the rr path — write
  `source_digest: String::new()` and `source_generation: 0` as literals, and
  `TableRow::new` copies whatever the event carries, so `ct/update-table`
  inherits the same. Measured on the wire as well: `noir_space_ship`, `calc`
  and `wide_state` each report one distinct value for each field (`""` and
  `0`). Consequence for testing: no recording in this workspace can falsify a
  decoder that drops either field, so a mutation on them SURVIVES by
  construction rather than for want of a fixture.

- PLAT-25's edit algebra lives in `src/frontend/viewmodel/editor/change_set.nim`
  and `.../transaction.nim`. **The `before` flag of the rebase double mapping is
  not a parameter of anything exported**: `mapOver` is private to
  `change_set.nim` with exactly two call sites, both inside `rebase`, and
  `test_editor_change_algebra.nim` scans the editor directory to assert that
  (unexported, declared once, called twice, and no other module spelling
  `before: bool`). If you need to move a change set over another — PLAT-26's
  `changeByRange`, PLAT-32's `mapEvent`, PLAT-33's `receiveUpdates` /
  `rebaseUpdates` — call `rebase(a, b)` and read the arm you want. Hand-writing
  a copy makes that scan go red, which is the point.

- Two things about that module that will surprise you if you read CodeMirror
  first. (1) The `this.empty ? other` shortcut in `compose`/`map` is
  deliberately NOT ported: it makes `LAW-A3` (`A ∘ id == A` as a VALUE) a test
  of an `if`. (2) Composition is associative as a MAPPING and not as a value —
  `(A∘B)∘C` and `A∘(B∘C)` can split one changed run differently, 933 times in
  200,000 generated triples. CodeMirror's own associativity test compares
  applied documents and never the values. Use `sameMapping`, not `==`, when you
  mean "the same edit".

- Nim's structural `==` REFUSES a `case` object outright ("parallel 'fields'
  iterator does not work for 'case' objects"). Every object variant in this
  tree that is compared needs a hand-written `==`; `change_set.nim` has three.

- `std/random` is NOT usable in a suite that must draw the same population on
  the C and JS backends: its state is two `uint64`, which `nim js` emulates.
  `viewmodel/tests/generators/change_generator.nim` carries a 32-bit xorshift
  for that reason, and pins its first draws in a case that runs on both lanes.

- A generator that segments a corpus document per draw is the difference
  between a 7-second suite and a 6-minute one. `change_generator` hoists every
  corpus document's cluster boundaries into a module-level `let`
  (`CorpusBoundaries`); before that the `vm-unit-js` run of
  `test_editor_change_algebra.nim` took 5 m 55 s, after it 7.7 s, same output.

- A cluster-aligned WINDOW of a corpus document is not automatically of that
  document's class: the first window cut from `c7-illformed-long` contained no
  ill-formed byte at all. `change_generator.classWitness` searches forward for a
  window that witnesses its class, and the suite asserts every one does. If you
  cut windows out of that corpus for anything else, do the same.

- A `staticRead` source scan is only as WIDE as its subject list, and
  `staticRead` takes a string literal — so the list is frozen and a NEW file in
  the scanned directory is invisible. Measured: planting
  `viewmodel/editor/rebase_copy.nim` with the exact forbidden spelling left
  `test_editor_change_algebra.nim` at 130/130 green. The scan now also
  enumerates the directory with a compile-time `walkDir` (which works on the JS
  backend too, because it runs in the VM) and asserts the `.nim` set equals the
  set it reads. Copy that pattern for any "no other module does X" check.

- `git checkout -- <path>` on a file that was only `git add -N`'d TRUNCATES IT
  TO ZERO BYTES: intent-to-add records the path with an empty blob, and the
  checkout faithfully restores it. `git status --porcelain` shows such a file as
  `" A"`; check `git cat-file -s :<path>` before reaching for a checkout. If you
  lose one this way and it is a subject of a `staticRead` source scan, every
  compiled test binary carries a verbatim copy — locate the file's first bytes
  in the binary and grow the slice until its SHA-256 matches the recorded
  control digest. That is how `change_set.nim` was recovered on 2026-09-18.

- THE LINE INDEX AND THE GRAPHEME SEGMENTER DISAGREE ON A CRLF LINE.
  `viewmodel/editor/text_store.nim` is `\n`-delimited (`lineCount` is
  `newlineCount + 1`, `lineLen` excludes only the terminating `\n`), so the END
  of a line ending `\r\n` is the offset BETWEEN the CR and the LF — and UAX #29
  GB3 keeps CR LF together as ONE grapheme cluster, so that offset is INSIDE a
  cluster. Any "go to end of line" or "land at column C of line L" built on the
  store must clamp to the last cluster boundary at or below the answer;
  `selection_ops.boundaryAtOrBefore` is that clamp. Found by a sweep over the
  corpus (`c6-terminators-short`), not by a law — no property in
  `Editor-Model-Conformance-Suite.md` §3.2 asks this question.

- `offsetAtColumn(line, columnAt(line, b))` is NOT `b` for every cluster
  boundary `b`, and it never will be: a combining mark is zero cells wide, so
  the boundary before it and the boundary after it are the SAME column and a
  column cannot name both. Measured over the 18-document corpus: 158,575 of
  165,244 boundaries round-trip in offset space; 6,270 are displaced forward by
  a zero-width cluster and 399 backward by class 7's ill-formed tails (where the
  segmenter stops short of the line end and `clusterBoundariesOf` appends it).
  State the round trip in COLUMN space — the offset a column resolves to is AT
  that column — which is exact and is what a goal column actually needs.

- A PUBLISHED KILLING MUTATION CAN BE UNOBSERVABLE UNDER THE INVARIANT THE CODE
  CHOSE. `LAW-S1`'s killer is "drop the merge step for touching ranges"; under
  CodeMirror's rule (merge a touching range only when it is empty) the mutated
  output is still ordered, still non-overlapping, still non-empty — so the arm
  SURVIVES and reads as "harmless". `viewmodel/editor/selection.nim` therefore
  merges on touch as well as on overlap and the invariant is strict separation.
  Read every published killer against the implementation before trusting the
  law: the question is "which assertion goes red", and if the answer is "none",
  repair the ASSERTION or the DESIGN, never the killer.
  (Verification-Harness-Traps §36.)

- A MUTATION HARNESS IS NOT SAFE UNDER A SHARED CHECKOUT — AND "ANOTHER SESSION
  IS WRITING" IS EASY TO CONCLUDE WRONGLY. Every `run-plat*-mutations.py` writes
  a MUTATED file to disk and restores it from memory afterwards, so a concurrent
  writer can be clobbered by the restore and a concurrent reader can compile a
  mutated subject. Verify the control digests BEFORE and AFTER (`sha256sum -c`),
  twice and separated in time — a single clean reading of a tree under
  concurrent mutation is indistinguishable from a clean tree.
  **But attribute transient files before raising an alarm.** On 2026-09-18
  during PLAT-26 the implementation agent saw `src/frontend/viewmodel/
  zz_*_probe.nim` files appear and vanish within seconds and reported a foreign
  session; they were the VERIFICATION agent's throwaway probes, created and
  `rm`'d inside a single `bash -c`, and the one transient digest disagreement
  was that same agent applying an arm by hand and restoring it. Two agents
  working the same milestone look exactly like an intruder. Check with the
  other agent before concluding the tree is contended.
  Probes were deliberately placed OUTSIDE `viewmodel/editor/` so the
  compile-time `walkDir` enumeration the source scans assert over was never
  perturbed — do the same.

- `pgrep -f <name>` MATCHES YOUR OWN WAIT LOOP. `until ! pgrep -f
  run-plat26-selection; do sleep 30; done` never exits, because the shell
  running it has that string in its own command line — so the wait reports
  "still running" forever and any monitor armed on it times out instead of
  firing. Match on the interpreter plus the script (`pgrep -f "python3.*
  run-plat26"`), or exclude your own pid.

- "PRIVATE FIELDS + ONE NORMALISING CONSTRUCTOR" IS NOT UNREPRESENTABILITY IN
  NIM. `viewmodel/editor/selection.nim`'s `EditorSelection` has two private
  fields and only `editorSelection()` builds one, which does stop a caller
  ASSEMBLING a bad range sequence. It does not stop the type's ZERO VALUE:
  `var s: EditorSelection`, `default(EditorSelection)` and `reset(s)` each give
  an empty range sequence, and `isNormalised` returns false for it. That is the
  one un-normalised inhabitant. `{.requiresInit.}` closes `var s:` only —
  `default` and `reset` still compile — so it buys a third of the claim.
  If you write "unrepresentable" about a value object, test those three first;
  the honest claim is "normalisation cannot be skipped when building one".

- MERGING TOUCHING SELECTION RANGES IS NOT FREE, THOUGH IT WAS FIRST RECORDED
  AS FREE. `selection.nim` merges ranges that touch (see the §36 entry above).
  The stated cost was "identical rendering, identical document under every
  operation". The second half is FALSE: every motion and `delete` agree, but an
  INSERT does not — over `"abcdefghij"`, inserting `"X"` across `[0,3)` and
  `[3,6)` as two ranges gives `"XXghij"`, across the merged `[0,6)` it gives
  `"Xghij"`. One insertion instead of two, which is what multi-cursor is for.
  It is reachable from the twelve primitives, not just from a constructor: from
  `[0,2)` and `[4,6)`, two `extend-right` steps give `[0,4)` and `[4,8)`, which
  abut and merge — so extending two selections into contact silently drops a
  cursor. PLAT-30 is to revisit it. Generally: "moving the design costs
  nothing" is a claim that needs the operations enumerated and checked, because
  the one that differs will be the one the feature exists for.

- A DISPLAY COORDINATE `(row, column)` CANNOT DISTINGUISH TWO LOGICAL POSITIONS
  AT ONE COLUMN, AND THE CORPUS HAS 7,352 OF THEM. A zero-width cluster — a C0
  control, a lone combining mark, a stray ZWJ — occupies no cell, so it shares
  its column with whatever follows. `viewmodel/editor/wrap.nim`'s round trip is
  therefore NOT the identity at every position; it is the identity at every
  position that has a column of its own, and `columnCanonical` names the rest.
  33,465 cluster boundaries across PLAT-27's 90-cell population are in the
  "rest". If you are about to write "the mapping is a bijection", say WHICH TWO
  SETS, and assert their cardinalities — `boundaries + interior cells ==
  canonical display positions` is arithmetic and needs neither mapping.

- SCREEN-LINE `$` ON A WRAPPED ROW IS NOT THE ROW'S WIDTH. The position one past
  the last cell of a row that CONTINUES is the same point as column 0 of the
  next row, so returning it makes `g$` report the following row. Vim lands `g$`
  ON the last character for this reason. `wrap.lastColumnOf` answers the width
  only on the last row of a logical line. Found by running the twelve-cell
  motion sweep, not by reasoning: nine of twelve cells went red.

- A CORPUS CROSS PRODUCT IS A POPULATION AND NEEDS THE SAME HISTOGRAM A
  GENERATOR DOES. `Editor-Model-Conformance-Suite.md` §6 declares "18 documents
  x {20,40,80,120}", which reads like full coverage. Measured: 35 of those 72
  cells have NO wrapped line, and six documents never wrap at any declared
  column because their widest line is under 20 cells. Every coordinate law was
  trivially true for a third of the corpus. A declared cross product is not
  self-evidently a population — classify the cells and assert the counts as
  EQUALITIES, exactly as for a random generator (Verification-Harness-Traps
  §34).

- `isonim-tui`'s `wrapLineToRows` TREATS A TAB AS ZERO CELLS, because
  `clusterDisplayWidth` does — a tab is a control character, not a glyph. When
  you diff a model's wrapping against the widget's, run the model at
  `tabSize = 0` or the two disagree on every tab-bearing document for a reason
  that has nothing to do with wrapping. Four of the eighteen corpus documents
  carry a tab: c6-terminators-short, c8-tabs-short, c8-tabs-long,
  c9-ascii-control-short. The widget can be driven WITHOUT a renderer —
  `TextAreaWidget(lines: …, width: …, softWrap: true)` is enough for
  `allDisplayRows` — and it compiles and runs on both the C and JS backends.

- DO NOT RUN A MUTATION HARNESS'S `--record-control-hashes` WHILE THE HARNESS
  ITSELF IS RUNNING. It records whichever bytes are on disk, and mid-arm those
  are the MUTATED bytes: one of PLAT-27's four digests was recorded from a
  mutated `wrap.nim` and looked like a legitimate control until it was compared
  with the value from before the run. The harness restores the file when the
  arm ends, so the corruption is invisible a minute later. Record digests only
  against a tree you have just seen green.

- A MUTATION ARM ON A COORDINATE HAS TO MOVE THE VALUE **OUT OF THE CLUSTER'S
  OWN SPAN**. PLAT-27's first `LAW-C1` arm rounded a mid-cluster byte to the
  cluster's SECOND cell — still inside the cluster, so the return leg resolved
  back to the same cluster and the round trip was unchanged. It reported
  MISDIRECTED. The arm that lands adds one cell PER BYTE, which runs a
  three-byte two-cell ideograph off its own end.

- A MUTATION HARNESS MAKES THE WHOLE TREE UNTRUSTWORTHY WHILE IT RUNS, NOT JUST
  ITS OWN BUILD. It writes a mutated subject to disk, compiles, and restores —
  so ANY other compile started in that window reads mutated sources. A `nim js`
  verification run of PLAT-27's suites launched beside
  `run-plat27-coordinate-mutations.py` came back with 28 red cases (all of
  `LAW-C6` and all of `FUZZ-6`) — which is precisely arm `M6`'s kill set, not a
  JS-backend divergence. The harness's own control digests protect the HARNESS;
  they say nothing to a second reader. Either wait for it, or run your check
  before you start it. `--nimcache:` to a private directory does NOT help: the
  contamination is in the SOURCE, not the cache.

- AND THE CONTAMINATION OUTLIVES THE RUN, IN THE NIMCACHE. The bullet above is
  right that a private `--nimcache:` does not protect a reader DURING the run;
  it is wrong to conclude the cache never matters. After
  `run-plat27-coordinate-mutations.py` has finished and restored every subject
  byte-for-byte, `~/.cache/nim/test_editor_wrap_laws_d` can still hold object
  code built from a mutated (or simply older) `wrap.nim`, and `nim c -r`
  happily links it. Measured on 2026-09-18: with all four control digests
  verifying OK against a clean tree, the two suites reported 10 red cases —
  nine `dispRowEnd` motion cells and the `goalColumn` example, which are
  exactly the two defects `lastColumnOf` and `landingOf` were written to fix.
  A probe built from the same sources into a DIFFERENT nimcache directory was
  correct, and `rm -rf ~/.cache/nim/test_editor_wrap_{laws,examples}_d`
  followed by the identical command was green at 276/0 and 30/0. So: a red run
  whose failures look like a mutation you have already fixed is a stale cache
  until proven otherwise. Clear the suite's nimcache before believing a red,
  and before believing a green you intend to publish a number from.

- A PER-FILE `<module>.nim.cfg` IS THE WAY TO GIVE ONE SUITE A COMPILER FLAG
  THE LANE SUPPLIES BUT A DIRECT `nim c -r` DOES NOT. Every `vm-*` lane passes
  `--path:src/frontend/viewmodel` (`ci/lib/test-lane-files.sh`), because
  `codetracer_embed` is imported by bare module name; `ci/test/editor-model-
  case-floor.sh` runs `nim c -r` itself and carries no lane flags, so a suite
  that imports `frontend/view_vocabulary/editor_surface.nim` compiles in the
  lane and not in the gate. `test_editor_decoration_examples.nim.cfg` carries
  the one switch, spelled `--path:"$config/../.."` so it resolves relative to
  the cfg rather than to the working directory. Nim reads `<file>.nim.cfg` for
  the main module only — `src/frontend/lsp.nim.cfg` is the same mechanism — and
  a `nim.cfg` in `viewmodel/tests/unit/` would change every suite in the
  directory at once. Verified 2026-09-18 on the C, JS and wasm32 backends.

- `viewmodel/editor/` NOW HAS THIRTEEN MODULES AND *THREE* SUITES SCAN IT BY
  COMPILE-TIME `walkDir`. Adding a module to that directory turns
  `test_editor_change_algebra.nim`, `test_editor_wrap_laws.nim` and
  `test_editor_decoration_laws.nim` red BY NAME on the next run, before the new
  module's own suite exists. That is the mechanism working (Verification-
  Harness-Traps §35) and the repair is one entry in each list plus each file's
  `ExpectedAssertions`. Re-read the CLAIM as well as the list: PLAT-27's scan
  said "no module other than `wrap.nim` mentions `WrapSettings`", which stopped
  being the right claim when `inlay.nim` arrived taking the settings as a
  PARAMETER — the repair was to split projection modules from state modules and
  assert both halves, not to add a name.

- AN INLINE WIDGET IS A `ClusterCell` OF ZERO BYTES AND W CELLS, and that is why
  `wrap.nim` knows nothing about decorations. `inlay.inlayLineMetrics` injects
  them and `wrap.wrapCacheOfMetrics` takes the metrics; `wrapLine`, `toDisplay`,
  `toLogical` and the twelve display motions are untouched. Two things to know
  before editing either: (1) the columns after a widget must be RE-DERIVED with
  `cellsOf`, never shifted by W, because a tab re-grids — at `tabSize = 4` a
  3-cell widget moves a following tab's END by 4; (2) the WRAP DECISION in
  `wrapLine` accumulates each cluster's `cells` and never consults its
  `column`, so zeroing a widget's column advance does NOT stop a line wrapping
  — `wrapLine` does read `column`, at `wrap.nim:354`, but only to label a new
  row's `startColumn` once the break has already been decided. Zeroing the
  advance therefore moves where rows say they start, not whether they exist.
  `updateWrapCache` refuses a
  decorated cache outright, because the splice recomputes a touched line's
  metrics from its TEXT.

- `emcc` IS IN THE DEV SHELL, SO THE WASM32 LANE CAN BE VERIFIED LOCALLY —
  WHICH THREE EARLIER MILESTONES RECORDED AS IMPOSSIBLE. Measured 2026-09-18:
  `emscripten-4.0.12-git`, on `PATH` inside
  `nix develop --override-input reprobuild path:../reprobuild`. The earlier
  "emcc is absent" reports came from a PARTIALLY-RESOLVED shell — the check was
  run somewhere the flake's inputs had not finished resolving, and the absence
  was then carried forward as a property of the repo. `vm-unit-wasm` is
  `vm-unit` minus six files and its build command is in
  `ci/lib/run-nim-test-lane.sh`'s `wasm` branch: `nim c --cpu:wasm32 --os:linux
  -d:emscripten --cc:clang --clang.exe:emcc --mm:orc`, run under node. If a
  bare `node` is not on PATH in your shell, use the `nodejs-22.22.2` store path
  from the repo's direnv profile.

  **The rule this replaces a wrong bullet with: a backend you did not run is
  not evidence, in EITHER direction.** The bullet that stood here claimed a
  wasm32-only miscompilation in `wrap.wrapCacheOfMetrics` — that deriving rows
  from the `seq[LineMetrics]` parameter rather than from `result.metrics` read
  widget clusters as zero cells, and that eleven cases were red on that target.
  Running the lane disproved every part of it: the wasm32 suites are 129/1,266
  and 46/327 with ZERO failures, identical to C and JS; reverting to the
  pre-fix spelling leaves the laws suite green on wasm32; a direct probe reads
  the widget cluster as TEN cells with `rowsInLine(0) == 2`; and `--mm:orc` and
  `--mm:arc` agree at debug, `-d:release` and `-d:danger`. `result.metrics[i]`
  is kept as a neutral refactor and carries no defect claim.

  Practically: the wasm lane is CHEAP now, so run it before publishing a "green
  on both backends" number — and if you find it genuinely disagreeing with C
  and JS, that is a real finding worth this much space. Do not write one you
  have not seen.

- A MUTATION HARNESS MAKES EVERY READ OF THE TREE UNTRUSTWORTHY, NOT JUST EVERY
  COMPILE. The bullets above say this about builds; it is equally true of
  `git diff`, `git status`, an editor and a reviewer. A `git diff` taken while
  `run-plat27-coordinate-mutations.py` was running showed arm M12's edit to
  `wrapLine` as though it were a local change. Check quiescence with
  `fuser <subject files>` — no output and exit 1 means nobody holds them — and
  re-read after.

- STASHING THE TREE WHILE A MUTATION HARNESS IS RUNNING KILLS ITS CONTROL RUN,
  AND IT LOOKS LIKE A BROKEN HARNESS. The bullet above says a mutation run makes
  every READ of the tree untrustworthy; this is the other direction and it cost
  a forty-minute run. `git stash -u` during PLAT-30's harness took the subjects
  out from under the control compile, the harness reported `CONTROL IS NOT
  GREEN: rc=1 failed=[]` — an empty failure list, because nothing had compiled —
  and exited before mutating anything. Nothing was corrupted, which is the
  harness working; what was lost was the run. Treat a harness run as a lock on
  the whole worktree, not only on `--enumerate-touched`, and re-record the
