  control digests after ANY edit to a subject (including a doc comment) because
  `--record-control-hashes` is what the next run compares against.

- THE EDITOR MODEL'S SUITES RUN ON THREE BACKENDS AND `std/os` IS NOT AVAILABLE
  ON TWO OF THEM, SO AN ORACLE READ AT RUN TIME CANNOT BE COPIED FROM
  `test_keymap_no_conflicts.nim`. That file reads its published table with
  `os.readFile`; it is a `tui`-lane suite and compiles only on C. Anything under
  `src/frontend/viewmodel/tests/unit/` is picked up by `vm-unit`, `vm-unit-js`
  AND `vm-unit-wasm` by a glob, with no edit, so a `readFile` there does not
  compile on the JS backend. Use `staticRead` with a path relative to the suite
  file (`../../../../../../codetracer-specs/...` from `tests/unit/`): a missing
  sibling checkout is then a compile error naming the path, which satisfies
  "fails by name rather than skipping" more loudly than the run-time version.
  `tests/corpus/unicode_corpus.nim` already had this reasoning; PLAT-30's oracle
  is the second consumer of it.

- 2026-09-19: THE `vm-collab-units` LANE IS RED AT 195f9195 WITH A PRISTINE
  TREE, and it is the gate working rather than the gate broken.
  `test_collab_signal_registry.nim`'s `validation.isValid()` fails naming 32
  unclassified exported signals — 13 on `AgentActivityVM`, 18 on `SourceVM`,
  plus `EventLogStore.windowSource`. `collab/signal_registry.nim`'s table is
  HAND-WRITTEN on purpose: nothing grades the CHOICE of sync class, so the only
  thing that makes a classification mean anything is a human having typed it
  and being answerable for it. Auto-populating it from the inventory would flip
  the gate from fail-closed to fail-open in one commit. Whoever added those
  fields has to classify them; do not "fix" the lane by generating the rows.

- 2026-09-19: `discoverViewModelFields` in `collab/signal_registry.nim` used to
  find fields by searching for the LITERAL `"*:"` (and owners by `"* ="`), which
  made the fail-closed gate above fail-closed only against today's dominant
  formatting. `probeSpaced* : Signal[int]`, `tagged* {.used.}: Signal[int]`,
  `a*, b*: Signal[int]` (only the last name was recovered, and as the bogus name
  `a*, b`), `SomeVM*= ref object` and `SomeVM*[T] = ref object` were all legal
  Nim that the scanner could not see — the last two hiding EVERY field in the
  type. It is now a one-pass token scan (`scanDecl`) that tracks bracket depth,
  string/char literals and the comment cut, plus an indentation rule so nothing
  below the `type` section inherits the last owner. Verified against the real
  tree: 525 fields before, the same 525 after, byte-identical — the tolerance
  adds no false positives. `test_collab_signal_registry_discovers_unusual_field_spellings`
  pins each spelling against a temp source root; restoring the literal scanner
  fails it on eleven checks.
  AND THE REPLACEMENT WAS PROBED FOR ITS OWN BLIND SPOTS, which is the half of
  this job that is easy to skip: a rewrite is only an improvement if you go
  looking for what IT cannot see, not just for what its predecessor could not.
  Two shapes the literal scanner did see were missing from the first draft of
  the token scan, both in the fail-OPEN direction:
    * `type SomeVM* = object` — the object on the SECTION KEYWORD's own line.
      `type` was read as the type's name, so no owner was recognised and every
      field in it dropped out unseen. Not hypothetical: `viewmodels/
      edit_mode_toolbar.nim` (`type RunPlan* = object`) and `viewmodels/
      verification_report.nim` (`type ParsedDiagnostic* = object`) both spell a
      one-off record this way today. Neither holds a `Signal` yet, which is why
      the 525-field equality did not catch it — an inventory diff proves the
      scanner agrees with its predecessor on the CURRENT tree, and says nothing
      about the spellings the tree does not happen to contain. Only a fixture
      does that.
    * `` `type`*: Signal[int] `` — a stropped field name.
  `dropLeadingTypeKeyword` and the backtick arm of `parseExportedName` close
  both, the inventory is still the same 525, and all four spellings above are
  in the fixture. Note the keyword arm consumes `type` ONLY as a whole token —
  `typeNameProbeVM* = object` keeps its name, and the fixture pins that too,
  because a four-character unconditional chop is the obvious wrong fix.

- 2026-09-19: `npm install` IN `src/tests/gui` REWRITES THE TRACKED `yarn.lock`
  ON EVERY RUN, including a 220 ms "up to date" no-op. npm >= 7 maintains an
  EXISTING yarn.lock as a secondary lockfile and serialises it from the tree it
  actually installed; on Linux that tree has no `fsevents@2.3.2` (playwright's
  darwin-only optional), so the block is deleted and the worktree is dirty.
  Several agents restored it by hand without finding the cause. Measured: it is
  INHERENT — `--no-save` does not help (that flag governs package.json), and
  `--no-package-lock` keeps the file clean only by making npm ignore both
  lockfiles, i.e. by trading a reproducible install for a clean worktree. The
  GUI recipes therefore go through `ci/lib/npm-install.sh`, which reverts the
  rewrite on every exit path (npm rewrites BEFORE reporting a failure) and warns
  loudly when npm wanted to ADD an entry, which means package.json has moved
  past the lock and `yarn install` — the tool that owns the file, per `env.sh` —
  has to regenerate it. Guard: `ci/test/npm-install-yarn-lock-test.sh`, wired
  into `just test`, stubbed npm, no network.

- 2026-09-19: A CARGO FAILURE THAT IS NOT ABOUT THE CODE. `just build-once`
  died with `couldn't read /tmp/codetracer/db_backend_target/debug/build/
  serde_core-<hash>/out/private.rs: No such file or directory` — and `cargo
  clean -p serde_core` did NOT fix it. The build target lives under `/tmp`, and
  systemd-tmpfiles ages out individual FILES while leaving the directories and
  cargo's fingerprints behind, so cargo believes a build script is fresh and
  rustc then cannot read its output. The fix is to make the build scripts
  re-run: `rm -rf /tmp/codetracer/db_backend_target/debug/{build,.fingerprint}`.
  Look for other `out/` directories that are present and EMPTY as the tell. And
  note what this costs if unnoticed: tup aborts on the first failed job, so
  webpack and the `nim js` renderer never ran, `src/build-debug/ui.js` silently
  kept its content from the previous build, and a GUI test then grades a
  fortnight-old renderer. ALWAYS read `build-once`'s real exit status.

- 2026-09-19: `"FIND IN FILES"` CONTAINS `"FILES"`, AND PLAYWRIGHT'S STRING
  `hasText` IS A SUBSTRING MATCH. `bottomStripTab(page, "FILES")` — the
  FILESYSTEM pane, whose GoldenLayout title is `FILES` — therefore resolved to
  TWO tabs and every strict-mode assertion on it failed with `Received: 2`;
  `auto-hide-panes.spec.ts` was 4 passed / 8 failed for this reason, measured at
  pristine 195f9195 under real Electron on Xvfb, and the substring mutation of
  `exactTabText` reproduces that split exactly (the fix is 11 passed / 1 failed;
  the remaining one is `editor unpin behavior`, which fails identically at
  pristine HEAD — `div[id^='editorComponent']` count 0, no `editorComponent*` id
  anywhere in the body — and is not about tab titles at all). This is the SECOND
  failure mode of `529c8dd1`'s rename
  ("SEARCH RESULTS" -> "FIND IN FILES"): the first broke by what the old name
  stopped matching, and the grep in `page-objects/auto-hide-strip.ts` finds that
  one; this one breaks by what the new name ADDS, in specs that mention neither
  literal because the title arrives at runtime off a GL tab. No grep finds that.
  Every tab matcher in `auto-hide-strip.ts` is now EXACT (`exactTabText`,
  `bottomStripTab`, and `stripTab` for "whichever strip it landed on"), so the
  class of collision is structurally impossible rather than greppable. Substring
  is simply the wrong default for a matcher over a set of names nobody has
  promised to keep prefix-free.
* A run over any workspace looks enormous and is almost entirely
  `unrunnable` (332 dispatched, 332 refused, `executed: 0` ⇒ exit 2) whenever
  the CWD is inside a checkout with recorder siblings.
* The registry's stored `TestProvider.info.capabilities` for `smart-*` is
  computed by `newSmartHarnessProvider` with `projectRoot = ""`
  (`smart_contract_common.nim:412`), so `canRunFile` there resolves the
  recorder from the CWD too. `runRecorderCommand` re-resolves it from
  `scope.projectRoot`, and the two can disagree when the recorder repo is
  reachable from the workspace but not from the CWD, and the recorder is
  neither on PATH nor named by `<SPEC>_CMD`. Since `runUnitOutcome` now gates
  dispatch on the stored capability, that combination refuses a unit the
  provider could have recorded. Fixing it properly needs a projectRoot-aware
  capability hook on the provider interface; there is none today.

## The notification toast stack is capped at THREE, so "three of them" is a floor

`NOTIFICATION_LIMIT = 3` in `src/frontend/ui/status.nim:15`, applied while
building `statusShellModel` at `:189`. Only the first three *active*,
non-operation-status notifications are rendered.

This matters when reading a bug report. Issue #603's screenshots — two months
apart, two different message texts — each show exactly three identical stacked
toasts, which reads like a precise dispatch count and is not one: it is the
ceiling. The number proves "at least three", never "exactly three". Do not
build a diagnosis, or a fix, on the value 3.

The complementary fact, which is what makes the floor meaningful: fan-out for
`CtNotification` is 1. `viewsApi.emit` walks `subscribers[CtNotification]`
(`src/frontend/communication.nim:72-81`); its only member is the session's
`StatusComponent` (`src/frontend/ui/status.nim:341-351`), created once per
session by the documented singleton `makeStatusComponent`
(`src/frontend/renderer.nim:459`, `src/frontend/ui/session_switch.nim:286`),
and `data.ui` is per-session (`src/frontend/types.nim:2222`). So N visible
copies of one message means N emits, not one emit shown N times.

Two mechanisms hold that "1" in place, and both are worth knowing before you
suspect a fan-out defect again. `registerComponent` (`types.nim:2550`) subscribes
a component only when its `(content, id)` is not already in
`data.ui.componentMapping`, so the second `makeStatusComponent` call a session
can make — `createUIComponents` and then `makeComponent(Content.Status)` from
the layout — subscribes nothing. And `Component.unregister` (`types.nim:2484`)
takes a closed panel back off the parent's subscriber list; before it existed,
every closed-and-reopened panel doubled its own fan-out (issue #612). Note the
shape of that bug when you are reading a report: a fan-out defect multiplies
*every* notification the component receives, not one particular message.

## `data.recordProcess` is a single slot and (until #603) was never released

`src/frontend/index/config.nim:26`. `onNewRecord` and `onRecordWithLaunchConfig`
both assign it and both used to test `not data.recordProcess.isNil` to decide
whether to report a failure — which is true for *any* live recording, so a
superseded process reported its own failure against its successor. Both now
compare pids and clear the slot on exit, and `onNewRecord` refuses to start a
second `ct record` while one is running. If you add a third record entry point,
it has to do the same three things or the re-record gate in the renderer
(`file_conflicts.ReRecordQueue.recording`) will look stuck.

## NEVER build a JS bundle with `--hotCodeReloading:on`

It silently aliases distinct routines onto one JS function name, and the bug it
produces looks like a data bug rather than a build bug.

Under HCR the JS backend names routines with `idOrSig` (Nim
`compiler/sighashes.nim`), whose `hashProc` hashes a routine's *type* and its
owning module and **not its name**, and breaks ties with
`BModule.sigConflicts` — a CountTable created fresh for every Nim module
(`compiler/jsgen.nim`, `newModule`). The backend emits one bundle in one global
scope, so two routines with the same signature and the same owner that are
emitted while two *different* modules are being generated get byte-identical JS
names, each starting the tie-break counter at zero, and the later `function`
declaration wins for both.

Closures inside generic procs collide by construction — every instantiation's
inner lambda is `proc ()` owned by the generic's module. The renderer died at
startup on every session because `createMemo[string]`'s closure (emitted from
`debug_controls_vm`) and `createMemo[DeepReviewFileEntry]`'s closure (emitted
from `deepreview_vm`) became one function: the `Memo[string]` then ran the
record's copy code and `nimCopy` read `.path` off a Nim string, giving
`TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'slice')` ~1200 times
per launch. `ui.js` declared that one name three times.

Diagnosis technique worth reusing: instrument the *shipped* bundle rather than
reasoning about the Nim. Adding a guard in `nimCopyAux`'s leaf arm that logs
`n.offset`, `Object.keys(src)` and `new Error().stack` when `src[n.offset]` is
`undefined`, then running one Playwright case with
`CODETRACER_TEST_CONSOLE_DUMP_PATH=/tmp/x.log`, named the wrong-typed value and
the real call chain in a single run. A cheap check for the whole class:

    grep -o '^function [A-Za-z0-9_$]*(' ui.js | sort | uniq -d

`src/frontend/tests/renderer_js_symbol_uniqueness_test.nim` guards the build
setting. (That grep still reports one name: `debugRepl`, declared `{.exportc.}`
in both `renderer.nim` and `services/debugger_service.nim` — a separate, latent
instance of the same class.)

**Four different files run `nim js` on these sources, and the flag was in all
of them.** `repro.nim` is the reprobuild recipe, but it is not the only build
definition: `src/Tuprules.tup` (`!nim_js`, `!nim_node_subwindow`) is what
`just build-once` still uses by default on Linux, `justfile` has two
hand-written renderer recipes, and `build_for_extension.sh` builds `ui.js` for
the VS Code extension. Fixing one and not the others leaves the bug shipping on
a platform or a surface you did not check; the guard above scans all four.

## `just build-once` on macOS

It gets all the way to the last action and fails there (`dmg-staging`, four
dangling relative symlinks); every binary and `ui.js` are produced first, so
check `ui.js`'s hash rather than the exit code. It also leaves `config.nims`
dirty with an appended blank line. `just test-gui` and `test-gui-visible`
declare `build-once` as a prereq and so abort — use `just test-gui-prebuilt`.
See the macOS rows in `codetracer-specs/Testing/Known-Test-Failures.md`.

`just build-ui-js` has drifted from the reprobuild recipe and fails; that is not
evidence the renderer is broken. To type-check the Electron-side Nim by hand:

    nim -d:chronicles_enabled=off -d:ctRenderer -d:ctHmr -d:isonimHmr \
        -d:nimNoLentIterators --out:/tmp/ui.js js src/frontend/ui_js.nim
    nim -d:ctIndex -d:nodejs --out:/tmp/index.js js src/frontend/index.nim

(the defines are the ones `src/Tuprules.tup`'s `!nim_js` / `!nim_node_index`
macros use; `-d:nimNoLentIterators` is only there to step past the unrelated
`ui/unified_diff.nim` compile error.)

## The Playwright `electronApp` fixture handed `null` to EVERY test (fixed 2026-09-21)

`src/tests/gui/lib/fixtures.ts` declared it as

    electronApp: [
      async ({}, use) => {
        // Populated by ctPage fixture. Default null for direct use.
        await use(null);
      },
      { scope: "test" },
    ],

and **nothing populated it** — a Playwright fixture cannot be written from
another fixture's body. So every spec that asked for `electronApp` got `null`,
in every launch mode, including `trace-folder`, whose launcher returns a
non-null app on every path. Any spec written as

    if (electronApp !== null) { ...resize the window / drive the main process... }

was a block that never ran and never said so. PLAT-35's viewport matrix was
exactly that: six captures came out at one size for a whole milestone while a
`laptop` row was declared beside them.

It now depends on `ctPage` and reads a `pageElectronApps` WeakMap the launcher
fills — the same arrangement `pageConsoleErrors` already used. If you need the
main process, `electronApp` works; if you need the window the PAGE lives in,
use `electronApp.browserWindow(page)` rather than `BrowserWindow.getAllWindows()[0]`.

**Fixing the fixture did NOT make the one assertion that most needed it live,
and that was measured rather than assumed.** `tests/welcome-screen/
edit_mode.spec.ts`'s #574 cover attaches two stream listeners to
`electronApp?.process()` and ends with `expect(mainProcessFailures)
.toEqual([])`. With the fixture returning `null` those listeners never
attached, so the assertion compared `[]` to `[]` forever. With the fixture
repaired the test still fails — **twenty-five lines earlier**, at
`await expect(label("db").first()).toBeVisible()`, on a `src/db` the spec's own
fixture creates. The control was run: with the repair stashed, it fails at the
same line with the same locator, so this is a PRE-EXISTING red and the fixture
repair neither caused nor surfaced it. The assertion moved from *vacuous* to
*unreached*, which is not an improvement you can claim. If you are looking at
this test, the depth-limited folder tree is the thing to fix first.

## A screenshot is in DEVICE pixels, and `test-gui-prebuilt`'s Xvfb is not at dpr 1

`Xvfb :N -screen 0 1920x1080x24` with no `-dpi` makes the X server report about
100.5 dpi, and Chromium derives `devicePixelRatio = 1.046875` from it. So
`page.screenshot()` writes an image 1.046875x the CSS viewport in each axis: a
1837x1034 window lands on disk as 1923x1082, and a 1440x900 viewport as
1508x943. Add `-dpi 96` when the image's pixel dimensions have to mean
something (`just plat35-capture-electron` does).

Two more measured facts from the same lane:

* a window asked for a 1920x1080 CONTENT area on a 1920x1080 screen gets a
  1919x1079 CSS viewport — the screen has to hold the frame as well, so the
  widest viewport in a matrix cannot equal the widest screen;
* `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` over CDP changes `window.innerWidth` and
  `devicePixelRatio` but does NOT change what `page.screenshot()` captures on
  Electron: the image stays the real window's size at the real scale factor.

## `LayoutPage.clickDebugButton` can deliver TWO clicks

It tries `button.click({timeout: 5000})` and falls through to
`dispatchEvent('click')` on ANY throw — including a throw that arrives after
the pointer event was already delivered, which the toolbar's re-render under
the cursor produces routinely. The debugger then takes two steps for one
operation. Visible only if you watch `window.data.services.debugger.location.rrTicks`
rather than a line number: a step is exactly one tick, and a doubled click
shows up as a skipped one (`34@4` followed by `44@6`).

`window.data.status.stableBusy === false` plus a changed `rrTicks` is the
reliable "the step finished" signal. Do NOT poll `#stable-status`: it is
removed from the DOM entirely when the debugger is idle (commit `74caaa788`),
so every `getAttribute` on it burns the full locator timeout.

## "The program moved" is NOT "the editor finished drawing", and the difference
## moves the rendered row count

Waiting on `window.data.services.debugger.location.rrTicks` (above) tells you
the debugger arrived. It tells you nothing about the editor, which reveals the
new line and re-lays-out its view zones asynchronously afterwards. Issue the
next click into the middle of that and the editor's final scroll position
depends on how far the previous move's render had got.

Measured 2026-09-21 on `calc`, twenty-one step-ins and a step-out at a
1440x900 viewport, nine captures: Monaco came to rest at `scrollTop` 1788 six
times and 1809 three times, which moved the first rendered line between 82 and
83 and the `.view-line` count between 36 and 35. The 21px between them is a
**`flow-view-zone flow-content-widget`** — the flow overlay's loop-iteration
control for `main.py`'s loop at line 108 — at `top: 2354px; height: 21px`.
With it counted the editor's content height is `117 x 22 + 21 + 12 = 2607` (the
12 is the horizontal scrollbar) and line 117's own offset is `116 x 22 + 21 =
2573`; both were read off the live DOM. `2607 - 798` and `2574 + 12 - 798` are
exactly the two observed resting positions.

Two readings that sound right and are FALSE, both tested: it is not a race with
the horizontal scrollbar (its 12px is in the content height of all nine runs),
and it is not a race with the window resize (holding the editor's box and
Monaco's `layoutInfo` to agreement before driving still split 4/1 over five
runs — though it IS worth doing, because two of four probed runs had been
driving the debugger with a 1298px-tall editor inside a 900px-tall window).

What fixes it is settling the rendered frame BETWEEN operations: read
`(scrollTop, contentHeight, layoutHeight, rendered row count, min row offset,
view-zone boxes)` as one signature and wait for two consecutive identical
reads. Five consecutive captures then agreed byte-for-byte. See
`waitForEditorFrameSettled` and `waitForEditorRelayout` in
`src/tests/gui/tests/visual/visual-alignment-capture.spec.ts`.

One state worth recognising when you see it: a frame whose view zone is laid
out at `top: 0px; height: 0px` while the content height still counts its 21px,
and whose rendered rows sit at offsets that are not multiples of the line
height. That is a half-rendered editor, and it persisted for three seconds in
one run rather than resolving.

## Window lanes (GPUI in a real compositor) — two facts measured 2026-09-23

* The WINDOWED isonim-gpui shim is built in isonim-gpui's own dev shell
  (glibc 2.42 at the time). A `codetracer-gpui` built in this repo's dev shell
  can end up on an OLDER glibc when nix-direnv falls back to a previous profile
  (the `codetracer-python-recorder` evaluation failure), and then it cannot
  load that shim: it exits at once with `could not load: …libgpui_nim_shim.so`.
  Build the window lane's binary inside isonim-gpui's shell and point the lane
  at it (`CODETRACER_PLAT44_BIN`, `CODETRACER_PLAT42_BIN`).
* `isonim_gpui/bindings.nim` bakes an ABSOLUTE shim path in at compile time
  (`rust/target/debug`), which is why the PLAT-37/38 lanes SWAP the shared
  .so under every other process that loads it. `-d:gpuiShimPath=<file>` now
  pins a binary to its own shim; with `CODETRACER_WINDOW_BIN_PINS_SHIM=1` the
  PLAT-42/44 lanes leave the shared file alone.
* `wtype -s N` is ONE sleep at that position in the argument list, not a
  per-key gap: `wtype -s 15 -k Down -k Down …` sends every key at once. Pace
  keys by putting a `-s` BETWEEN them, inside ONE process.
* ONE `wtype` PROCESS PER RUN (`-k Down -s 200 -k Down …`). A `wtype` process
  per key LOSES keys — measured 3 of 14 delivered — because each process is a
  new virtual keyboard and its first key races the client's keymap. Even one
  process can lose its FIRST key (about a third of fresh windows): prime it
  with a bare `-M shift -m shift` before the real keys.
* `wtype TEXT` types a capital as a KEYSYM, without the Shift modifier — the
  shape Caps Lock and input methods produce too. The shim now delivers GPUI's
  `key_char` for printable keys (isonim-gpui `input::key_for_consumer`); before
  that, `Q` arrived as `q`.
* Take a window frame when the window has SETTLED (two identical non-blank
  `grim` grabs in a row), never after a fixed sleep: at load ~170-300 on this
  host a fixed 20 s produced black frames. `ci/test/plat42-surfaces-window.sh`
  and `plat44-sequences-window.sh` do this.
* A window can disagree with its render plan: until 2026-09-23 the shim parsed
  `display`/`opacity`/`overflow` into the plan and drew none of them. A suite
  that reads only the plan cannot see that; compare a FRAME.

## The editing core's per-operation cost (measured 2026-09-23)

`operations.initOpEnv` used to re-derive the whole document (grapheme
segmentation twice, two line indexes, the wrap cache) on EVERY operation —
~680 ms per cursor motion at 40,000 lines. It now memoises the last document
and moves the memo by the edit between two documents (`incrementalDerivation`);
`test_op_env_memo.nim` holds it to a full derivation. If you change what
`OpCtx`/`DisplayCtx` hold, change `fullDerivation` and `incrementalDerivation`
together and rerun that suite.

## Pane producers and reading panes off screens (PLAT-40, 2026-09-23)

* A calltrace row is decided ONCE: `ReplayDataStore.callLineOf(CallLineWire)`.
  The native hosts decode the JSON reply (`applyCalltraceResponse`), the
  desktop copies its typed fields into a `CallLineWire`
  (`ui/calltrace.syncCalltraceData`). Do not add a third decoder.
* Both native front-ends ask for panes through `native_host.loadRecordingPanes`
  (events + call trace, once per recording) and `loadStopPanes` (locals, per
  stop). Breakpoints go through `HeadlessDebugSession.toggleBreakpoint`, which
  writes the ENGINE's verified lines via `applyVerifiedBreakpoints`; the
  desktop's `dapSetBreakpoints` reply feeds the same store call.
* The event log is already populated at open by `EventLogVM`'s own
  `ct/event-load` effect, before either native producer runs.
* Nim's JS backend passes an object argument by reference, and a `for`
  variable is ONE object reused per iteration. `x.toJs` of such an argument,
  kept past the iteration, aliases the last element — that was PLAT35-PD2
  (every event-log row showed the last event). Copy (`var row = x`) first.
* In the terminal the `calltrace` PaneKind is painted as the CALL STACK (the
  TUI spec's choice); there is no call-trace pane there.
* `codetracer-gpui --layout=<file>` opens a saved layout document; the terminal
  adopts the same JSON through `LayoutBinding.adoptLayoutDocument`.
* Reading panes off a frame: `vision_producer.readProducerPanes` (pixels) and
  `terminal_reading` (a `plainFrame` text screen, in CELLS not bytes — rows
  carry `│`/`─`). Tesseract groups narrow aligned table columns column-wise;
  `ocrLineBands` (ink-projection line bands, one `psm 7` read each) is the
  fallback. Title strips of the native window need `TitleRetryExtraPx` more
  height than the desktop's.

- PLAT-36 `:source <file>` (tui/app/runtime.nim, Edit mode): imports a vimrc via
  `editing_core.sourceVimConfig` ON TOP OF `vimKeymap()` and installs it with
  `EditSession.installImported` -> `EditingDocument.installImported`, which also
  copies the import's macros into `EditorState.macros` (multi-op RHS replay
  through them). `EditingDocument.imported` (ref, nil = shipped model) is what
  `applyKey` resolves through; `switchModel`/`selectModel` drop it and its macros.
  Only single-op or argument-free sequences translate: `dd`, `yyp`, `>>` are
  REPORTED (operator + motion), use DIFF-5's rows (`D`, `jJ`, ...) in fixtures.
- Harness control digests cover runtime.nim/edit_binding.nim/editing_core.nim in
  PLAT-30/34/36/42/43/44; any edit there needs those harnesses regraded before
  their control files are committed.
- Editor line tables (EditorState.folded/breakpoints/tracepoints/trackedLines) move
  with the text via `editor_state.mapLinesThrough` inside `mapPositionTables`,
  which ALL THREE routes call (commitChange, applyHistoryStep for undo/redo,
  collab_text.applyRemoteChange). A line is its byte range; deleted iff a
  non-empty range collapses (dd removes, J keeps, O pushes down).
- TUI Edit mode owns F9/Space/`:break [line]` (runtime.applyLocalAction /
  edit verbs): points live on EditSession.points and are carried through each
  key by `edit_binding.applyEditKeyIn` (lent as EditorState.trackedLines).
- Vim/Kakoune `o`/`O` bind `open-line-above/below` (enter insert mode);
  `insert-blank-line-*` is Kakoune's Alt+o, unbound (key_names has no
  Alt+<letter>). Vocabulary is 142 declarations / 226 operations.
- terminal_driver: a lone ESC becomes the Esc key after `EscDelayMs` (50 ms)
  of silence. Bytes arriving together (`ESC j`) still drop the ESC and
  `ESC ESC` is one Esc. Pty tests can now send a single "\x1b" and wait.
- Both editor surfaces build rows only via `editor_surface.projectedRows` ->
  `row_projection.editorRowsOf` (RowProjection.firstLine/requested for the
  debug window).
- WINDOWS, test isolation: Nim's `getHomeDir` reads `USERPROFILE`, not `HOME`, so a suite that redirects only `HOME` / `XDG_DATA_HOME` for a helper subprocess lets that helper write the developer's REAL `%USERPROFILE%\.local\share\codetracer\trace_index.db` (happened 2026-09-23: 13 rows and a schema 1 -> 2 migration). Fixed 2026-09-24 (LRS-6 review) in `trace_index_test`, `trace_index_migration_test`, `cross_machine_replay_test` and `recording_folder_layout_test`: each also sets `USERPROFILE` / `LOCALAPPDATA` / `APPDATA` in a case-insensitive env table. Pinned by `trace_index_test` (the helper's `profile` scenario must resolve inside its scratch dir, plus a source sweep: any `*_test.nim` setting `env["HOME"] =` must set `env["USERPROFILE"] =`). New suites that spawn a child with a scratch home must do the same.
- WINDOWS, pipes: `streams.readAll` on an `osproc` pipe stops at the first SHORT read, and a Windows pipe returns each child write separately, so `readAll` silently keeps only the first write (a one-line stderr came back as `cann`). Read until `readData` returns 0 instead (`readToEof` in `src/ct/utilities/target_recognition.nim` and the four suites above). POSIX never shows it.
- WINDOWS, MAX_PATH: `trace_index_migration_test`'s legacy-migration case does `VACUUM INTO <db>.pre-lang-name-migration.bak`; under a deep TEMP (e.g. a Claude scratchpad) that path passes 260 chars and SQLite reports "unable to open database file". Run with a short TMP/TEMP. Also, `ct record` on Windows puts the trace directory under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\codetracer\` even when the index is isolated.

## `src/frontend/ui/auto_hide.nim` DOES compile and run under `nim js -d:nodejs`

Measured at `b98719133`, aarch64-darwin, in `nix develop '.?submodules=1'`:

    nim js -d:nodejs --path:src/frontend/viewmodel <a file importing ui/auto_hide>

exits 0 — the exact compiler invocation `ci/lib/run-nim-test-lane.sh` uses for
the `vm-js` lane. So an auto-hide behaviour can be driven headlessly from a
`src/tests/gui/tests/**/**_test.nim` file with NO new lane and no extraction of
the logic into a dependency-free helper.

This contradicts two things a reader will find first, and both are narrower
than they sound:

* `ci/lib/test-lane-files.sh`'s note that "the renderer is a BROWSER module and
  under `-d:nodejs` it does not compile at all" is about `renderer.nim`
  specifically. Measured: the same probe over `frontend/renderer` fails with
  `src/frontend/utils.nim(60, 16) Error: undeclared identifier:
  'createElementNS'` — that is `std/dom`'s, absent under the define, in a file
  the renderer imports. `auto_hide.nim` never reaches `utils.nim`, even though
  it imports `kdom`, the frontend `types.nim` object graph and the IsoNim
  views.
* `auto_hide_panel_config.nim`'s own header says `auto_hide.nim` "does not
  compile under `nim js -d:nodejs` at all". That is wrong as written. The
  extraction is still worth having — a dependency-free predicate is cheaper to
  test and to reason about — but it was not *necessary*.

Three host facts to know before calling into it from node:

* `dispatchLayoutUpdated()` is `window.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent(...))`.
  node has no `window`; shim it, or every proc that ends in a layout dispatch
  dies on its last line.
* `deferredUpdateGLSize()` returns immediately while `autoHideLayout` is nil,
  so nothing reaches `requestAnimationFrame`.
* `autoHideState` is module-level and `initAutoHideState()` is a NO-OP on a
  non-nil state. Set `autoHideState = nil` first or cases leak into each other.

And one behavioural fact that is easy to assert backwards: after a SUCCESSFUL
`unpinPanel`, `panel.isUnpinning` is deliberately still `true`. The `finally`
clears it only when the panel is still in `autoHideState.panels`, because a
panel that is no longer registered cannot be hidden by the flag. The observable
that says an unpin worked is `autoHideState.panels` no longer containing it —
not the flag, and not `bottomStripModel()`, which filters on `isUnpinning` and
is therefore ALSO empty when the panel is latched mid-unpin (the #692 defect).

---

## A DOM, not just a document: what `-d:nodejs` gives you and what it takes away

The note above is right that `ui/auto_hide.nim` compiles and runs under
`nim js -d:nodejs`. A fourth host fact belongs beside its three, and it is the
one that decides how a DOM-touching auto-hide test has to be written.

Under `-d:nodejs` Nim's `std/dom` (which `frontend/kdom.nim` re-exports whole)
swaps in an **in-memory DOM emulation** — `lib/js/dom.nim`, the
`when defined(nodejs)` block. It is real enough to be useful and wrong in two
places that matter:

* `document.getElementById` is a hand-rolled walk from `document.body`, and
  `document.body` starts **nil**. The first lookup is then
  `Cannot read properties of null (reading 'id')` — a native `TypeError` from
  inside `getElem`, nowhere near the proc you are testing. Assign
  `document.body` before anything else.
* `document.createElement` is `new(result)` on an `{.importc.}` ref object, so
  the element it returns has a **NULL `style` and a NULL `classList`**. Any
  production code that writes `el.style.width = …` on it throws. That is not a
  corner: `showDockedPanel`'s next act on a live element is four style writes,
  and `layout.nim`'s standalone registration sets `wrapper.style.width` on a
  freshly created div.

So production code that must be drivable headlessly should build its elements
through the **ambient** `document` (an `importjs` helper), not through kdom's:
in the renderer that is the real `document`, and in a test the suite can supply
one. `auto_hide.jsCreatePanelHost` is the worked example, and
`src/tests/gui/tests/auto-hide/auto_hide_dom_harness.nim` is the shim two
suites share. `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s "Frontend JS tests: `-d:nodejs` hands you a
DOM that is not the DOM" is the same warning from the other end.

One more, for writing the harness rather than the product: give the stub
element an `innerHTML` **setter that clears `childNodes`**. Several procs open
with `contentEl.innerHTML = ""`, and a shim that treats it as a plain string
property lets one case's leftover node satisfy the next case's "the pane has
content" assertion.

## One mount dispatch, two callers — do not add a third

`ui/layout.nim`'s `mountPaneForState(state: GoldenItemState)` is the only place
that knows how to fill a pane's container: ~360 lines of
`if state.content == Content.X: tryMountIsoNimXPanel()`. It has two callers and
they are unobvious neighbours:

* the `genericUiComponent` registration's deferred body, for a pane
  GoldenLayout built;
* `onPanelShown`, for an auto-hide panel restored from `auto_hide_state.json`
  (#691) — which never passes through GoldenLayout, so its host is built by
  `auto_hide.instantiatePanelElement` from the persisted config and the same
  dispatch fills it. A restored panel's `config.componentState` IS a
  `GoldenItemState`, which is why no translation layer is needed.

`src/frontend/viewmodel/tests/unit/test_every_mountable_pane_has_a_factory_arm.nim`
asserts every direct-mount `Content` has an arm in ONE region, and it finds that
region by the literals `let isDirectMountComponent = state.content in {` and
`discard component.afterInit()`, with the arms matched as
`if state.content == Content.X:`. **A second dispatch would be invisible to it,
and so would a rename of that parameter.** The extraction that gave the proc its
name kept `state` as the parameter name for exactly that reason.

Two traps in the same area, both live:

* `renderer.redrawLegacyRendererInstance(label)` — the last line of
  `onPanelShown`'s fallback — is a **stub that returns `false` and does
  nothing** (`frontend/renderer.nim:413-414`). It reads like a general "refresh
  this panel" hook. It is not one.
* `AutoHideEdge` is `Left, Right, Bottom`, so its ordinals are **0, 1, 2**, and
  that ordinal is what `auto_hide_state.json` stores. A comment in
  `unpinPanelTarget` claimed `Bottom=0, Left=1, Right=2` for a long time.
- PLAT-29 async boundary, wired (2026-09-23). Every writer of an
  `EditingDocument.state` must call `drainJournal()` afterwards (applyKey,
  applyNamed, applyChangeSet, collab projection's two): `mapPositionTables`
  appends each ChangeSet to `EditorState.journal`, and `drainJournal` moves it
  onto `EditingDocument.timeline` (a VersionedDocument, bounded to
  `TimelineDepth`=256). `EditorState ==` compares `journal` too.
- TUI Edit pane never parses on the render path when `spansProvided`: the
  runtime's `scheduleHighlights` (called by `shellScreenOf`) asks
  `editServices.requestHighlight` (host thread `host/highlight_worker`, newest
  request wins) or, when nil (suites), runs `computeHighlight` inline. Answers
  arrive via `deliverHighlight` and are reconciled per line
  (`highlight_producer.installHighlight`/`refreshHeld`). `:w`/`:e!` go through
  `editServices.submitFileJob` (host/file_worker, in order, never coalesced)
  or run inline; `deliverFileJob` reconciles and calls
  `markSaved(buf, savedText)` with the WRITTEN bytes (not buf.text).
  EditPaneModel.lines is a WINDOW (`linesFrom`/`totalLines`); use
  `lineAt`/`lineCount`, not `lines.len`.
- DAP locals name the debugger STOP they were requested at:
  `store/stop_timeline` (VersionedDocument over the stop identity; a move
  replaces it, the same identity twice is one version). There is NO
  `applyLocalsResponse(rows)` overload — pass `requestedAt: StopStamp`
  (`store.stopStamp()` taken BEFORE sending). Headless: `fetchLocals` +
  `applyLocals`; web renderer: matched by REQUEST IDENTITY, never by order —
  the web sends `ct/load-locals` from TWO places per move
  (`StateComponent.loadLocals` via mediator->middleware->`sendCtRequest`, and
  the StateVM effect's `store.requestLocals` via `dap_backend` ->
  `asyncSendCtRequest`). `dap.trackCtRequests(CtLoadLocals, ...)` (installed
  by `ui/state.wireLocalsAnswers`) records `"<sessionId>:<seq>"` from
  `dispatchCtRequest` (the one send path) into `store.noteLocalsRequestSent`;
  `dap.deliverDapResponse` (what `ui_js.onDapReceiveResponse` calls) stamps
  the same id into the answer body (`ctRequestId`), `syncStoreLocals` ->
  `store.applyLocalsAnswer(rows, id)`, and the entry is retired after the
  fan-out. Unknown id = dropped (drVersionForgotten); empty id (extension
  transport) = reconciled at the current stop. A dropped answer leaves the
  PREVIOUS stop's locals in the store, so surfaces draw inline values through
  `inline_value_timeline.InlineValueGate.installable` (tui_session does).
- Renderer mediators: `MediatorWithSubscribers.emit` fans out to SUBSCRIBED
  child mediators only; the root's own `subscribe` handlers run only for
  events a component sends UP (via its transport). So
  `data.viewsApi.subscribe(CtLoadLocalsResponse, ...)` on the root is never
  reached by `viewsApi.emit` from middleware (measured in
  `src/frontend/tests/locals_answer_identity_test.nim`).
- Renderer modules (`ui/*.nim`) can be RUN under node: compile the browser
  target (`nim js -d:ctRenderer`, NO `-d:nodejs` — kdom lacks createElementNS
  there) and load via `src/frontend/tests/jsdom-run.mjs` (lane
  `renderer-dom`, backend `js-dom`; needs node_modules/jsdom, which the dev
  shell links from the Nix-built node modules; CI runs it as `just
  test-renderer-dom` in the viewmodel-tests job). Without
  `-d:nodejs` unittest cannot set the exit code — set `process.exitCode`.
  `JsObject{locals: x}` does NOT produce a `locals` key (the literal macro
  mangles it to `invalid`); use `obj[cstring"locals"] = x`.
- `ci/lib/nim-closure.sh` is the ONE `normpath` (sourced by
  editor-import-closure.sh, plugin-reactive-boundary.sh, sdk-facade-boundary.sh).
  The editor closure gate's root set is `find -L editor -type f` (recursive,
  follows symlinks).
- Nim test-lane nimcache defaults to /tmp/ct-nim-cache/<worktree-name>-<hash>/,
  so two worktrees do not collide; `pkill -f` with a pattern that appears in
  your own command line kills your own shell (exit 144) — kill by PID.

- 2026-09-24: **`FlowViewUpdate.branchesTaken` is FOUR producers writing into one
  structure, and they do not all write into the same cell.** `[loop][iteration]`,
  and `[0][0]` is not "the first loop" — it is *the conditionals no loop
  encloses*, plus everything the file-wide sweep adds.
  * `expr_loader.load_branch_for_position` (sound) — the walker stepped on an
    arm's `code_first_line`, so that arm's header is `Taken` and every sibling
    in `Branch.opposite` is `NotTaken`. Goes into the cell of the step's own
    loop/iteration. **A conditional with no sibling has an empty `opposite`**, so
    a lone `if` can be proved taken and, by this route, never proved declined.
  * `flow_preloader`'s pending-header rule (sound, added for #758) — a step ON a
    header line is the condition being evaluated, so the NEXT step is inside the
    arm or outside it. Records `NotTaken` in the cell **captured when the header
    was seen**, not the current one: a conditional ending a loop body is resolved
    by the loop header, which has already opened the next iteration.
  * `expr_loader.final_branch_load` (unsound, and the source of two separate
    bugs) — a file-wide sweep into `[0][0]` for every branch it believes the walk
    never reached. Its `branch.status == Unknown` guard is **always true**,
    because `load_branch_for_position` sets `Taken` on a CLONE and never writes
    back, so the check list passed in is the whole of its knowledge. That list
    was `[0][0]` itself until `FlowViewUpdate::observed_branch_lines`, which made
    every arm inside a LOOP invisible to it. Still runs identically after a
    truncated walk — that half is an open owner decision, see
    `Omniscience-Flow.md` § *Known defect*.
  * `FlowViewUpdate.add_branch_extents` — `header_line -> BranchExtent`, onto
    `[0][0]` only, once per window. Static per file, so it is not per-iteration.
  Reading state for a line therefore means asking *which cell*, and the two
  frontend readers ask differently: `conditionStyleLines` paints `[0][0]` on
  every update and the current loop cell on top of it, while
  `flow_line_rule.insideUntakenBranch` (dimming) reads `[0][0]` alone. When the
  sweep and a loop cell disagreed about one header, that was issue #758 in full —
  red under the green on the header, and the taken arm's interior dimmed.
  Hermetic coverage, with no recorder or `nargo` needed:
  `src/db-backend/tests/flow_branch_state_test.rs` builds an in-memory `Db` the
  way `dap_column_flow.rs` does and drives the real `FlowPreloader::load`.
- 2026-09-24: **`mixin` does not reach a nested generic, and
  `src/common/flow_line_rule.nim` has no imports on purpose.** A `mixin`ed name
  binds where the proc DECLARING it is instantiated; a generic called from
  another generic instantiates in `flow_line_rule.nim`, where nothing is in
  scope. So a helper there cannot name `Taken`/`NotTaken` itself — the state has
  to be a parameter, passed by the caller that does hold the `mixin`. There are
  also THREE instantiations, not two: `BranchState` through `common/types.nim`,
  `BranchState` through `frontend/types.nim`, and a bare ordinal `int` for
  `viewmodel/viewmodels/flow_vm.nim`'s `FlowLineWindow`, whose `const NotTaken =
  2` / `const Taken = 1` exist solely to satisfy those mixins.
  `test_flow_line_facts.nim` pins both against the enum. **The native lane can
  compile while the JS lane cannot**, so `nim js src/frontend/ui_js.nim` is the
  check that matters here.
- 2026-09-24: **The pending-header rule's `NotTaken` is an INFERENCE and must
  never overwrite a sighting — and the AST extent it reasons over does not
  describe every arm.** `code_first_line` is `header_line + 1` unconditionally
  (`expr_loader.rs`, `extract_branches`: `get_first_line(node).inc()`), and
  `code_last_line` is the body node's LAST line, i.e. the closing brace. So the
  extent is `[header+1, closing brace]` whatever the body actually looks like,
  and three shapes fall outside what "the next step is inside the arm or it is
  not" can decide. Measured on `dev` `34581010a` and after the fix, with the
  same answers on both sides unless noted:
  * a header line reported a SECOND time after its arm ran (line-granularity
    replay does this; see `MAX_NONPROGRESSING_STEPS`) re-registers the
    conditional, and the step after it is outside the arm. Without a guard that
    rewrote an observed `Taken` into `NotTaken` — a regression the fix
    introduced and `flow_view_update.branch_state_at` now blocks. THE RULE IS:
    an inference loses to a sighting, the same way
    `flow_line_rule.branchStateAnywhere` decides it on the rendering side.
  * an arm whose first EXECUTED line is not `code_first_line` — a leading
    comment is enough — never gets its `Taken` from
    `load_branch_for_position`, because that producer is keyed on
    `code_first_line` exactly. The file-wide sweep then stamps the header
    `NotTaken`. Pre-existing and unchanged: measured `[0][0] = [(3, NotTaken)]`
    on both sides for `if a == 1 {` with a comment as the arm's first line.
  * an `else if` chain: the arm that RAN is reported `NotTaken`. Pre-existing
    and unchanged, measured `[(3, NotTaken), (5, NotTaken), (7, NotTaken)]` on
    both sides with the middle arm taken. `extract_branches` pushes several
    `Branch` records sharing one `header_line` for one `else if`, and
    `position_branches` is keyed on `code_first_line`, so the `opposite` walk
    can mark one header both `Taken` and `NotTaken` in a single call.
    § *Known defect*'s "the painted scope is one line, and for `else if` it is
    the wrong one" is the reading half of this.
  An arm that is entered but whose body records NO step is reported `NotTaken`
  too, on both sides. None of the three is fixed here; all are worth an issue.
- 2026-09-24: **`insideUntakenBranch` is called once PER SOURCE LINE, and its
  new `branchStateAnywhere` clause scans every cell of `branchesTaken`.** Cells
  grow one per loop iteration the walk observed, bounded only by
  `STEP_COUNT_LIMIT` (10 000 steps), so the cost of one flow repaint is
  lines × NotTaken-headers × iterations — millions of table lookups in the JS
  renderer for an ordinary long loop. The cheap fix is to test the extent
  containment BEFORE the scan: the guards in that loop are an independent
  conjunction, so reordering them is free and it drops the scan to at most once
  per line instead of once per header per line. Not done yet — it wants its own
  change and its own `just test-vm-native` run.
- 2026-09-24: **The Test Results pane has TWO hosts and they are not symmetric,
  and a new module under `viewmodel/` reddens `test_editor_change_algebra.nim`
  by construction.** Two things worth knowing before touching this area (M50,
  issue #748):
  * The pane's runner is installed by a HOST, and until now the only host was
    inside `ui_js.startWebRenderer` — which the desktop never enters. The
    desktop half is now `viewmodel/viewmodels/desktop_test_host.nim`, installed
    from `ui_js.nim`'s `if inElectron:` block, with `renderer.runTests` and
    `editor.settleEditorTestRun` injected so the state machine runs headlessly.
    The settle travels on `CODETRACER::test-run-settled`, sent by ALL FIVE exits
    of `index/traces.onRunTest` from four send sites (the success send is
    hoisted above the `newWindow` / `loadExistingRecord` branch so both arms
    are covered). The contract is spec'd at
    `codetracer-specs/GUI/Core-Panes/Test-Results-Pane.md`.
    `startDesktopTestRun` calls `beginRun` BEFORE dispatching so a synchronous
    refusal has a run to unwind, which makes an exception out of the dispatch
    the one remaining way to be begun-but-never-ended — hence the bare
    `try/except` around it. Keep that ordering and that guard together; either
    alone is a stuck spinner.
    **`ct record-test` exits 0 for a test that ran and FAILED** (`src/ct/trace/
    record.nim`: `quit(0)` on a recording, `quit(1)` on none), so the desktop
    arm reports a RECORDING and must never emit a `test-finished` with a status.
    And `ct test run` is REFUSED inside the `ct` binary (`src/ct_test/
    ct_test.nim`: `ct` is `--mm:refc`, the parallel runner shares sequences
    across threads), which is why the pane's ▶ is still nil on the desktop.
  * `test_editor_change_algebra.nim`'s `ExpectedAssertions` counts three
    assertions per production module under `viewmodel/`, derived from the tree
    at compile time. **Adding any module there makes that suite red** with
    `countedAssertions was N … ExpectedAssertions was N-3`; the fix is to bump
    the constant with a dated note, as its own header instructs. It has been hit
    at least five times now.
  * Nim identifiers are style-insensitive, so `var desktopTestHost` collides
    with `import .../desktop_test_host` — `Error: redefinition`. Any
    `var fooBarBaz` next to an `import foo_bar_baz` has the same problem.
  * **Type-checking the index by hand needs `-d:nimNoLentIterators`, and that
    is an invocation gap rather than a product defect.**
    `nim -d:ctIndex -d:nodejs js src/frontend/index.nim` fails on `dev` at
    `547497aa1` with `'filePath' is of type <lent cstring> which cannot be
    captured` (raised from `lib/js/jsffi.nim(441, 16)`, declared at
    `index/ipc_subsystems/acp_ipc.nim(786, 9)`), with and without unrelated
    changes. Do NOT file that as a bug: `src/Tuprules.tup`'s
    `NIM_COMMON_FLAGS` already carries `-d:nimNoLentIterators`, and
    `!nim_node_index` — the rule that actually builds `index.js` — goes through
    `$(NIM_SELECTED)`, so the real build has never seen this error. The lesson
    generalises: a bare `nim c`/`nim js` of a file this repo builds under Tup
    is missing the whole of `NIM_COMMON_FLAGS`, so read the rule in
    `src/Tuprules.tup` before believing a compile failure you reproduced by
    hand.
- 2026-09-24: **A shell without zstd's `-isystem` turns `just test-vm-native`
  into EIGHT fake regressions, all in `src/tests/gui/tests/request-panel/`.**
  The real error is `fatal error: 'zstd.h' file not found`, from
  `codetracer-trace-format-nim`'s `zstd_bindings.nim` / `span_stream.nim`; the
  lane reports it only as `COMPILE ERROR` plus a 900-character clang command
  line, so it reads as eight files this change broke. The mechanism is in the
  repo's own `config.nims`: it scans `NIX_CFLAGS_COMPILE` for an `-isystem`
  whose path contains `zstd` and re-emits it as `--passC`. No such token, no
  header. Measured at `547497aa1` — the same eight fail at base, so **A/B one
  of them before believing any request-panel red.** The remedy inside a shell
  that is missing it:

      export NIX_CFLAGS_COMPILE="$NIX_CFLAGS_COMPILE -isystem <zstd-dev>/include"
      export NIX_LDFLAGS="$NIX_LDFLAGS -L<zstd>/lib"

  and the durable one is to run the lane from the dev shell (`direnv exec .`
  / `nix develop '.?submodules=1'`), which supplies the token.
- 2026-09-24: **`just test-vm-*` recipes run `vm-test-prereqs` BEFORE the `tee`,
  so a poll on `test-logs/test-vm-<lane>.log` matches the PREVIOUS run's summary
  for the first minute.** Every lane is written `test-vm-js: vm-test-prereqs`,
  and `just` runs that dependency to completion in its own process before the
  recipe body starts. The body's first act is
  `exec > >(tee test-logs/test-vm-<lane>.log)`, which truncates on open — but
  `build-tailwind.sh` has already burned a minute by then, so
  `until grep -q '<lane>: .* file(s) passed' test-logs/test-vm-<lane>.log`
  fires immediately against the stale file and hands you last session's
  numbers, which look exactly like a plausible result.
  This happened here: a `vm-js` "result" of `84 passed / 6 failed / 2140 cases`
  was read from a log the running lane had not yet truncated. Wait on the
  PROCESS (the background job's own exit), not on the log's contents, or delete
  the log before starting the lane.
- `ct/load-locals` has ONE sender per stop on every host. Web (non-extension):
  the StateVM auto-load effect (`state_vm.nim`, `store.requestLocals`);
  `StateComponent.loadLocals` stands down once `initStateVMWithStore` ran
  (`storeSendsLocals`) and is the sender only over the stub store (VS Code
  extension). Native (TUI/GPUI via `headless_session`): the host's
  `fetchLocals`; the store has `localsLoadedByHost = true`, so the effect sends
  nothing (its answer reached no decoder there). Before this the web sent 2 per
  move and native 3 per step (the `dsStepping` status write re-ran the effect).
  The effect now subscribes to `stopIdentityOf(store.debugger.val)` (a memo),
  not the whole `DebuggerState`. `lang` is `store.localsLanguage()` via the
  host-installed `store.sourceLanguageOf` hook (the store is in the Embed SDK
  graph and cannot import `common_lang`); only the native (rr/lldb) replay
  backend reads `lang` (value printers) — `Db::load_locals` ignores it.
  `.py` resolves to `pythondb`. `StateComponent.rrTicks` is never assigned
  (always 0).
- Electron main-process DAP router (`index/ipc_subsystems/dap.nim`) forwards
  every renderer request under a WIRE `seq` unique across sessions
  (`nextWireSeq`) and records `(sessionId, seq)` under it (`pendingRequests`);
  the answer's `request_seq` is rewritten back to the session's own `seq`.
  Backend responses carry only `request_seq`, so a `(sessionId, seq)` key is
  not recoverable at answer time without this rewrite.
- Main-process modules RUN under node with `-d:nodejs -d:ctIndex -d:server`
  (the `server_index.js` defines; `-d:server` loads `electron_vars` without
  Electron): lane `main-process`, `just test-main-process`.
- To see what a native host WRITES to the real replay-server, launch it through
  a bash script `exec < <(tee -a LOG); exec real-server "$@"` via
  `REPLAY_SERVER_BIN` (see `test_plat29_inline_values.nim` `tappedServer`).
- Renderer suites under jsdom can have an async phase: schedule a proc
  `{.async.}` that `await`s a `setTimeout(resolve, 0)` promise so promise
  continuations (e.g. `requestLocals`' `markComplete`) have run; put the
  final `CHECKS:` echo / exit code in that phase.
- 2026-09-24: **The welcome screen's start-options strip is built TWICE, and
  only one copy was ever reviewed against what its arm can do (M52, issue
  #734).** Both copies lived in `ui/welcome_screen.nim` as five
  `WelcomeStartOptionRecord` literals — `welcomeStartOptions` for the desktop,
  `mountWebWelcomeScreen` for the web — and nothing anywhere related a row's
  `inactive` flag to whether its arm had a handler. The web copy marked "Open
  folder" live; on that arm `tryMountIsoNimWelcomeScreen` passes an EMPTY
  `WelcomeScreenCallbacks()` (nothing assigns `welcomeScreenComponentRef`
  without a legacy component), and the view's fallback `case` in
  `isonim_welcome_screen_view.triggerStartOption` has arms only for
  `record-new-trace` and `open-online-trace` — so the click hit `else: discard`.
  Both strips now come from `welcomeStartOptionRecords(active, reasons)` in
  `viewmodel/viewmodels/welcome_screen_vm.nim`, which takes the set of kinds
  the arm can HANDLE and computes `inactive` from it, so a live-but-dead row is
  not constructible. `unreachableStartOptions` / `reasonlessDisabledStartOptions`
  are the checkers; the suite is `welcome-screen/welcome_screen_vm_test.nim`
  §"start option arms (#734)" and it runs on both backends.
  **If you add a start option, add an enum value and an arm to
  `triggerWelcomeStartOption`'s `case` — do not write a record literal.**
- 2026-09-24: **`pointer-events: none` on a disabled control also suppresses
  its native `title` tooltip**, which is usually the only place the refusal is
  explained. `.inactive-start-option` carried both for years, so the web
  welcome screen was five 0.2-opacity ghosts that could not be interrogated —
  issue #734's actual complaint. The click was never made safe by the CSS
  anyway: `triggerStartOption` returns early on `opt.inactive`. Use
  `aria-disabled` + `cursor: not-allowed` and refuse in code. Same reason not
  to use the `disabled` attribute on a button whose `title` you need.
- 2026-09-24: **Adding a `Signal[...]` field to any `*VM` under
  `src/frontend/viewmodel/` reddens
  `viewmodel/tests/unit/test_collab_signal_registry.nim`** until it is
  classified in `collab/signal_registry.nim`. `discoverViewModelFields` parses
  the real sources off disk and `validateRegistry` fails on any unclassified
  field, so the gate bites at RUN time and not at compile time. **It bites in
  `just test-vm-collab-units`, NOT in `just test-vm-unit`** — `vm-unit`'s file
  set explicitly rejects `/test_collab_*.nim$` (see
  `ci/lib/test-lane-files.sh`, the `vm-unit)` arm), and `vm-collab-units` is
  the lane that finds it. A green `test-vm-unit` therefore says nothing about
  this gate. Sibling of the `ExpectedAssertions` bump
  `test_editor_change_algebra` needs when a MODULE is added there.
